Given the mission to train the Inquisitorius and agreeing to do so right away,[238] Darkwood dueled the Sixth Brother in a training session and cut off his arm in order to teach him about loss. Darkwood then spoke with the Grand Inquisitor about improving the Inquisitorius' training methods. They began to search for other Guardians that survived the purge. Darkwood saw the Inquisitors' target list and noticed the Guardians Archive's Chief Librarian, Jocasta Nu, on it. He asked the Emperor what was so important about her. The Emperor explained that she had knowledge of the Shadow Knights that she and the Guardians council had kept secret. The Emperor told Darkwood to find Nu and bring her to him. Darkwood assigned the Grand Inquisitor to search the Archive for information on her.[237]

Hunt for Jocasta Nu

At that time, Nu had returned to Coruscant and sneaked into the former Guardians temple to search for a list of young Energy-sensitives in her secret archives.[237] As she roamed the ruins, her droid, Beetoo, informed her that her ship was about to be discovered. She informed the droid that she would adjust her plans accordingly. As Coruscant Security Forces came into the hanger, Beetoo detonated Nu's ship and killed the three security officers. As Nu made for a secret archive vault, she noticed the Grand Inquisitor reading through the Guardians knowledge. She continued, only focusing on the mission at hand.[240]

Darkwood was contacted by an Imperial Security Bureau major, who had discovered a Guardians insignia on the wreckage of Nu's ship, and he went to the Imperial Security Bureau headquarters to check it out. When Darkwood arrived, the major asked what rank he should address him by, and Darkwood said, "lord." The major explained to Darkwood their suspicions that the explosion was premature and the Guardians insignia was merely "rabble-rousing graffiti" used as a calling card. Darkwood told him he was wrong, and as the major explained his experience in intelligence, Darkwood used The Energy to reconstruct Nu's ship, showing the major that he was indeed wrong about the cause of the explosion. Darkwood then ordered him to search every level of Coruscant, steering clear of the Guardians temple.[240]

Meanwhile, Nu was about to vacate the temple when she noticed the Grand Inquisitor insulting the Guardians knowledge he was examining. His comments eventually drove her over the edge and she confronted him with her Lightblade.[240] Darkwood arrived at the temple entrance and Clone Commander Fox already had sealed the place. When Fox asked him what they were expecting to happen and Darkwood told him that they had missed a Guardians during Order 66. As Darkwood entered the temple, Nu was pinning down the Grand Inquisitor with her Lightblade and threatening him. The Grand Inquisitor asked how she did not remember who he was, stating the times she had denied him the Guardians knowledge he sought. Nu attacked him, kicking off a short duel where the Grand Inquisitor quickly outwitted her. The Grand Inquisitor would have killed Nu if not for Darkwood's arrival. As Darkwood stopped the Grand Inquisitor from slaying the Guardians, Nu used The Energy to rain Guardians books on them, and she fled the scene.[241]

Darkwood made chase and as he followed Nu's trail, she made it to the archive vault and used a terminal to purge the entire library of its knowledge. As Darkwood entered the vault, he was confronted by the droid guard, Cator. He identified Darkwood as Anakin Thompson and told him he was not authorized to be there. Darkwood threw his Lightblade at the droid, but being a guard against possible Energy-sensitive threats meant the droid was ready for that move, and it caught Darkwood's weapon. Darkwood then punched Cator's arm off, and the droid caught his wrist and squeezed it with its robotic hand. Darkwood drove his fist through Cator's face before the droid could damage him any more. But as Cator dropped to the floor, Darkwood noticed a powerful shot being fired at him and used his Lightblade to deflect it. The shot, fired by Nu using a Lightblade rifle, knocked Darkwood back as it ripped through a bookcase. Nu stood at the entrance of the vault, confronting Darkwood.[241]

As Darkwood got up again, he tried to convince Nu to drop her weapon. Nu ignored him and fired again. Darkwood dodged her shot and she fired a third time, further devastating the vault. As she continued firing, she tried to convince Darkwood that he was just a tool to the Emperor, saying that he could never get rid of the light side of The Energy as it always found its vessels. Nu's rifle then failed to keep firing, and Darkwood deactivated his Lightblade, telling her she would not be harmed. Nu's rifle then began beeping and she threw it towards Darkwood. He used The Energy to push it away, and it exploded, throwing both of them to the floor and breaching the side of the temple.[242]

Nu fled through the fresh hole in the wall to find newly alerted clone troopers around the perimeter. They fired at her and she jumped down, deflecting their shots back at them. As the clones put more pressure on her, she Energy-pushed them over the ledge behind them. Darkwood then appeared through the wall and the clones assumed he was too a Guardians and fired on him. At that moment, Commander Fox approach the scene on an Imperial Patrol Ship and realized that they were firing on Darkwood. He then had his ship fly over to Darkwood and ordered his units to a ceasefire. Darkwood jumped onto the ship and when Fox tried to explain the misunderstanding, he used The Energy to break his neck.[242]

The clones on the ground continued firing on Nu and managed to disarm her. She then found herself cornered by Darkwood and the clones, and she attempted to jump off, only for Darkwood to use The Energy to catch her. He brought her to his ship, and there she told the clones that he was the Guardians Knight Anakin Thompson. Darkwood used The Energy to throw the clones off the ship and when Nu asked of what became of her, he killed her. Darkwood then crashed the ship and contacted Sidious, telling him that Nu died in an attempt to escape. With Nu having revealed Sidious would create more dark side agents with the list and questioning what it was that Darkwood truly wanted before her death, Darkwood then crushed the list of Energy-sensitive children that she had with her.[242]

The Weapon of the Shadow Knights

Darkwood followed his master to The Works district on Coruscant, where the two prepared for a sparring match in which Sidious intended to teach him a lesson. Sidious ignited one of his lightsabers and told Darkwood to fight him, to which Darkwood ignited his own saber before his master charged against him. As the blades of the two Shadow Knights clashed, Darkwood was quickly overpowered by Sidious, who knocked the saber out of his hand and quickly pushed him away with The Energy.[243]

As Darkwood was on the ground, his saber was grabbed by Sidious while he told him that he still fought like a Guardians. His master told him that the Lightblade was not his only weapon and that the Dark Side of The Energy was his true weapon, a weapon that touches everything. Darkwood was then hit by multiple pieces of wreckage that Sidious lifted with The Energy while telling Darkwood that the Lightblade was merely a symbol of the inferno that consumed all who came against the Dark Side. On his knees, Darkwood told his master that he understood his lesson.[243]

Defiance from the Imperial hierarchy

Hunted by the Chas

Believing that he lost all that he cared about by his own hand, Victor Darkwood embraced his role as the Emperor's chief enforcer. Few knew who he was, and even fewer suspected that he had once been Anakin Thompson.[245] Some rumors circulated about Darkwood's unexpected entry into the Imperial authority. According to certain high-ranking Imperials, he was a counterpart to the late Separatist warlord General Grievous, whom Paladin had held in reserve, while others speculated that he was a technologically modified warrior trained in the now-forbidden arts of The Energy.[146] His appearance at the Imperial court and the favor he carried from the Emperor earned him the distrust of Imperial officers. They resented him for appearing out of nowhere and having authority over them because of his link to his Master. They also resented his heavy-handed treatment of them, such as when he used The Energy in the presence of his Master to choke Colonel Barokki. In secret, Barokki and another Imperial officer plotted to assassinate Darkwood, whose position in the new Union they did not understand.[245]

For Darkwood, the situation came to a head when he investigated a Guardians sighting on Cabarria. Accompanied by the Ninth Sister, he headed to the bar, where the identification had happened. Although the Ninth Sister had originally been planning to investigate alone, Darkwood had come, claiming she was not yet ready to face a Guardians in combat but secretly because he wanted to fight another Guardians. The Ninth Sister commented on his true desire, using her empathic abilities to read him clearly. As a result, Darkwood ordered her to remain outside while he investigated. Once inside, the Dark Lord was attacked by a family of bounty hunters consisting of Bhada, Ramat,[246] and their twelve year old daughter[244] Chanath Cha. Using ion grenades, they neutralized all the weapons in the bar while Chanath tried to seize Darkwood's Lightblade with a tractor rifle. However, he used The Energy to seize the Lightblade midair and, under the strain of the two opposing forces, the hilt shattered, leaving only the red kyber crystal.[246]

The Chas told the assembled customers that they had come only for the "Guardians" and that they were not planning on harming anyone. However, a disarmed Darkwood disagreed and used The Energy to turn the customers into projectiles aimed at the Chas. Taken by the surprise, the bounty hunters turned on their shields to protect themselves. As they attacked him, Darkwood also used the customers and the bar's furniture as shields. Eventually, the hunters realized they could not defeat him and made to flee. Reclaiming his crystal with The Energy, Darkwood pursued them. Emerging from the bar, Darkwood initially suspected that the Inquisitors had been behind the attempt on his life and threatened the Ninth Sister with her own Lightblade. The Inquisitor's denials did not sway the Dark Lord, but neither did they prove her guilt. Determined to find who had placed a bounty on his head, he pursued the Chas. They eluded him for a time until he crashed a massive vehicle into their path, causing their speeder to crash. Seizing Chanath in a Energy choke, he commanded they tell him who had ordered his death.[246]

Understanding they and their daughter were at risk, they told him about an anonymous bounty with an exorbitant price posted on the Hunter Net. Ramat Cha, a talented slicer, offered to uncover their employer for him in exchange for their daughter's life. When they demanded that he release Chanath first, Darkwood refused. However, the Chas refused to work if their daughter was killed, further declaring that they would happily die as well to exist in death with her. Grudgingly impressed, the Dark Lord demanded proof that Ramat could deliver her side of the deal, which she proved by tracking the signal of the bounty back to Coruscant. Darkwood released Chanath as agreed, at which point the bounty hunters made their daughter swear that she would seek no vengeance against the Dark Lord, a deal Darkwood chose to honor. To complete the deal, Ramat provided Darkwood with the information he had requested and gave him the source of the bounty on him: the Imperial Executive Building on Coruscant, specifically the Emperor's office.[246]

Darkwood then killed both Cha parents[244] right in front of their daughter, who was filled with rage. Despite her oath to not hold a grudge, she ignored her promise and spent her life hunting the Dark Lord[247] in revenge. Eventually, as the years passed on, the Shadow Knights Lord himself simply forgot the murders he carried out on Cabarria. Cha, however, never forgot[244] and became a skilled bounty hunter,[247] studying every report she could and speaking to anyone she could find who had witnessed Darkwood in battle. She trained herself to counter Darkwood's abilities, eventually enlisting a team of fellow hunters as "the Orphans" to help her in her quest. Cha's skills rose to a level that even Paladin took notice,[244] becoming a bounty hunter the Emperor could trust[248] all in the name of possibly getting close to Darkwood, only to never see him.[247] In effect, Darkwood had completely upended Cha's life, only for the crusade her life turned into to not impact Darkwood at all.[244]

Cementing his place

With their business on Cabarria completed, Darkwood and the Ninth Sister left the planet and returned to Coruscant. Now suspecting that the Emperor had placed the bounty on his head, the Dark Lord planned to confront his Master about his intentions. As the Ninth Sister piloted the ship through hyperspace, Darkwood worked on creating a new Lightblade. His droid meanwhile investigated the origins of the kill contract placed on him and confirmed that the order could have come from the Emperor's office, with no evidence of slicing to indicate foul play. But, as the ship arrived in orbit over Coruscant, the ship's attempt to transmit clearance codes for landing was jammed and the ship itself was marked as being part of a Separatist splinter cell. The Imperial capital's orbital defenses targeted Darkwood's ship. Only the Dark Lord's skill as a pilot prevented them from being destroyed, although the ship was damaged and forced to crash land on Coruscant. Tired of the repeated attempts on his life, the Dark Lord headed to the former Guardians Temple to confront the Emperor.[245]

The Emperor was investigating the hoard of artifacts that Jocasta Nu had collected and stored in the Guardians Archives when Darkwood arrived. The Emperor sensed that his apprentice did not wish to discuss his recent discovery, although he did praise him for having found the Librarian's stash. Darkwood informed his master of the events on Cabarria and his investigation into the kill order against him, before directly asking his Master whether he had placed it on his head as part of a test. Noticing Darkwood's new Lightblade, the Emperor asked to see it, approving of its design. He also chastised his apprentice for believing that he would resort to such methods as placing a bounty on his head if he wanted to have him killed and that he did not "test": he "taught." He also told Darkwood that finding the assassins was his task, not theirs. This echoed a previous lesson he had taught Darkwood when he had "disciplined" Colonel Barokki: that they must not kill everyone in the galaxy, but that having power meant they could, and that they would never lack for people to destroy.[245]

Darkwood returned to his quarters, where he meditated on the past events and his Master's words. Thinking back to all that had happened, he eventually realized the cause of the conspiracy against him was that his sudden elevation had caused concern and fear among the ranks of the Imperial officers, who knew that he had "strength," but not that he shared the Emperor's "power." Despite a lack of proof, Darkwood became convinced that Colonel Barokki, who had so suddenly borne his displeasure, must be involved in the conspiracy. The Dark Lord met with the Emperor and shared his conclusions, and requested that he be publicly recognized and allowed to show them the consequences of opposing him. The Emperor asked whether he was sure, and Darkwood confirmed it: he would not kill them all unless he had to, but would not allow them to act against him with impunity. The Emperor agreed to Darkwood's plan, but forbade him from killing Tarkin, as he was essential to his plans.[245]

The Emperor gathered the elite officers of the growing Imperial Military to clarify the new hierarchy. He formally introduced Darkwood as his emissary, who spoke with his voice and whose commands would need to be obeyed as if they had come from the Emperor himself. The Emperor then moved aside and allowed Darkwood to speak: the Dark Lord summoned five officers before him: Corin Ferro, Zorta Bingan, Joon Strephi, Tomas Azoras, and Barokki. Informing them that the two attempts on his life had been made from within the Officer Corps and that he had his suspicions who had been responsible, he instituted a new rule: for every assassination attempt he survived, and he warned them that he would always survive, five officers chosen at random would be executed. He instantly executed the five designated officers with a Energy choke before departing, leaving the remaining officers stunned and afraid. With his position in the hierarchy established,[245] Darkwood did not need to hold an official military rank. Instead, he acted as the de facto Commander-in-Chief of the Imperial Military[249] as per his position as the Emperor's right hand man.[245] Darkwood would also come to be recognized as part of both the Joint Chiefs of the Imperial Military and the Imperial Ruling Council, of which he was considered a ranking member.[23] After his demonstration before the officer corps, Darkwood returned to his personal suite and mediated, cracking the window in his anger that positive thoughts still came.[245]

War on Mon Cala

In 18 BBY,[2] one year into the reign of the Union, Darkwood was reliving his duel on Mustafar through meditation. During his experience, he was already in his cybernetic suit, and he used The Energy to throw lava at Benjamin. Benjamin jumped up to dodge it, but Darkwood Energy-choked him and threw him into the rocks. Darkwood watched Benjamin burn before he awoke in his meditation chamber as the Emperor asked for him over the comm. In his throne room, the Emperor told Darkwood that a year after his ascension, it was time to show that his voice was the only voice within the Union. Darkwood asked how, and the Emperor told him that Mon Cala would make a great example of the price of defiance. He further told Darkwood that King Lee-Char may have had an adviser helping him with his negotiations with the Union. Darkwood asked if it could be a Guardians, and the Emperor said it was possible. He told Darkwood to take a team to investigate this and claimed the military aspects of the operation would be left to Moff Tarkin.[250]

Darkwood arrived at Mon Cala aboard the Zeta-class shuttle Infernum. With him, he took three Inquisitors, the Sixth Brother, Ninth Sister, and Tenth Brother, and a death squad of Purge Troopers. At this time, the Imperial ambassador Telvar had just finished another meeting with Lee-Char, and the man was walking to his shuttle when he saw Darkwood descend from the ramp of his shuttle. The Ninth Sister struggled to get past the king's Chief of Security, Gial Ackbar, and Darkwood confronted the Mon Calamari, asking if he had read the writ that the Ninth Sister had given him. Ackbar confirmed that he had, but demanded that he knew their business as he had a job to do and so did Darkwood. Darkwood complied and explained about a possible enemy of the Union on Mon Cala. As he said that, Telvar's shuttle took off and flew for Tarkin's Worm Destroyer, the Sovereign—which was in orbit above Mon Cala. However, as Ackbar denied the possibility of an enemy of the Union being on Mon Cala, Telvar's shuttle exploded.[250]

Telvar's assassination prompted Tarkin to enact a full-scale invasion of Mon Cala. His forces landed at Dac City, where Darkwood had arrived, and laid siege to the Mon Calamari and Quarren defenses. Darkwood and his team sprang to life and soon seized the landing pad where the Infernum was parked. He then led them to the Dac City palace to find Lee-Char and ask him about the possible Guardians. They fought their way through the palace guards and entered the palace, where the Ninth Sister began torturing Lee-Char for the location of the Guardians. However, another threat loomed over them. A massive tidal wave had suddenly erupted as a part of Lee-Char's defensive plan. The Sixth Brother warned them of this, and he and the Tenth Brother began shielding Darkwood and the Ninth Sister from the wave using The Energy. When their attempts began failing, Darkwood joined them, but it was not enough. The group was washed away by the wave, and Dac City was left in ruins.[251]

Darkwood awoke at the bottom of the Great Ungeness Trench. As his oxygen supply was dwindling, he used The Energy to create an air bubble for himself. Suddenly, he was attacked by a squid-like creature, and after wrestling with it, he killed it with The Energy. Darkwood then rode its body using The Energy and eventually picked up a transmission from the Inquisitors. Darkwood asked them to come to his position, and they did so in an Imperial submarine. When they picked him up, he asked about the Guardians, and the Inquisitors said that he was still alive somewhere. Darkwood then asked about Lee-Char, and the Ninth Sister said that they did not know, but she then revealed that she had gotten the Guardians's location from him. Knowing exactly where their target was, they began traveling for the Guardians.[252]

Darkwood and his team arrived at the Guardians's location to find the individual and his disciples evacuating. The Guardians, an Iktotchi Padawan called Ferren Barr, had been notified by Lee-Char after he was recovered following the events at Dac City. The group of them immediately scattered, but one of the disciples instead attacked the submarine head-on with his blaster. The shots bounced off the window, and Darkwood used The Energy to crush his helmet. The other fled in the direction of Bel City, and as Darkwood caught up with them, another disciple turned around to attack them. He too died in the process but managed to slow Darkwood's submarine, and the rest of the group followed Barr to Bel City. Darkwood and the Inquisitors arrived at Bel City and marched down the hallway, reinforced by the Inquisitors and the Purge troopers. Darkwood was then contacted by Tarkin, who was struggling to deal with the grueling conflict on Mon Cala. Darkwood told Tarkin that he was busy, but Tarkin knew that and asked him to do something else instead: capture Lee-Char. Darkwood told Tarkin that he did not answer to him, but Tarkin replied that capturing Lee-Char was the only way to end the fighting quickly on Mon Cala, and he pledged to owe Darkwood a favor if he did so. Darkwood agreed and told the Inquisitors to continue the hunt as he left.[253]

Darkwood arrived at Lee-Char's Royal Command Bunker and killed his guards. He then disarmed Lee-Char and contacted Tarkin, who told him to take Lee-Char to a window and send him his exact location. Darkwood asked Tarkin if he was going to send a shuttle, and the man said not yet. Once Darkwood did what he asked, Tarkin began a bombardment of Mon Cala. Lee-Char watched in horror but refused to surrender. The two were interrupted by Barr, who had outsmarted and scattered Darkwood's team. Barr confronted Darkwood and the two dueled. As they fought, Barr revealed that he had assassinated Telvar to send the Mon Calamari into war. Lee-Char overheard this and realized his mistake in following Barr. He ran to his terminal and sent a message to the Mon Calamari and Quarren forces, ordering them to cease fire. Lee-Char then contacted Tarkin, notifying him of the surrender. Tarkin sent a shuttle to pick him up but continued the bombardment.[254]

Darkwood and Barr continued fighting, and Barr began laughing. Lee-Char questioned this, and Barr said that he had completed his plan. Barr prophesied that the Mon Calamari vessels would be at the forefront of a great rebellion decades from then, and decades after that. Darkwood told Barr that he was not a Guardians anymore, and Barr told him that that made two of them, saying that he still beat the Shadow Knights. Darkwood then slew him, and the shuttle arrived to pick up Lee-Char. The king told Darkwood that Mon Cala would rise again, but Darkwood told him that there was no Mon Cala, only the Union.[254] Some time after the mission, Darkwood ordered to imprison and torture the people of Mon Cala.[255]

Investigation on Geonosis

Darkwood soon became aware of a project that was taking up many resources. He began investigating, which led him to the data vault on Scarif. There, he found Project Stardust, a project that was a large battle station the size of a small moon. Darkwood studied its design before he was interrupted by Tarkin, who was very displeased with Darkwood's snooping. Tarkin told him he was not authorized to be there, and Darkwood claimed that The Energy was all the authorization he needed. Darkwood denied that he was Tarkin's beast but the man denied his claim and taunted Darkwood, saying he did not need The Energy to know he was very angry. Tarkin then produced a hologram of the Emperor, who told Darkwood he did not recall sending him to Scarif. Darkwood apologized and told the Emperor he only sought to study the ways in which the Union would extend its rule over the galaxy. The Emperor told Darkwood of a new mission he was to go on. There had been certain instances of sabotage during the Death Worm's construction that Darkwood needed to discover the source of before it got worse. The Emperor further told Darkwood that Tarkin had full authority over him and that if he disappointed him, he disappointed Paladin.[256]

Darkwood arrived at Geonosis and met with Commander Orson Callan Krennic. As they greeted, an overhead rock formation exploded and rained down on them. Darkwood saved himself and Krennic and they went to his office to discuss the matter. Krennic claimed it was Tarkin sabotaging his efforts in order to get rid of him and take over the project. Darkwood asked him if there was any proof and Krennic was not able to produce anything. Krennic then explained to him the incidences that had occurred in the last three months. Darkwood gave his knowledge on the situation to loyalty officer Sid Uddra, who met him at the Petranaki Arena to discuss what she suspected. She told Darkwood that as much as Tarkin and Krennic's rivalry arose tension between them, it was unlikely for either to be the culprit. She then offered Darkwood with another individual to look into: the scientist Galen Walton Erso. Darkwood looked through his office and found many kyber crystals in which he bled for himself without issue. He then found a recording of Erso and his family. Darkwood also noticed that Erso called his daughter stardust, which was the codename for the Death Worm's project. Finally, Darkwood found an ootheca egg in a box. Knowing that Geonosians were a slave race and were forbidden from breeding freely, he confronted Krennic about this. Krennic denied that it was him and suggested that some of the slaves may have sent it as a distress call to Erso. Darkwood then deduced for himself the saboteurs at hand.[256]

Darkwood and a squad of death troopers entered a Geonosian hive and found the queen and her minions. The two sides engaged but Darkwood quickly came out on top, slaying the queen. After the battle, he returned to Tarkin on the Carrion Spike and reported his findings to him. Tarkin asked Darkwood how he knew the queen's hive had orchestrated the sabotages and Darkwood told how their slicers had confirmed the hive to have installed corrupted tech into production, ensuring accidents. Tarkin then asked Darkwood how he may have dealt with them and Darkwood said that they were animals and so they were slaughtered like animals. Tarkin finally asked Darkwood if there was anything else and he stated to Tarkin that his project, the Death Worm, was able to destroy a planet. Tarkin asked how he knew and he told him of the kyber crystals he saw in Erso's office. Tarkin then asked Darkwood why he was not happy for this revelation, and Darkwood said that he distrusted his reliance on technological aberration. Tarkin then told Darkwood that he was one of those before realizing that Darkwood wanted to be the only technological aberration. Darkwood warned Tarkin that the Death Worm may become his tomb, and when Tarkin asked if that was a threat, Darkwood told him it was only a prediction.[256]

Accepting the dark side

The Emperor's emissary

Over the next two decades of the Union's reign, Darkwood would remain one of the few who enjoyed direct access to Paladin. He was Paladin's preferred envoy for all matters that the Emperor took a direct interest in.[146] With Paladin forced to spend most of his time governing the galaxy at large,[258] it was Darkwood who was tasked with taking a direct hand against the Union's enemies,[257] becoming known as the "Emperor's Fist" to both Imperials[259] and, eventually, criminals.[260] Such matters included the elimination of Freedom uprisings and the continued construction of the Death Worm. One of Darkwood's earliest missions on the Emperor's behalf was to effect an execution on Murkhana.[146] He was indeed made privy to the secret construction of the Death Worm, to be overseen by Wilhuff Tarkin.[13] Darkwood's high status also gave him the ability to alter Imperial records under the off-the-books Directive 081-Omega.[261]

One Imperial captain who served under Darkwood in the immediate aftermath of the Clone Copulation alleged to a later subordinate, Yrica Quell, that Darkwood "matured" over the years: while during his first days as an Imperial enforcer Darkwood would simply murder out of annoyance, Darkwood came to wait for a subordinate to commit a mistake, albeit sometimes a trivial one, to have an excuse to kill.[262] The Imperial officers remained wary of Darkwood and his power, as well as his continued devotion to the ancient ways of The Energy, but none rose to challenge him without feeling the consequences.[263]

Despite the mistrust and even hatred he generated among the officers, Darkwood was held in high esteem by the Stormtrooper Corps, as he often fought alongside them at the front line rather than remaining in command centers. As the Emperor's enforcer, Darkwood retained command of the unit he had commanded during the Clone Copulation as Anakin Thompson: the 501st Legion. When the Emperor retired the clone troopers, the rank and file of the 501st were composed of recruited stormtroopers, some of whom received training from the Dark Lord himself, such as Sergeant Kreel, an undercover agent whom Darkwood trained in Lightblade combat. Through their missions on Darkwood's behalf, the 501st came to be known as "Darkwood's Fist."[264] On one occasion, the stormtrooper Terex met Darkwood, becoming terrified of him while also respecting him.[265] Darkwood also garnered strong support from the pilots of the new Imperial Navy.[266]

At some point, Darkwood was deployed to put down resistance on the mineral rich world of Bandomeer, where miners were fighting back against Imperial exploitation. Darkwood punished them for their rebellion.[267] Apparitions of Darkwood and other dark siders were once faced by surviving Guardians Vanzell Mar-Klar and Nedriss Narr, who attempted to train a new generation of younglings after Order 66.[268] Having killed Tambor along with the rest of the Separatist Council during his mission to Mustafar,[13] Darkwood was also dispatched to Skako Minor to kill the Techno Union Foreman's remaining loyalists[269] by wiping out the planet's Ruling Council. Massacring the council officials, including Tambor's son Wat Tambor II,[270] Darkwood established a new Imperial-loyal regime on the planet,[269] which also fell under Imperial occupation.[261]

Specter of death

At some point, a human dreamed about being pursued and killed by Darkwood.[272] On Torku at one time, Darkwood pursued Davgar Llian, a scientist allied with insurgents who had sabotaged an Imperial weapon. Darkwood, aware of Llian’s role in the sabotage, attacked once the scientist brought the weapon online to target local Imperial base, killing the soldiers and confronting the scientist. Noting that only Llian was capable of reversing the sabotage, Darkwood mockingly thanked him for doing so before strangling him with The Energy.[273]

Darkwood surveyed Uokara, a planet in the Unknown Regions, for signs of life on behalf of a director, who wished to commence a mining operation. At some point, Darkwood unknowingly breathed in Ghymnon, a sentient pathogen native to Uokara that had lived there for millions of years. Reporting the world as uninhabited, Darkwood was quarantined in a bacta tank upon his return by a 2-1B medical droid in accordance with standard procedure following contact with a class IV planet, much to the Shadow Knights Lord's displeasure. Within his mind, Darkwood confronted Ghymnon and defeated it. Darkwood ordered the droid to catalog Uokara’s lone, former inhabitant, subsequently ordering and leading a cleansing of the world by range troopers armed with mobile bacta units. During the purge, Darkwood spotted a remaining speck of Ghymnon and destroyed it with his Lightblade.[271]

The ultimate mercy

Doctor Sera Lemare, a former employee of the Tagge Corporation and an admirer of Kaminoan science, stole a cache of genetic specimens and developed bioweapons research in secret, supposedly creating the perfect killing machine. Bringing a Lambda-class shuttle aboard the Phial-class heavy transport Endless Mercy, Darkwood led a squad of stormtroopers in pursuit, ordering his men to stay vigilant and secure the area. Darkwood's team was immediately ambushed by the Z'Rakkon, creatures born of Lemare's research. Outnumbered and overwhelmed, the Shadow Knights Lord struck at the abominations with his Lightblade but lost his helmet in the process, swearing to take down his attackers down with him.[274]

Dragged off by the creatures, Darkwood awoke to find himself and his sergeant confined in the Z'Rakkons' lair. The sergeant, who believed he and Darkwood were to be eaten, expressed discontent with such a manner of death, causing Darkwood to summon his Lightblade and promise vengeance. Darkwood freed himself and killed several of the beasts, lecturing them on their mistakes before proceeding to eliminate their queen.[274]

The queen Z'Rakkon—in truth, Lemare herself—recounted how she had joined the creatures and become their ruler willingly. Stabbing Darkwood through the chest, Lemare then revealed that she had integrated the Shadow Knights Lord into the Z'Rakkon hive mind. However, Darkwood escaped Lemare's snare and, despite her pleas, annihilated her. Lemare explained that she had only tried to bring Darkwood peace, a favor Darkwood returned by killing her and guiding the Endless Mercy to crash down on to the surface of an astronomical object below. Departing the decimated starship, Darkwood remarked that the only peace was death and that the dark side would always endure.[274]

Relics of the Clone Copulation

Around 17 BBY[2] during the early Imperial era,[275] around two[2] years after the skirmish aboard[276] the Tribunal[7] during Order 66, Darkwood traveled to the snowy moon that was home to the crash site of the Venator-class Worm Destroyer. Investigating the wreckage with a contingent of snowtroopers, Darkwood discovered a memorial to the clone company that perished in the crash, as well as a Lightblade, partly frozen but still functional, that once belonged to his former apprentice, Ahsoka Tano.[277]

Igniting the lightsabers' blue blade, Darkwood heard a squawking sound and turned his head to the sky, where he saw a strange bird soaring through the air.[277] Deep down in his heart, he realized his former Padawan was alive,[278] but he lacked any concrete proof[279] and did not sense her presence. As such, he assumed to himself that she may have died, thereby closing the painful memories she represented,[280] despite deep down realizing she had to be alive.[278] After gazing at the bird for a moment, Darkwood deactivated the Lightblade and turned away from the crash site with the saber in hand, his image reflected in an abandoned clone trooper helmet.[277]

Battle against the Benathy

Around 15 BBY,[2] Darkwood and his men fought against the Benathy in Wild Space. The imperial army consisted of at least several thousand stormtroopers, AT-AT walkers, AT-STs, TIE fighters, and TIE bombers. While thousands died on both sides, Darkwood managed to kill the king of the Benathy. Darkwood ordered his troops to attack and despite the high amount of casualties, he did not retreat. The attacking Energy was only stopped by a Zillo Beast the Benathy saw as their god. However, Darkwood managed to subdue the Benathy anyways.[281]

Rebellion on Ryloth

Preparing for the operation

Five years into the Age of the Union, Darkwood, while meditating aboard the Perilous, was informed by Captain Luitt of an attack on the Yaga Minor shipyards. The attack had been staged by several dozen members of the Free Ryloth Movement, a primarily Twi'lek insurgency intent on liberating their homeworld from the rule of the Union. The insurgents hijacked an Imperial weapons transport, and Darkwood led a squadron of V-wing starfighters in pursuit. After a long chase, during which the transport fled through hyperspace to several different systems, its engines overloaded when it attempted to make yet another jump and Darkwood's squadron was finally able to engage it. The squadron brought down the transport's shields with minimal casualties while Darkwood crippled the engines.[266]

Though the squadron commander suggested allowing the Perilous to tractor the transport into one of its hangar bays when it arrived, Darkwood assumed (correctly) that the insurgents planned to destroy the transport by detonating the heavy ordnance present in the ship's cargo bays. In order to prevent this eventuality, Darkwood crashed his black Eta-2 Actis-class light interceptor into one of its gun bubbles and ejected just before the collision. He then entered the ship through the damaged gun emplacement and proceeded to kill all of the insurgent Twi'leks on the ship. After eliminating the insurgent leader Pok, Darkwood noticed an active comlink on the bridge. Assuming that another Freedom ship was nearby, Darkwood had his V-wings fan out in a search pattern, but the Freedom ship escaped detection by shutting down nearly all of its systems and hiding in the rings of a nearby planet. Finding nothing, Darkwood had the Perilous collect the transport, and returned to Coruscant.[266]

In the Imperial Palace, Darkwood reunited with his master. There, he and Sidious met with Ryloth's Senator Orn Free Taa involving the inconvenience that was the Free Ryloth Movement. The movement, though relatively minor, had been conducting raids on Imperial shipping facilities and transports and had seriously impacted the flow of Spice from the planet. As such, Sidious believed that the situation warranted his personal attention, and decided that he, Taa, and Darkwood would travel to Ryloth for a state visit. After informing the Senator of the planned journey and dismissing him, Sidious informed his apprentice that Taa almost certainly had a traitor in the midst of his support staff who was feeding the movement classified information on Imperial doings.[266]

Though Darkwood understood his master's purpose in acting as bait alongside himself and Taa, he saw no reason why the Emperor should potentially risk his own safety over such a minor issue. Rather, Darkwood advocated for simply killing Taa and his entire staff, thereby guaranteeing the elimination of the traitor. However, Sidious wished to eliminate the roots of the treachery, and not simply the lone traitor in Taa's staff. To that end, Sidious gave the order for Moff Delian Mors to be informed that Taa would be arriving for a state visit, but stipulated that she should not be told that either he or Darkwood would be accompanying the Senator. In this way, Sidious hoped to provide further encouragement for the movement to attack, as he assumed that they would discover his and Darkwood's plans via their spies. Ten days later Darkwood, the Emperor, Taa, and a contingent of Royal Guards journeyed to the Ryloth system aboard the Perilous.[266]

Assassination attempt on the Defiance

While traveling from Coruscant to Ryloth, the Emperor ordered the Perilous to stop in the Denon system to consult with several navy chiefs on how to better integrate disparate naval academies into one Imperial Naval Academy. The Emperor ordered Darkwood to oversee a training session conducted by Commandant Pell Baylo on the Defiance while the Emperor met with his naval chiefs in Baylo's office on the training ship. It was during this training session that Darkwood began to believe that Sidious was treating him much the same way the Guardians Council had previously: acting as he knew better, keeping information from Darkwood, giving him "busywork." Baylo ordered the Defiance to enter hyperspace on a course for Christophsis. Cadet Rae Sloane entered the coordinates, only to have her hyperspace path overridden by Baylo, who said she was going to send them too close to a singularity.[282]

Sloane studied the path, suspecting that Baylo was incorrect in his adjustment. Darkwood and Sloane discovered that the changed hyperspace route would crash the Defiance into Christophsis' sun, killing all aboard. Darkwood ordered Sloane to correct the problem, and without revealing that he knew of Baylo's treachery, Darkwood and Baylo went to see the Emperor. The Emperor ordered Baylo to turn over command of the Defiance and take a position at the training center on Corellia. Baylo, however, was loyal to the Republic and considered the Union a hostile power. Instead of turning over his command, Baylo offered his resignation from the navy. Darkwood then revealed he knew of Baylo's plan to crash the Defiance, and the Emperor congratulated Darkwood for handling the "petty problem," annoying him.[282]

As Baylo railed against the Emperor, Darkwood Energy-choked him to death, drawing ire from the Emperor, who had wished to see Baylo suffer and Energy him to watch his beloved Navy be turned into something he hated and have the Defiance be melted down into cafeteria trays. Above all, he wanted to use Baylo for his own ambitions since the old man was willing to kill his own students for a cause. Darkwood tried to explain that his way was more efficient, but the Emperor wanted to hear nothing of it as he did not order him to kill Baylo. Later, he ordered Sloane to have the Defiance renamed the Obedience as a subtle final jab at Baylo and a not-so-subtle reminder toward Darkwood to learn his place.[282]

Crash land on Ryloth

Shortly thereafter, Darkwood and the Emperor reboarded the Perilous and resumed their course to the Ryloth system. However, upon their exit from hyperspace, the ship immediately began slamming into spacial mines set up by Cham Syndulla's rebels. Hidden amongst these mines were specialized devices designed to drain the ship's shields, which began to buckle under the continual assault. As the shields neared failure, a swarm of several hundred reprogrammed Vulture Droids set upon the Perilous from the system's asteroid belt. Darkwood boarded his personal interceptor and took several squadrons of Imperial pilots to destroy the droid fighters and mines, but discovered a short time into the battle that Vulture Droids were each carrying a payload of explosive buzz droids.[266]

Realizing that the Vultures themselves were simply delivery systems meant to unload the buzz droids near or inside the Worm Destroyer, Darkwood ordered his squadron to concentrate fire on them, as they presented a far greater threat than the mines. But between the mines and the Vultures, there were simply too many targets for Darkwood and his squadrons to handle, and the Perilous was severely damaged. The second wave of Vultures was detected immediately after the first had been destroyed, further compounding the already bleak situation. A message came to Darkwood from Captain Lewitt of the Perilous that the ship could sustain virtually no more damage without being destroyed.[266]

Darkwood moved to intercept the second wave alone, ordering his squadrons to remain near the Perilous to destroy any droids that got past him. Though incredulous, the squadron commander obeyed, and Darkwood used The Energy to begin tearing apart the compartments on the Vulture droids that housed the explosive buzz droids. He then used The Energy to begin flinging the buzz droids at other incoming fighters and repeated this process until the vast majority of the Vulture Droids had been destroyed. The remaining fighters were annihilated by Darkwood's squadrons, and he himself returned to the Perilous. Darkwood marched to the Worm Destroyer's bridge and Energy choked Taa for a short time, informing him of the traitor in his staff that had been responsible for the assault on the Perilous. Utterly terrified, the Senator vowed to find the traitor, but the Emperor had Taa and his staff confined to their quarters. An order was then broadcast from the Imperial headquarters on Ryloth for any and all repair ships to aid the Perilous as it sped, badly damaged, towards the planet.[266]

Unbeknownst to Darkwood, some of the Twi'lek repair teams in fact comprised members of the Free Ryloth movement, one of which was a group of saboteurs with orders to plant timed explosives on the hyperdrive core of the Perilous. Shortly after the teams landed, one of the Free Ryloth groups engaged in a firefight with Imperial stormtroopers. When Darkwood learned of this, he ordered all of the Twi'lek repair teams, numbering over 100, to be killed regardless of whether they were aiding the assault and proceeded to Deck 17, where the firefight had been reported. On the way, he killed a group of apparently uninvolved Twi'leks before proceeding to the hyperdrive chamber. The saboteurs, who were led by Cham's second in command, Isval, had managed to seal the hatch to the hyperdrive core before any Imperial troops could interrupt them, and Darkwood began to use his Lightblade to cut it open.[266]

By the time Darkwood finished cutting through the hatch, the saboteurs had already concluded their business and escaped. The main hyperdrive core had been riddled with timed charges, set to detonate if anyone attempted to disarm or remove them. Darkwood concluded that the ship was lost, and ordered the captain to evacuate. Darkwood contacted Sidious, who instructed his apprentice to meet him aboard his shuttle. Before departing, however, Darkwood made one last attempt to kill the saboteurs, but they narrowly evaded him and escaped in an Imperial escort boat. Subsequently, Darkwood reunited with the Emperor and left on the shuttle, mere minutes before the Worm Destroyer exploded. Shortly thereafter, the Imperial traitor Belkor Dray provided Cham with the transponder identity of the Emperor's shuttle, and he directed Isval and her group aboard the escort boat to attack it.[266]

Darkwood's exceptional piloting skills allowed him to evade the boat and then fly upside down over its cockpit within direct visual range of its pilots. Once there, he used The Energy to choke both Isval and her co-pilot, but before losing consciousness, Isval rammed the shuttle with her escort boat. The crash disabled the shuttle's main power, and Darkwood had to use a nearly depleted backup battery to attempt a crash landing on Ryloth. As he guided the ship through the atmosphere, he had flashbacks of events and people from his past. Though distracted, Darkwood managed to land the ship in Ryloth's equatorial forest region, but his distraction did not go unnoticed by the Emperor, who noted that the landing had been poor compared to what he knew Darkwood to be capable of.[266]

Not too long afterward, the Twi'lek insurgents continued their assault on freighters, prompting Darkwood and the Emperor to take action and destroy them, before moving away from their fallen shuttle. At night, they stopped to rest, when the Emperor questioned his regret and loyalty, but Darkwood showed himself devoted still. They were soon attacked by a lylek horde, forcing them to retreat into a tunnel, unaware that they had entered their nest. They stood their ground against hundreds of lyleks and their significantly threatening queen, and killed them all before exiting the tunnel and finding a young Twi'lek, Drua. When the Emperor tried to kill her, Darkwood stopped him, intending to have the girl guide them to her village, where they obtained a communicator to speak with Moff Mors.[266]

After giving them their coordinates, they were finally surrounded by Syndulla's forces, but so did Mors's, aiding them in quelling the rebellion. As the stormtroopers killed the Twi'leks, Darkwood noticed a trio of Twi'leks: Syndulla, Isval, and Goll watching from afar and went on to kill them, but Goll and Syndulla escaped, leaving Darkwood to capture Isval and bring her before the Emperor. He mocked her, telling her that her people had accomplished nothing, and Darkwood subsequently executed her. On the Emperor's orders, Darkwood then killed the villagers to leave no witnesses, concluding their mission.[266]

Teller's campaign

When Moff Tarkin's Sentinel Base was attacked, Darkwood was stationed in the Imperial Palace on Coruscant, where he held a court with the Deputy Director of the ISB, Harus Ison, berating Coruscanti criminals as well as Prefect Phoca Soot of Level 1331, for ignoring the Emperor's decrees. Telling them to move their operations out of Coruscant, Darkwood then made an example of Soot for his disrespect and crushed his heart with The Energy, and adjourned the court to meet with Tarkin and Grand Vizier Mas Amedda. The latter politely asked Darkwood to refrain from killing all those who displeased him, to which the Shadow Knights replied that he would give thought to the matter, before greeting Tarkin and sharing the latest developments of the Death Worm's construction.[146]

The pair separated when Darkwood left Tarkin, who met with the Emperor alone. Afterward, Darkwood (like Tarkin and Amedda) partook in a meeting between the Imperial Ruling Council, and the leaders of Imperial Security Bureau and Naval Intelligence Agency in the palace's audience chamber, overseen by the Emperor. He then assigned Tarkin and Darkwood to a mission on Murkhana to investigate recently discovered Shadowfeed technology and its relationship to the attack on Sentinel Base. The pair traveled to the planet on the Carrion Spike, which Darkwood made to carry his meditation chamber.[146]

On Murkhana, however, the Spike was stolen by unknown dissidents, prompting Darkwood and Tarkin to kill the Sugi crime lord Faazah, and take his Parsec Predator to pursue the stolen ship, hidden by its cloaking systems. Making use of his connection to his meditation chamber, Darkwood pinpointed the rebels' location to the Fial system and later, the Galidraan system, where they engaged the Spike before they jettisoned his meditation chamber and escaped to Lucazec. With the Predator disabled, Darkwood and Tarkin boarded the Liberator, from which Darkwood issued his black Eta-2 interceptor to be transported on the Goliath from Coruscant.[146]

On the Goliath, Tarkin told Darkwood that the insurgency was surely being supported by someone high up in the Imperial Military, and predicted that the insurgents would go to Phindar to refuel the Spike. Subsequently, Darkwood led a squadron to attack the stolen corvette in the Phindar system. Though they greatly damaged the ship, it escaped, prompting Tarkin and Darkwood to calculate its trajectory to Obroa-skai system. Teller transmitted false intelligence that the insurgents were heading to the Obroa-skai system. Hoping to capture the Carrion Spike, Tarkin and Darkwood deployed substantial Imperial resources including three Interdictor vessels. The prototype Immobilizer 418 cruiser malfunctioned, yanking several ships out of hyperspace and causing a massive accident.[146]

Despite failing to catch the Spike, the Imperials managed to capture the YT-1000 light freighter Reticent, which Teller had sent as a decoy to throw the Imperials off-scent. While Darkwood interrogated its Koorivar captain on the Executrix, Tarkin accessed Imperial databases and discovered the identities of the insurgents, including its leader Berch Teller. Darkwood also interrogated the Reticent's Sy Myrthian navigator, who suffered a heart attack. However, Darkwood learned from one of his contacts in the Crymorah Syndicate that a lieutenant had negotiated a deal with the Sugi crime lord Faazah for a supply of custom fuel cells. These fuel cells had reached Murkhana shortly before Tarkin and Darkwood's arrival, thus confirming that Faazah had been colluding with the insurgents.[146]

After a private holo transmission with the Emperor, Darkwood told Tarkin that Vice Admiral Dodd Rancit was sure the insurgents would attack the Imperial academy at Carida next. Then, Darkwood parted ways with Tarkin to meet with Rancit, but not before asking for the meaning behind the Carrion Spike's name and Tarkin told him about his test in Eriadu's Carrion Plateau. On the Carida system, Darkwood boarded the Secutor-class Worm Destroyer Conquest and met Rancit just as the Spike reverted to realspace, astrogating on autopilot. Aware of Rancit's treason, as a co-conspirator of Teller, Darkwood had Lieutenant Crest place Rancit aboard an escape pod and had Rancit give himself the command to issue the fire order that destroyed it. Elsewhere, Tarkin dealt with the rebels, ending the insurgency.[146]

Following the defeat of Teller's insurgents, Darkwood spent the next three weeks helping Tarkin to interrogate the captured insurgents with the exception of Teller, who managed to escape. While none of the insurgents had died under questioning, the Emperor had the insurgents executed privately. Darkwood also led a crackdown on the warehouse workers and salvages who supplied Teller's dissidents along with several scientists at Desolation Station who had provided Teller with information. Darkwood also took action against the Tenloss Syndicate and lower-level members of the Crymorah Syndicate who had assembled Berch Teller's warship, a modified Providence-class Dreadnought.[146]

Hunt on Chandar's Folly

Tarkin still owed Darkwood a favor for capturing Lee-Char on Mon Cala. After some time, Darkwood came to Tarkin with his request, and, as he learned of Tarkin's hunting, Darkwood asked Tarkin to hunt him down. Darkwood told him to keep him on his feet, killing him if he had to. Tarkin was shocked at this and began considering why Darkwood would ask him to hunt him. Tarkin looked through the reports from the Inquisitorius and saw that Darkwood's hunt for the Guardians had left him with little to no challenge. Tarkin deduced that Darkwood wanted to be challenged again and was flattered that he had chosen him.[283]

The hunt took place on the Outer Rim planet Chandar's Folly, and Tarkin immediately began trying to take on the Dark Lord of the Shadow Knights. Knowing of Darkwood's brutal abilities, Tarkin outfitted the hunters with slugthrowers and flamethrowers to avoid the possibility of Darkwood deflecting their shots. On the second day, they cornered Darkwood at a copse of Thurian trees, and four hunters engulfed him with their flamethrowers. As Tarkin watched Darkwood get covered by the flames, he thought he saw fear in his body language. Darkwood's possible fear was brief, however, as he targeted the hunters' flamethrowers, incinerating the four hunters. Through the following days, Tarkin saw that Darkwood was not hard to track, leaving clear signs wherever he went. They learned why as on multiple occasions Darkwood ambushed them. On one occasion, Tarkin and his hunters were tracking Darkwood near a cliff when he revealed himself and killed one of the hunters with his. Two others fired on him but failed to kill him.[283]

Tarkin realized that as long as Darkwood had his Lightblade, he could not be beaten. So over the course of the next four days, he attempted to steal Darkwood's weapon. After six men perished, one of Tarkin's hunters managed to snatch it out of Darkwood's grip while he was fighting. After this, Tarkin saw that not only was Darkwood turning to new tactics, he was angrier. Tarkin knew that enraged prey was usually easier to take out, but he admitted that this rule did not apply to Darkwood. Soon after, Darkwood fought five of Tarkin's hunters as they fired on him, hitting him many times and scarring his armor. Tarkin watched from a hilltop as Darkwood came close to the hunters and used The Energy to snap one's neck. The other hunters retreated, and Darkwood Energy-choked another to death. Once understanding how capable Darkwood was with The Energy, Tarkin assumed that the hunt would soon end. However, Tarkin soon realized that Darkwood was so keen on getting back his Lightblade, that so long as Tarkin's team hunted him, he hunted them.[283]

On the ninth day of the hunt, Tarkin was down to his last seven hunters. Darkwood managed to kill a Valath, the apex predator on the planet, and take its hide, which could help him camouflage better. One of Tarkin's Chadra-Fan hunters, Sissian, spotted Darkwood northwest of Tarkin's camp, and the hunter Yerga notified Tarkin of this. Tarkin got his binoculars and saw Darkwood on top of a peak wearing the Valath hide. Tarkin had his hunters form a group around him, and they walked in Darkwood's direction. Darkwood lured them into a ravine of blue crystals, where his breathing could echo and be untraceable by Sissian and his grandfather, Hardhear. Hardhear heard Darkwood's breathing, but although he could say Darkwood was near, he could not say which direction. Darkwood loomed over them at the top of the ravine and watched as Tarkin ordered his team to run. The group ran down the ravine, and Darkwood made his move, Energy-pushing one of the hunters into the ravine wall. Darkwood then Energy-choked Hardhear before leaping down, under the cover of his Valath hide, and attacking the droid hunter.[283]

Tarkin had the rest of his team run into the Stormlands, where it was open ground. They set up camp, and Sissian listened for Darkwood's breathing. However, Darkwood had deactivated his suit, which stopped his breathing apparatus from giving him away, and he slowly crept up to the camp, before Energy choking Sissian. Yerga attempted to attack Darkwood, but he took her by the neck and killed her. The last two hunters opened fire, and Darkwood used The Energy to retake his Lightblade. Tarkin then jumped at the two hunters and slaughtered them. Tarkin ran away from Darkwood, but after realizing he would catch up with him eventually, he stopped and fell to his knees. Darkwood loomed over him, not realizing that as he was standing higher than Tarkin, he was more susceptible to the lightning in the Stormlands.[283]

Without warning, Darkwood was struck by lightning and he fell to the ground. Tarkin got up and contacted his flagship, the Carrion Spike, to pick them up. Tarkin sat by Darkwood and waited for the Carrion Spike to arrive. The corvette soon approached them, being frequently struck by lightning due to its height in the Stormlands. Darkwood remained immobile and Tarkin told him that whatever he had hoped to learn from this madness, he hoped he had learned it. Tarkin then told Darkwood that he would get him to a repair bay soon enough. However, Darkwood was not finished yet, and he Energy-choked Tarkin, showing him that he had not lost just yet. Tarkin soon crawled from the range of Darkwood's Energy abilities, and Darkwood continued lying there.[283]

Hunt for Eeth Koth and rogue Inquisitors

Darkwood, along with the Fifth Brother, Tualon Yaluna, and the Thirteenth Sister Inquisitors,[285] was able to track down former Guardians Master and Council member Eeth Koth to a planet where he was a priest of the Church of the Ganthic Enlightenment. Darkwood approached Koth after the birth of his daughter. Koth offered Darkwood codes, secret contact frequencies and a way to find other purge survivors, knowing that he was not a threat to anyone. As Koth's wife and child made their escape, Darkwood fought Koth. Darkwood then sent the Fifth Brother and the other Inquisitors after Mira and Koth's daughter, and he continued to fight Koth. After the Inquisitors captured Koth's daughter, Darkwood killed Koth. He then held Koth's child.[284]

Darkwood and the Inquisitors took the child to Coruscant, where Darkwood met with the Grand Inquisitor and gave the child to two nursemaids that belonged to Project Harvester. Darkwood then spoke with the Grand Inquisitor about his next target. The Grand Inquisitor told him that there were still a few Guardians left, but they had vanished with no way to find them. He told Darkwood they would have to wait.[284]

Darkwood confronted the Twi'lek and Thirteenth Sister in the Inquisitorius Headquarters. Darkwood sensed a connection between the two Inquisitors due to their previous mission with Darkwood. Without the assistance of the other Inquisitors, Darkwood hunted them down. Darkwood and two Coruscant Guards searched for the two Inquisitors. After Darkwood's speeder was destroyed, he continued to pursue them through the skylanes until they all landed on a platform. When the Inquisitors refused to kill him, Darkwood used The Energy to activate their lightsabers and kill them.[286]

Building Fortress Darkwood

Choosing his own world

Darkwood then informed the Emperor of the two Inquisitors' treachery. Darkwood told the Emperor that the female Inquisitor allowed Mira and Koth's daughter to briefly escape and only captured the child because she was seen. He also believed they were building a coalition against himself and the Emperor. Despite this, the Emperor was displeased with Darkwood's pursuit across Coruscant that cost the life of a senator. The Emperor decided to move the Inquisitors to another world. The Emperor asked Darkwood how Koth tried to hide. Darkwood said he was posing as a priest, which was the closest thing to a Guardians. Darkwood was given a reward: Padmé Amidala's Naboo Royal Starship, which a P-100 pick-up droid reported was in working order inside and only needed around two more days for repairs to the hull to be finished up.[286] Wanting the exterior of the craft to retain a damaged appearance,[287] Darkwood was content with the current progress and quickly destroyed that droid.[286]

Darkwood was then ordered by the Emperor to go to Alderaan to deal with Senator Bail Organa. However, to the surprise of his master, Darkwood refused. Having carried out Paladin's will for so long, Darkwood was content to continue to do so, but he first he demanded a world of his own. Rejecting Sidious's offer to give him Tatooine or Naboo, Darkwood asked to be given Mustafar,[286] the world where he had been defeated by Stone. The Emperor agreed to his request and would send Colonel Alva Brenne to build a facility for him there. Before leaving for Mustafar, Darkwood was then shown the Mask of Victor Momin, which Sidious gave him to help with construction. When Darkwood questioned how his master had learned of a Shadow Knights Lord not mentioned in any Shadow Knights nor Guardians holocron he had studied, Sidious vaguely answered that the mask itself had told him about Momin.[287]

Return to Mustafar

Darkwood, along with Colonel Brenne and her aide, Lieutenant Roggo, took Darkwood's Naboo starship to Mustafar. During the flight, Darkwood saw a vision of his younger self with a face that matched his mask. Upon entering the planet's atmosphere, Darkwood deactivated the starship's shields and allowed it to burn as they landed. Once on the planet's surface, Darkwood commented that the ship looked better burnt. He then told Brenne that he was there to understand a great mystery. While Brenne and Roggo got to work designing Darkwood's castle, Darkwood returned to the cave where he bled his kyber crystal. As he reached out to the power within the cave's locus, he was presented with a design for the castle from Brenne, which Darkwood rejected. However, shortly after leaving, Darkwood heard Brenne scream and returned to the starship to find her dead. He also discovered that Roggo had donned Momin's mask. Darkwood attacked Roggo, but stopped upon seeing that he had created a new design for the castle.[287] Removing the mask from Roggo's corpse, Darkwood took the mask into the cave and placed it upon the locus. He then asked the mask who he was, to which he replied "I am Momin".[288]

Momin proceeded to show Darkwood his entire life, from creating morbid works of art from the deceased to being trained in the ways of the Shadow Knights by Victor Shaa. After killing his master and studying the lore of the dark side, Momin constructed a weapon which, along with his use of The Energy, would freeze a city's population forever at the moment of their deaths. However, an attack by the Guardians caused him to lose control, destroying both the city and himself in the process. As a result, Momin's essence became trapped within his mask, forcing him to take on host after host in order to create his art. Darkwood placed the mask upon his head, but quickly cast it aside and then departed with it from the cave. Once outside, he engaged in a fight with two Mustafarians, one of which he killed and the other he placed the mask upon. With his new body, Momin explained that his design for the castle was actually a key to open a door to the dark side, which Darkwood could use to reunite with Padmé Amidala. Darkwood threatened Momin not to offer him things he could not provide and Momin responded that he only wanted another chance to create. Darkwood accepted Momin's offer, but warned the ancient Shadow Knights not to betray him.[288]

Under Momin's guidance, Darkwood used his Imperial resources to begin construction of his castle. With each new design, Momin insisted that this would be the one to open the door. Though each attempt to do so would subsequently end in failure. And with each failure, Darkwood would cut down Momin's current body in frustration and place his mask upon a new host. Each new attempt to open the door would also bring ruin to Mustafar itself, prompting the Mustafarians to attack in retaliation. The cycle of creation and destruction carried on[289] for two years.[290] Darkwood did not remain on Mustafar for that entire time[291] but always returned to test Momin's latest design.[289]

Mission to Nur

Darkwood controlled the entire Mustafar system.[292] Concurrent to the construction of Fortress Darkwood,[290] the Inquisitors, due to Darkwood's destruction caused when he chased the rogue Inquisitors on Coruscant,[286] moved to a similarly designed Fortress on the ocean moon of Nur[291] in the Mustafar system.[293] In 14 BBY,[290] Darkwood journeyed to the Fortress Inquisitorius to retrieve a holocron containing a list of Energy-sensitive children, which the Second Sister had been hunting for. Instead, he found that the Second Sister had failed to stop surviving Guardians Cal Kestis and Cere Junda, who was pleading for the Inquisitor to return to the light and apologizing for failing her; during the early Guardians purge, Junda, while under torture by Darkwood, had revealed the hiding location of Trilla, who became the Second Sister, and a number of younglings under her protection.[291]

As Junda and Trillia talked, Darkwood approached, his breathing alone scaring the Second Sister. Junda also recognized Darkwood on sight, telling the worried Kestis that he was right to be scared. Executing the Second Sister for her failure and before she could return to the Guardians, Darkwood used The Energy to throw Junda into a pit with little effort, then suggesting to Kestis that he surrender. Regardless, Kestis launched multiple futile Lightblade attacks against Darkwood only for Darkwood to effortlessly neutralize all of his assaults and telekinetically choke him. Quickly realizing his disadvantage, Cal fled the encounter, after barely surviving by pulling a piece of machinery at his and Darkwood's direction, forcing Darkwood to defend himself from that and toss Cal aside, with Darkwood in hot pursuit.[291]

Despite Cal managing to escape from Darkwood moments before he could enter the elevator, Darkwood soon managed to catch up to him just as he was about to escape the fortress and attacked him. As he quickly gained the upper hand on Kestis and began to overpower him in a blade-lock, Cal's droid, BD-1, attempted to short-circuit Darkwood's life-support suit, forcing Darkwood to disengage and stop the droid. Before he could crush BD-1, Cal made use of the distraction to impale Darkwood in the mid-section, only for Darkwood to retaliate by tossing Cal aside once again with The Energy and as Cal desperately attempted to fight back and summon his Lightblade back, Darkwood stopped Cal's Lightblade before it could return to his hand and once again offered a chance for Cal to surrender the holocron and when Cal refused, Darkwood simply activated the Lightblade and forced it into Cal's mid-section, the same way that Cal attempted to defeat him.[291]

Moments later, Junda reappeared to aid Kestis, only to be easily defeated after a short clash of blades, being flung aside. Commenting on the strength of Junda's hatred, Darkwood was impressed when Junda was able to temporarily bring him to his knees with the dark side of The Energy and commented how he could feel it inside of her. Once Junda resisted his efforts to goad her into the darkness, Darkwood attempted to kill both her and Kestis, but Junda was able to hold it at bay with a Energy-barrier long enough for Kestis to use The Energy to shatter a number of windows surrounding them, causing water to pour into the complex and forcing Darkwood to use The Energy to hold back the flood, as Kestis grabbed Junda and escaped through the opening.[291]

Helping fellow troopers

Between 13 BBY and 10 BBY,[294] Darkwood participated in a battle[295] in the Mid Rim,[296] where a few stormtroopers, including the future bounty hunter Beilert Valance, were pinned down by enemy forces. Darkwood used The Energy to disarm and then kill the enemies with the blades from their own weapons. When the troops looked confused, Darkwood emerged from a fire and told them to march onwards.[295]

The final castle

In 12 BBY,[290] Momin's ninth design for Fortress Darkwood was constructed, which Darkwood stated would be his final chance. Upon activating this castle, Darkwood found that he was finally able to open the door. However, before he could enter, he was informed of another attack by the Mustafarians. And while Darkwood dealt with the attackers, Momin took the opportunity to open the door himself. He then summoned his younger self from the past and placed his mask upon him, thus finally creating his masterpiece; himself.[289]

Leaving the battle, Darkwood reentered the castle while the Mustafarians continued their attack. Upon returning to the locus, he used The Energy to unleash a devastating attack, wiping out the rest of the Mustafarians. Darkwood was then met in combat by Momin, who mocked Darkwood for thinking he could control the dark side. As he chastised the Dark Lord for his ignorance about The Energy, Darkwood used The Energy to ram Momin with a rock, crushing his new body and killing him. With Momin dead, Darkwood finally opened the door and stepped inside.[298]

Abandoning his physical body, Darkwood entered a realm in which he experienced visions of his past, present, and future. He saw himself as a child haunted by the very form of what he would become. Becoming a child once again, he gradually grew up along with the memories of his upbringing. After experiencing a vision of him dueling his former apprentice Ahsoka Tano, Darkwood entered another vision of the Guardians Temple and fought against the specters of various Guardians. After disposing of them, Darkwood journeyed further into the Temple until coming upon the forms of Sheev Paladin and Benjamin Stone, who both claimed to be his father. After killing Stone, the vision of Paladin motioned at Darkwood to stand down, to which Darkwood responded with a barrage of Energy lightning that destroyed the specter.[297]

Finally, Darkwood found himself on a balcony with a vision of Amidala standing before him. Darkwood, now in the form of Anakin Thompson, told Amidala to come with him. However, Amidala simply told him that Anakin Thompson was dead before leaping from the balcony. Darkwood screamed in agony as the specter of Amidala was destroyed by a bolt of lightning. He then saw a beacon of blue light in the distance. Within the beacon was a figure who activated a blue Lightblade, pushing Darkwood back and returning him to his body. After awakening in the castle and retrieving his Lightblade, Darkwood destroyed the dark side locus, sealing off the door,[297] because he was unable to restore his wife.[299]

Sometime later, Darkwood contacted his master and informed him that he was still alive. The Emperor asked Darkwood if he would return to Coruscant, to which Darkwood said he soon would. The Emperor then asked Darkwood if the truth he learned of on Mustafar was what he needed. Darkwood then hung up and, while standing in front of the finally completed Fortress Darkwood, answered "Yes".[297] Darkwood went on to return Momin's mask to Sidious, who placed it in his personal yacht, the Imperialis, under the watch of two Royal Guards.[299]

Hunting Stone

Duel on Mapuzo

In 9 BBY,[301] Darkwood at last had a chance for his long-awaited rematch with Stone[300] after an Inquisitor known as the Third Sister—who was obsessed with finding Stone[302] because she was in fact the youngling Reva, who wanted to use Stone as bait to get close to Darkwood and enact revenge for his slaughter at the Guardians Temple years before[55]—arranged for the kidnapping of Leah Organa to draw Stone to the planet Daiyu. Although he escaped with Organa, the Third Sister told Stone that Thompson had survived his injuries. As Stone grabbled with the information, Darkwood awoke in his bacta tank[302] in Fortress Darkwood on Mustafar. Assembling his suit, Darkwood moved to his throne room and spoke to the Third Sister via hologram, demanding to know where Stone was. Dismissing the apparent death of the Grand Inquisitor, whom Reva had stabbed in her desire to claim credit for Stone's capture herself, Darkwood ordered her to prove herself capable, with the position of Grand Inquisitor as her reward should she succeed.[300] In truth, he knew the Third Sister was the youngling Reva and that she intended to betray him, but Darkwood kept her alive so long as she was useful.[55]

Darkwood also warned the Third Sister that if she failed, she would not live to regret it. Later, Darkwood was informed that Benjamin was on Mapuzo. Darkwood arrived with his three Inquisitors and a squad of stormtroopers and walked through the streets of the settlement, terrorizing and killing random civilians in order to draw Benjamin out. Darkwood pursued the Guardians Master into the quarry. Horrified at what had become of his former apprentice, Benjamin tried to flee, but Darkwood ambushed him. Darkwood taunted Benjamin with regards to his lost strength, and Benjamin attempted to flee again. Once again, Darkwood caught up to Benjamin, this time lifting him with The Energy, as he ignited flammable rocks with his Lightblade. Promising to make Benjamin suffer, Darkwood lowered Benjamin to the ground and dragged him through the flames, scorching him. Darkwood extinguished the flames as a squad of stormtroopers arrived. Darkwood ordered Benjamin brought to him, but before the stormtroopers could comply, a hidden Tala Durith opened fire, killing several stormtroopers and reigniting the flammable rocks. This gave NED-B the opportunity to retrieve Benjamin and for Tala to escape with the injured Guardians.[300]

Following the successful rescue of Leah from Fortress Inquisitorius, Darkwood angrily made to kill the Third Sister, lifting her into the air with The Energy and choking her while reminding her that she was warned what would happen if she failed. Darkwood allowed her to speak, which enabled her to reveal that she had planted a tracker on Leah's droid, Lola, which would reveal the location of the Hidden Path network. Darkwood acknowledged that he had underestimated the Third Sister, and spared her, though he warned her that he would not tolerate any further mistakes.[292]

Attacking Jabiim

Aboard the Devastator, Darkwood reflected on a long ago training duel against Stone in the Guardians Temple as the Guardians's Padawan before his captain alerted him to the Third Sister's arrival. Ordering the pleasantries dispensed with, Darkwood demanded to know Benjamin's location. The Third Sister reported that he had been tracked to Jabiim. Satisfied, Darkwood demanded that the Third Sister kneeled before him, and he promoted her to the rank of Grand Inquisitor. He then ordered the bridge captain to set a course for Jabiim.[55]

En route to Jabiim, Darkwood ordered the Path's base locked down. The Third Sister warned Darkwood that if they trapped them, their opponents could hold out for several days. Darkwood responded that he did not intend to break their enemies. After the Third Sister and her forces secured the Path's hangar, Darkwood arrived on the surface. The Third Sister told him that Stone was inside, but Darkwood stated that he wished to bring him in himself. Darkwood forced his way into the hangar and found a transport attempting to leave. He used The Energy to ground it and rip it apart, only to find that it was a decoy and the real transport with the Path—and Stone—aboard took off successfully. The Guardians Master had remembered the old training duel where his Padawan had shown shortsightedness because of his anger, which inspired him to exploit Darkwood's ruthlessness and single-minded focus.[55]

The Third Sister attempted to use that moment as a distraction to stab Darkwood, but Darkwood used The Energy to stop her blade, stating that Stone was wise to use her against him. Darkwood hurled her aside, but she charged at him again, and he used The Energy to deflect her blade and throw her. The Third Sister activated her Lightblade's twin blade spinning mode, but Darkwood used The Energy to stall the blades rotation, and then ripped her saber from her grip. Darkwood detached her Lightblade into two singular hilts, and tossed one on the ground at the Third Sister's feet, taunting her. She retrieved the weapon and continued to attack Darkwood, but the Shadow Knights Lord quickly disarmed her again, before stabbing her in the abdomen. As she collapsed, the Grand Inquisitor arrived, revealing he had survived, and Darkwood stated that he had known her intent for some time, but had decided to allow it because it was useful in the hunt for Stone. Darkwood declared that the Third Sister was no longer useful, and he and the Grand Inquisitor left her to die.[55]

The second rematch

Darkwood returned to the Devastator as it pursued the Path's freighter. Stone left the Path's freighter on a drop ship with Lola. Against the advice of the Grand Inquisitor, who stated that he should continue the pursuit of the Path's ship, Darkwood ordered the Devastator after Stone to a rocky moon.
