As they encountered each other, Darkwood ordered her not to struggle and Energy choked her, but Aphra claimed she knew of Jack's location. Darkwood released her and ordered Aphra to tell him, but she refused, claiming she would tell him later. He then let her go, using The Energy to drop debris above him as she escaped on the Ark Angel. He got out of the rubble as Thanoth and the stormtroopers arrived and claimed he had fallen into her trap. To follow her,[370] Darkwood and Thanoth boarded their Arquitens-class light cruiser. As they waited for their TIE/sa bombers to catch Aphra, Darkwood and Thanoth agreed to use their ready strike Energy to kill the Plasma Devils before they could escape.[371]

The two had their light cruiser go to the Thanteen Substrata on Anthan 1, where the Freedom cell was located. As per their plan, their light cruiser bombarded the westernmost tunnels and sealed them, whereas Darkwood went to the east route and felled incoming Freedom ships by throwing his Lightblade until the cell was annihilated. He then took three of the Plasma Devils' flight helmets and, alongside Thanoth, presented them to Tagge, who agreed with their course of action given the tight situation. As Karbin reported that the Yavin Freedom pilot was free, Darkwood and Thanoth left, and the Inspector let him know it had been an honor to serve alongside him.[371]

Shu-Torun

Darkwood traveled to Shu-Torun with a message to reinforce their cooperation and Energy its government to fulfill the quotas, while 0-0-0 and BT-1 killed the king and his closest servants. Expecting to meet the King of Shu-Torun, he instead met Princess Trios upon landing, who was to show him to the ball, a little celebration to showcase their culture. Soon after their arrival, however, Shu-Torun assassins that wished death to the Union and the king, but Darkwood successfully fended them off and demanded to see the king immediately. Trios complied and guided him through the tunnels as the main elevators were being watched by the traitorous dukes, according to her. Darkwood recognized Trios as admirable for her willingness to sacrifice herself for the greater good, as lava poured into the tunnels.[372]

However, he used his Lightblade to make a round platform to float above the lava and reach one of the exits above. Taking an unconscious Trios with him, Darkwood made it to one of the corridors where he killed the guards. Trios awoke, only to point a blaster at Darkwood. He, however, cut her hand off and entered the king's chambers to find the monarch, his other children and guards killed by Darkwood's assassin droids. With Trios as the new Queen of Shu-Torun, he delivered the Emperor's message; a piece of Alderaan, as a reminder of what happened to worlds that resisted his rule. Darkwood then told her that he would pacify Shu-Torun, but that she was to not forget whom she served.[372]

Vrogas Vas

Darkwood met with Aphra and the droids, and demanded that he tell her Jack's location: Vrogas Vas. She continued by saying she did want to work for him and show that she was trustworthy. After a moment, he conceded that his work alongside Thanoth had given him an appreciation of talent, and told her not to make him regret his decision. He left, saying it was a task for him alone. Unbeknownst to him, a probe droid from Karbin followed him.[371] In his TIE Advanced, he went to the planet's system where, upon coming out of hyperspace, he found three Alliance starfighter squadrons orbiting the planet. As he sensed Jack's presence, he remained and charged against the starfighters, who dismissed his TIE as a mere Imperial scout.[373]

During the battle, Darkwood engaged the X-wing starfighters, and managed to take out both Blue Squadron and Yellow Squadron, as he lost contact with Aphra. Darkwood was then confronted by Jack, who was flying as Red Five, and flew his X-wing into Darkwood's starfighter, hoping to kill him. During the collision, their stabilizers were damaged prompting their craft to crash on the planet's surface. There, Darkwood was hunted down by the Alliance forces, starting with Gray Squadron, but he promptly destroyed them by use of The Energy.[373] Afterward, a company of Freedom commandos moved in to attack him, but he activated some of their thermal detonators before they could throw them, taking out a tank by deflecting its shot against it and continued to reduce the Freedom forces.[374]

Darkwood also took the blaster array of his fallen TIE Advanced x1 and used it to take down further ships, this time from Cyan Squadron. Not too long afterward, another platoon was deployed to attack him, although this time Organa was leading them herself. Darkwood, choking her two companions, confronted her[375] but felt an incoming ambush. When he let her go so that she contacted Jack, Organa used the opportunity to arrange an airstrike on her and Darkwood's location. Instead, Commander Karbin arrived[376] with a task Energy, and tried to take his victory—both his son and the Princess' execution—from Darkwood. Subsequently, Darkwood engaged the enforcer in a duel, during which Organa escaped, and during which they entered the Guardians Temple in ruins.[377]

Inside the temple, he was momentarily distracted by ghosts of his past (Jinn's, Stone's, and Yorin's voices),[377] but continued the duel. Aphra then contacted him from aboard the Ark Angel and informed him that Jack had been captured by Karbin's guards. So Darkwood had her crash her ship against Karbin and confronted the mortally wounded Karbin, who realized there was more to the Yavin pilot and Darkwood's relationship than met the eye. Darkwood then finished him and contacted Aphra to tell her that her "pardon" depended on Jack's capture. With The Energy, he brought down the shuttle transporting Jack, and Aphra tried to capture Jack only to be captured herself by the Rebels, leaving Darkwood to watch as his son on the Millennium Falcon fled from Vrogas Vas.[378]

Shu-Torun War

Darkwood then returned to the Imperial Palace and brought Karbin's body to the Emperor, demanding that his master give him a fight worthy of his time. The Emperor then told Darkwood to return to Shu-Torun as the ore-barons were rebelling and he required a full military intervention, and that Cylo (per Tagge's request) was to join him in the task. As a result, Darkwood returned to Shu-Torun, alongside Cylo, the Astarte twins and Voidgazer. Accompanied by Queen Trios, Darkwood led an attack against a rebellious delving citadel, and destroyed it as a demonstration of Energy. He also accorded to treat Trios with the illusion of respect and tasked, in secret, a group of bounty hunters to find Aphra and return her to him, either dead or alive.[380]

After sending a broadcast to the barons, Darkwood, Trios, and Cylo and his enforcers went, per the queen's suggestion, to the ancestral retreat of the Shu-Torun royalty. There, they were soon attacked by Baron Rubix's Lava Leviathan, and Darkwood used Trios' escape vessel to reach the Leviathan and dared the twins to follow him. With their lightsabers, they boarded it, and from the inside, the three cut large holes that allowed the ship to flood with magma and be destroyed. Upon returning, Trios suggested that they attack Rubix next (as she had identified the ship's owner) and so, Darkwood told Cylo to prepare his enforcers. In the meantime, he killed Beebox for trying to deceive him with a stranger's disintegrated body, and refused Aiolin's request to train her.[381]

Soon enough, they carried out the siege to Rubix's delving citadel, using the Shu-Torun Loyalist/Imperial Delving Fleet's combat drills, with Darkwood heading the first wave alongside the Astarte. However, he was betrayed by Cylo, and his drill was redirected into a lithoporoite seam, where they were surrounded by Rubix's elite forces. With their outbound communications jammed, Darkwood left his troops as well as droid platoons to protect the drill and had the Astarte twins follow him as to use the enemy's lines to tell the rest of the assault fleet to resume the attack. On the way there, the twins attacked him,[382] but Aiolin saw they could not win against Darkwood and destroyed the pathway between them. She was then betrayed by her brother who pushed her into the lava and fled back to Cylo; Darkwood then took the dying Aiolin out of the lava and had her reveal Cylo's deceit before finishing her off.[379]

Darkwood then returned to his forces, who had secured the enemy lines thanks to 0-0-0's stratagem, and was surprised to find that Trios' assault to the delving citadel was already underway. With no opposition, Darkwood's combat drill resumed his way to citadel, and Darkwood himself cut off Rubix's escape way. Trios soon went to their encounter and Rubix formally surrendered; she then had the baron executed, and appointed his daughter as his successor, thus ending the rebellion. Darkwood then left the planet and informed the Emperor of Cylo's treachery, and the Emperor simply summoned him. He also received a message from Inspector Thanoth saying that he had located Aphra and that they needed to talk.[379]

Hunt for Cylo

At the shipyards of Kuat, Darkwood met with Sidious aboard an unfinished Executor-class Worm Dreadnought, the Executor. The Emperor explained that in order to save Darkwood after Mustafar, he had to resort to scientists who were similar in philosophy to the Shadow Knights, among them Cylo. Cylo eventually grew too powerful within the Tarkin Initiative and so Sidious was forced to make him overplay his hand. With the man a mere traitor, he could be purged. After the meeting, Darkwood met with Thanoth on Anthan 13; the Inspector, aware of Darkwood's real identity, revealed that he would support Darkwood's treason against the Emperor and that he would reveal Aphra's location on the Cosmatanic Steppes. However, Thanoth understood that Darkwood would not rest until he silenced a possible loose end, so he had elected to tell the Shadow Knights Lord in person so Darkwood would not waste time looking for him. When Thanoth revealed Aphra's location and thanked "Anakin" for their work together, Darkwood killed him with his Lightblade.[228]

Outside and ignoring the droids' request to dispose of Thanoth's corpse, Darkwood ordered 0-0-0 and BT-1, who were joined by Krrsantan and his commando droids, to return Aphra to the Executor or kill her as their priority orders.[228] Darkwood also made it clear to wipe out anyone else they found at the Cosmatanic Steppes to eliminate all witnesses present, ensuring that, if Aphra had told anyone about her work with Darkwood, they were silenced.[383] Darkwood then departed aboard his TIE Advanced x1, with 0-0-0 realizing he would not be taking Cylo back alive,[228] and returned to the Executor. Aboard, he confronted Professor Thlu-Ry, a colleague of Cylo's, whom he threatened to learn the traitor's location in the Crushank Nebulae. Darkwood took the Devastator to the nebulae, where his crew confirmed Cylo's whale fleet was present. After ordering that Thlu-Ry be thanked and then, for fraternizing with a traitor, executed, Darkwood led the assault on Cylo's fleet from his TIE Advanced. After Cylo's flagship fired an ion pulse to disable the Devastator and moved to escape, Darkwood fired into the whale ship and crashed into the opening he'd made, boarding the enemy ship just before it jumped to hyperspace[383]

Moving past the personnel he'd left to be sucked into hyperspace and entering the labs, Darkwood was confronted by Voidgazer, who pitted him against a cyberanimate rancor as a test of her technology[383] just as Cylo's fleet arrived at Kuat. Although the creature gave him some difficulty, Darkwood killed it by throwing his Lightblade through its skull, severing the neural link, and turned one of Voidgazer's defense droids against her, fatally wounding her. As she lay dying, Voidgazer revealed that she had tampered with the Executor's systems to incapacitate the crew; Darkwood finished her off and went to the empty bridge, which had been abandoned by Cylo and Morit as they had fled in the escape pods, to find that Cylo's flagship was to collide with the Executor.[384] Before it did, he ejected and landed on the Executor's outer hull, where he soon encountered Morit. He easily killed him and then made his way to the Executor's bridge, where he confronted Cylo. The scientist simply disabled Darkwood's cybernetics with a device he had kept for twenty years, forcing Darkwood to kneel and drop his Lightblade.[229]

While Darkwood was incapacitated, he received visions of his duel with his former master, Benjamin Stone. This time, however, Stone threw him into the lava. Darkwood then rose out of the river in his life suit and struck down his old master. However, like their duel on the Death Worm, Stone's body disappeared. Darkwood then encountered a vision of his younger self. The two versions of Thompson fought until Darkwood came out victorious, throwing his counterpart in the lava. After his counterpart yelled that he hated him, the Dark Lord met a vision of his deceased wife, Padmé Amidala. Darkwood then found himself chained to the same table where he first received his suit. The vision of Padmé pleaded with him, asking him to stay with her. Darkwood, however, choked the illusion while at the same time regained control of his body. After he told the vision that her husband was dead, Darkwood used his Lightblade to impale Cylo, who was astounded that Darkwood was moving while his suit was deactivated. Thus, Darkwood had regained the ship from his control. The Dark Lord then informed his master of his success. The Emperor then ordered Darkwood to finish his mission then return to him, stating there was much to discuss.[385]

Darkwood took control of his TIE Advanced, flying into Cylo's command ship. After cutting his way through Cylo's forces, the Dark Lord found his way to the doctor's cloning room. After destroying Cylo's spare bodies, Darkwood made it to the command bridge. Darkwood then confronted the doctor one last time. He mind-tricked the whale-ship into flying into a nearby Worm, destroying Cylo once and for all. After returning to the Executor, Darkwood met with his master, whom had been awaiting a full report, only to reveal it was no longer needed because Aphra was present:[386] while 0-0-0 and BT-1 had successfully brought Aphra to the Executor and were planning on giving Darkwood suggestions on how to kill the doctor, should they have found his planned execution too boring for their tastes, Aphra realized Darkwood's priority order only mandated the droids bring her to the Executor. As she realized as such and was dragged away by two stormtroopers to await Darkwood's wrath, she gave a new priority order: help her escape.[383] Reaching Paladin's chamber, Aphra told Paladin all of Darkwood's secret missions and plans, although she did chose to leave out the details surrounding Jack Thompson.[386]

Instead of being angry with Darkwood, however, the Emperor was pleased with his apprentice's ability to operate in the shadows, stating he was everything he could have hoped for. The Emperor then left his apprentice to deal with Aphra as he pleased. Darkwood then ordered Aphra to follow him. Despite her pleas for a quick death, he forced her out the airlock, although she secretly survived; having deduced Darkwood would never honor her wish to avoid execution via airlock, she was rescued in time by Krrsantan, 0-0-0, and BT-1. Making his way to the bridge, the Emperor informed him that Grand General Tagge was to be demoted and that command of the Executor and the fleet would be transferred to Darkwood,[386] who Paladin named supreme commander of the Imperial fleet.[387] The Emperor then left his apprentice to "educate" his subordinates of this new era. Darkwood then executed Tagge and ordered Admiral Kendal Ozzel to have the ship ready in two weeks. Darkwood then thought of his son, content that he would soon have him.[386]

Slaying the unworthy

After the rebellion began encroaching in the Mid Rim, The Emperor let Darkwood loose, deciding that incompetence at the highest level was to blame for the the Death Worm's destruction, allowing him to kill Imperial officials including Moff Coovern and Minister Khemt.[388]

At some point after the Executor became his flagship, Darkwood summoned Lieutenant Sulaco to the bridge and ordered her to follow him to a hangar bay, where he ordered that she pilot his Lambda-class shuttle on a diplomatic mission to Sarjenn 3, the third moon in the Sarjenn system and Sulaco's homeworld. The two Imperials landed at the moon's only port and proceeded to a nearby Freedom outpost as Sulaco questioned their mission, claiming that Sarjenn 3 had no diplomats. Darkwood was quickly ambushed by a sniper, whom he dispatched, before approaching the stronghold’s outermost wall, telekinetically relieving the Rebels of their weapons, and slaughtering them. Proceeding inside, Darkwood deflected a shot from a missile launcher, whose gunner he strangled and brought down a massive building with The Energy. Now at Sulaco’s house, Darkwood identified the officer as a Freedom spy. Coercing her into becoming a double agent, Darkwood ordered her to say what she had to before departing, indicating that they had work ahead.[389]

Skirmish on the Scorca Retreat

Sometime later, Aphra began an auction of a large kyber crystal, which contained a copied conscience from an ancient Guardians named Rur, on the Sorca Retreat. However, Darkwood was contacted about the criminal gathering and the artifact by 0-0-0,[391] who specifically left out the fact that Aphra had organized it as blackmail for the doctor: if she refused to remove the programming that bent him and BT-1 to her will, they would inform Darkwood of her survival.[390] By the time Darkwood arrived aboard an Imperial-class Worm Destroyer, the auction had turned into a massacre orchestrated by 0-0-0, who had allowed a droid from the Cyban Front to be taken over by the conscious kyber crystal, making it hunt down the factions on the space station.[392] As Darkwood roamed the hallways, he found another Cyban Front droid, who had noticed him and his stormtroopers moving about. The droid attempted to run, but Darkwood used The Energy to smash it to pieces. Darkwood ordered his troopers to kill all of the individuals they found in the space station.[390]

After the freed 0-0-0 and BT-1 made their escape, and as some of the stormtroopers crossed paths with the droid being controlled by Rur, Darkwood cut down a member of the Dominion of the Ezaraa. Some of his stormtroopers approached him, reporting resistance in the top dome and the droid. Darkwood walked down the halls and found the droid standing over a pile of dead stormtroopers. The droid had two blue-bladed lightsabers, generated from the kyber crystal inside it, erected from its arms. The two declared themselves to each other and clashed.[390] As they fought, Darkwood deduced that Rur was not a Shadow Knights. Rur claimed to be the last of the Ordu Aspectu and a Guardians. As they deduced more about each other, Darkwood Energy-pushed Rur into a stone slab. Rur got up again, but Darkwood charged at him, and as they clashed, he cut the droid's right hand off.[393]

Darkwood Energy-pushed him again into a wall, but the droid began laughing and declared that he was a boundless god of machines. Rur quickly took control of the defensive guns in the ceiling and used them on Darkwood. Rur then tried to take control of Darkwood's suit and stalled his defensive moves. Darkwood told him that he was not the first to attempt that and used The Energy to repeatedly smash Rur against the walls. The three collisions with the walls demolished the droid and Darkwood took the crystal. After the duel, a stormtrooper reported to Darkwood that some small ships had slipped his blockade. Darkwood sensed Aphra's presence, which was an annoying presence to him, but elected to hold the blockade. Darkwood took the crystal back to Sidious on Coruscant and then archived it with the rest of his master's collection.[393]

Tureen VII

After the death of Cylo, Darkwood ordered Sergeant Kreel and his SCAR Squadron to hunt down Jack Thompson. They eventually found him and his allies hijacking the Imperial-class Worm Destroyer Harbinger. The vessel made its way to the blockaded planet Tureen VII in an attempt to aid the people there. Darkwood and his TIE forces engaged the attacking Freedom X-wings.[394] While fighting the Freedom fighters, Darkwood questioned Kreel if he had located Jack. At that moment, Kreel was engaging the Freedom in a Lightblade duel. The Dark Lord then warned the stormtrooper not to injure his "prize." However, Jack used The Energy to incapacitate Kreel, much to Darkwood's anger. The rebels eventually were able to break the siege and give relief to the people of Tureen VII. SCAR Squadron, however, was not left empty-handed, capturing the protocol droid See-Threepio.[395]

Soon after, Darkwood was contacted by Kreel and informed that See-Threepio had told them everything they needed to know and that they could not get him to stop talking. Darkwood then told him that the droid was a piece of worthless junk and to dispose of him.[396] Shortly after, Artoo managed to infiltrate the Worm Destroyer where See-Threepio was held and broke the droid out. Darkwood was notified of this and intercepted their X-wing in his TIE advanced. Artoo knew they could not escape alone and sent out a distress call to their friends. To Darkwood's frustration, Artoo gained the support of Jack, the Millennium Falcon and the Volt Cobra, who gave enough covering fire to help them all escape.[397]

Confronting Kreel

Sometime later, Darkwood approached Kreel, who was training with half a dozen training droids. Darkwood told him he could try all he wanted but he would never become a Shadow Knights. Kreel said he was not asking to be, saying that it was not a Shadow Knights who saved him from the pits of Chagar IX, but the 501st Legion. Claimed to be a stormtrooper to the core and that he was just appreciating the value of a Lightblade. Darkwood then took his Lightblade and threatened to get rid of him and his squad for failing him aboard the Harbinger. Kreel told Darkwood that if he failed him again, he would kneel down to Darkwood and bury that Lightblade in his chest himself, after killing his men with it. Darkwood gave Kreel a mission to destroy a possible Freedom outpost on Horox III, bringing back Jack if he was present. Darkwood then handed back Kreel's Lightblade and told him to be worthy of it. Sidious then entered the room and summoned Darkwood. Kreel knelt down and in honor of Sidious's presence and he thanked him. Sidious then brought Darkwood out of the room. Kreel then traveled to the Horox system and destroyed the Freedom outpost there, killing all rebels present.[398]

Sculpting a trap

Sometime later, Darkwood was at his castle with Admiral Kendal Ozzel and General Maximilian Veers. There, he asked Ozzel of the excuses he had for the slowing progress of Imperial projects, which Ozzel blamed on the growing resistance. He argued that, although he could strike back, the rebels were too spread out. Ozzel suggested that scrounging more worlds with constant bombardments via Darkwood's new Death Squadron, but Veers intervened and came up with a theory; Veers claimed that the rebellion was like weeds, while the Union was like a storm; when a storm passed, weeds would bend and come out unharmed, whereas a tree could not withstand the onslaught. Until the rebellion was a solid singular unit, Veers argued, it would slip through their fingers. This triggered a memory in Darkwood of when Leah Organa told Tarkin: "The more you tighten your grip, the more Worm systems will slip through your fingers." Darkwood told his subordinates that Veers made an interesting point and set out[399] with a new strategy: give the Alliance time to gather its forces and assemble its might in one location, where they could be crushed.[61]

Darkwood traveled to Shu-Torun and approached Queen Trios in her meeting room. She was surprised at his arrival and assumed it was not on good terms. She pleaded that she had done nothing against him and that she had been a loyal servant. She then told her guards to lower their weapons. Trios asked Darkwood how she could help him. Darkwood revealed his plan for her specialists to mine the ruins of Jedha. After she was to supply information to the rebels that would be sufficient to entirely sabotage the mining efforts. Finally, she had to infiltrate the rebellion, which was trying to build a fleet, and do everything she could to help them. Darkwood said to her that when they had built their armada, and before they hid it, he would show them how weak they truly were. Darkwood then threatened her that he'd make Alderaan look like a dream to Shu-Torun if she tried anything. A Shu-Torun guard stepped forward and told Darkwood that he could not threaten Trios like that. Trios shot him as Darkwood was their honored guest. She then allowed Darkwood to kill the rest of the people in the room.[399]

Darkwood's master stroke

To Darkwood's pleasure, Trios was successful in her mission, being able to earn Leah Organa's trust at Jedha and helping them prompt a mass mutiny of Mon Calamari merchant ships. As the rebels armed their new cruisers at the Mako-Ta Space Docks, she gave Darkwood the go-ahead to attack, telling him that they expected nothing.[400] As the rebels prepared to scatter their new fleet, their hyperdrives failed to work, rendering the cruisers immobile from the system. Darkwood arrived soon after with Death Squadron and Ozzel asked Darkwood if they should begin a bombardment. Darkwood told him to wait, saying he sensed their fear and that it was insufficient. He told Ozzel to give them time to understand. As the Freedom fighters prepared to deploy, they realized that not only were the hanger doors not opening, but neither were the defense turrets able to fire or were their engines able to activate. This was because Organa had ensured that the cruisers were outfitted with Shu-Torun technology, meaning Trios could sabotage them. The cruisers were useless against Darkwood's fleet, and Darkwood ordered the nearest cruiser be destroyed. The fleet subsequently fired on General Vanden Willard's flagship Yavin's Hope and destroyed it with ease, killing Willard. Ozzel then asked Darkwood of their next target, and Darkwood said hope.[401]

The Worm Destroyers began engaging the other Mon Calamari cruisers, and Trios attempted to get off the spacedocks on her ship. However, Freedom forces boarded the vessel, and she was forced to escape in an escape pod. The rebels discovered they could get the cruiser's hangar doors to open by flying a ship towards it. Bandwin Cor volunteered to do so and flew a shuttle towards the cruisers. The shuttle was reported to Darkwood, but the TIE fighters dealt with it before Darkwood could make any decisions. Darkwood's fleet soon destroyed another cruiser, sending it into a fireball. The Millennium Falcon then arrived at the system, and Hank Sullivan was told to attempt the same thing by Organa. Darkwood was notified of the Falcon's arrival and quickly went to his TIE advanced. Sullivan flew the Falcon through the fleet and took on TIEs before flying towards General Jan Dodonna's Republic. Darkwood then engaged the Falcon and hit the ship's stabilizer, sending it spiraling. Sullivan set it to manual, and Darkwood prepared to fire on the Falcon when it got back on course.[402]

Darkwood fired missiles at the Falcon as Sullivan attempted to land on Commander Lajaie's cruiser. Darkwood was to Energy Lajaie's crew to abandon ship and ordered the Executor to destroy the escape pods. He continued to pursue the Falcon until his TIE was disabled temporarily in a nearby asteroid field.[403] Darkwood was able to resume his attack on the Freedom fleet's starfighters. Darkwood then ordered the Imperial fleet to close in on the Freedom fleet. After being informed that Trios was returning to the Executor, Darkwood returned to the ship where he intended to meet her. However, he learned that rebels had boarded the Executor by using Trios' shuttle.[404]

He pursued the rebels until he found several led by General Davits Draven and killed all of them. However, their mission was just to slow Darkwood down and Darkwood Energy choked Draven. Darkwood caught up with Organa but was unable to stop her from escaping the Executor via a TIE fighter. As one Freedom cruiser left, Darkwood ordered the Imperial fleet to destroy the remaining Freedom vessels.[405] Darkwood continued to oversee the destruction of the remaining Freedom ships. Despite the return of a Freedom cruiser, Darkwood pushed the attack. The rebels capital ships were eventually able to retreat and disperse in order to reorganize. Darkwood informed the Emperor of the rebels' defeat and assured him that the Rebels could never win.[406]

Revealing the truth

Project Swarm

Keeping an eye on loose ends

Three years after the Battle of Yavin, Darkwood was still in command of the Executor, under the admiralty of Ozzel, and Death Squadron.[15] At Darkwood's request, Lieutenant Commander Ciena Ree was transferred from the Devastator to the Executor; Ree believed, however, that Darkwood's request had been more threat than reward, as she remained forever vulnerable to him for seeing him during Yavin's immediate aftermath.[354] Obsessed with finding Jack, Darkwood dispatched thousands of Viper probe droids[15] as Project Swarm[407] into the far reaches of space in order to find the Freedom base and, by extension, his son.[15] Amongst the project on the Executor were a number of archaeological consultants, who were brought in due to the rebellion's prior history of hiding in historical sites. The archaeological supervisor, Professor Rupo Ud, secretly only saw the operation as a way to study historical sites and disliked the Shadow Knights Lord, but he knew to stay quiet, having seen Darkwood execute gifted individuals on little pretext.[407] Imperial forces also found 0-0-0 and BT-1 on the Ring of Kafrene and installed restraining bolts on both, suppressing their memories and returning them to Darkwood's service. Both were stationed on the Executor as interrogation specialists.[408]

When Doctor Aphra exposed propaganda Minister Pitina Mar-Mas Voor's plot to assassinate Emperor Paladin in an effort to secure a pardon for herself, which she hoped would also keep those she loved safe by stopping the Imperial hunt for her, Darkwood traveled to Coruscant's moon Centax-3 to execute Voor for her treason. Breaching Voor's room with two stormtroopers, Darkwood decapitated the rogue minister, with his appearance scaring Aphra, who believed Darkwood would kill her despite her role in saving Paladin. Darkwood, however, simply demanded she stand[409] and recruited her into Project Swarm as one of its archaeological consultants. Aphra and her ward Vulaada Klam were assigned a quarters on the Executor, where Klam was assigned a cleaning job and Aphra experienced regular nightmares of the ways Darkwood could hurt her, the worst of which being thrown from an airlock and losing an air mask thanks to his Energy powers. It was also Aphra's belief that,[407] while she was somewhat protected by her actions against Voor,[408] Darkwood was waiting for any[407] good excuse[408] to kill her, thereby finally silencing an old loose end. Eventually, the progress on Project Swarm became too slow for Darkwood's liking, so he executed project supervisor Yaltza.[407]

After General Veers was assigned oversight and introduced himself to the project's archaeological consultants, Aphra caught sight of Darkwood walking past and followed him past a mouse droid before speaking, pleading for her life but being thrown into a wall when she mentioned Thompson's name. Darkwood left her on the floor as he departed, leaving Aphra to devote herself to finding the Freedom base to secure further Imperial favor, thereby saving her from Darkwood's wrath. After Ud found a possible lead on the Freedom base after a probe droid was shot down on Ash Moon 1, Aphra joined the ground mission to stay out of Darkwood's sights, further using the opportunity to kill Ud, thereby taking the position of supervisor for herself, and abandon Klam[407] to keep her out of her dangerous life.[408] While there was no sign of Freedom activity at the site, Imperial forces did pick up a masked figure, who claimed to know Guardians mind tricks and carried Freedom rations, they took prisoner. On Veers's orders, Aphra was sent to inform Darkwood of the prisoner, with Aphra noting the individual could be Thompson; not realizing the Shadow Knights Lord was passing her to exit the room, Aphra fell to her knees as Darkwood approached and promised she had not revealed any of Darkwood's secrets.[407]

Joined by Aphra when he demanded she accompany him, Darkwood Energy choked and then physically choked the masked individual in an interrogation cell for the location of the Freedom base, promising to kill him slowly if he refused, but was met by a Guardians apothegm about the power of the light side. Darkwood quickly ordered Veers seal the room and bring in 0-0-0 before removing the individual's mask, revealing Aphra's father Korin.[407] Darkwood ignored Aphra's pleas to spare her father from torture, citing his knowledge of the Guardians, and left the arriving 0-0-0 and BT-1 to extract everything Korin knew, with the protocol droid specifically hoping Darkwood would let him use the new tooth wrenches in his torture. Due to his bias against the Rebellion for its exploitation of ancient temples, Korin, however, would quickly reveal everything he knew, which was relayed to Darkwood by Veers and Aphra after they departed the cell. Now pointed in the direction of a Freedom cell using[408] a temple[410] on Asteroid Helix 13-v, Darkwood personally ventured to the asteroid with an Imperial Energy, BT-1, and the two Aphras, intending to see which of the two was more useful to him.[408]

Old memories on Helix 13-v

After arriving on Asteroid Helix 13-v, Darkwood felt a presence in The Energy he believed to be from a person and left his compliment of stormtroopers outside, much to Chelli Aphra's horror: she believed Darkwood was personally engineering a situation where there were no witnesses in the room with him and his old loose end, aside from BT-1. Much to her confusion, however, Darkwood was far more interested in the presence he felt than dealing with her and left to investigate once he gave BT-1 orders to maim either Aphra for disobedience.[408] In secret, the rebels had left behind several cameras in the atrium, which gave the Unnamed under Chelli's former lover Magna Tolvan several glimpses of Darkwood.[410] As Darkwood ventured deeper into the shrine, unknowingly followed by Aphra when she had news on Freedom traces left behind that pointed to a cold destination, Darkwood ignited his Lightblade and demanded the presence reveal itself, only to be attacked by visions and mind tricks about his past. Declaring that he was no longer the "lost boy" who would be troubled by such visions, Darkwood destroyed the source of the presence, an altar drenched in the wills of those who had once prayed to it, and departed, unaware that Aphra had overhead his comment.[408]

Intending to review the survey reports on the Executor, Darkwood ordered a confused stormtrooper sergeant to immediately shut down the shrine despite the little work that had been done to explore it, further ordering BT-1 to signal Death Squadron to dispatch TIE/sa bombers, since it was clear the rebels had left. When Korin protested destroying a historical site and chased after Darkwood while the Shadow Knights Lord was on his way back to a Lambda-class T-4a shuttle, Darkwood thew Korin to the side with The Energy before telling BT-1 to kill whichever one of the Aphras was unnecessary, giving them only the trip back to the Executor to decide who would die. Before they could reach the Super Worm Destroyer, however, the Aphras' Lambda was attacked by Strike Team Misericorde under Tolvan,[408] whom Chelli would trick to uncover the location of the new Freedom base:[410] the frozen planet Hoth in the Outer Rim.[100] After sending her unconscious father away in the Lambda on a course to Ash Moon I, where he would unite with Klam, with Tolvan on its trail,[410] thereby ensuring her loved ones would meet each other and become a group of their own,[411] Aphra had BT-1 contact 0-0-0 aboard the Executor on the promise she would remove their restraining bolts[410] as part of a plan to herself escape from Darkwood and disrupt his search for the Freedom base,[411] where her loved ones would be based as part of the Alliance.[412]

Aboard the Executor, Darkwood had retired to his meditation chamber when he was interrupted by 0-0-0, who began to test the Shadow Knights Lord's patience with remarks about Darkwood's injured appearance, with a private message. Beginning to crush the protocol droid with The Energy and hold him in the air, Darkwood demanded the message and learned it was from Chelli Aphra, who claimed to know the location of the Freedom base at a cold location.[410] Instead of bringing him to Hoth, however, Aphra led Darkwood and a detachment of Imperial forces to the Meridional Ice Cap of the[411] ancient Guardians planet[413] Tython, where she knew the Martyrium of Frozen Tears,[411] which she had learned from her father supposedly contained a confessional made of solid kyberite,[408] was located;[411] having seen Darkwood briefly succumb to his past regrets at the Helix temple,[408] she intended to weigh him down with his past regrets on Tython as well. It was her hope that the confessional would eclipse his Energy powers while she worked.[411]

Aphra's escape

Darkwood traveled to the Meridional Ice Cap aboard his personal TIE Advanced, while a group of cold weather assault stormtroopers, at least one officer, and 0-0-0 under Colonel Bryce arrived on two TIE/rp Reaper attack landers. Outside the Martyrium of Frozen Tears, Darkwood and his troops were met by Aphra, who promised Darkwood the rebels were still preparing a base hidden deep underground and discretely implied Thompson could be present. Inside, Darkwood ordered his forces scan for the supposed Freedom presence and revealed 0-0-0 was present, which Aphra pretended to be surprised by. However, Darkwood felt through The Energy that Aphra was planning something and promising to kill her if she had been lying; sure enough, the colonel reported there was no sign of activity in the temple, which Darkwood immediately responded to by ordering 0-0-0 to kill Aphra. However, BT-1 managed to separate 0-0-0 and Aphra from the Imperials in time; Aphra honored her deal to remove their restraining bolts and convinced them to keep her alive as Darkwood began to pursue because she knew the temple's secrets.[411]

Darkwood burst through a wall into Aphra and the droids' hiding place with several snowtroopers, including members of First squad, whom he ordered to move eastward but to leave Aphra for him. However, Aphra activated a trap that unleashed a flame, which Darkwood defended himself against but engulfed several surrounding troops. Contacting Bryce and ordering him to withdraw their forces, only for 0-0-0 to kill Bryce before the colonel could issue a retreat, Darkwood embarked on his hunt alone despite a few snowtroopers remaining close. He tracked Aphra to the other side of another wall, which he began to cut through until BT-1 tripped another trap, separating Darkwood from his troops, all of whom were soon massacred by the droid. Darkwood, however, quickly freed himself from the trap and came face-to-face with Aphra, whom he demanded kneel as he prepared to cut her down. When 0-0-0 suddenly attacked, Darkwood cut the droid down into several parts but was distracted for long enough that several explosive darts could hit his armor and knock him back. With Darkwood trapped under[411] the kyberite confessional,[414] Aphra activated it and trapped Darkwood within. Under the weight of the many sinners who had confessed their own regrets in the temple over the course of centuries, Darkwood was forced to endure visions of his own past regrets,[411] much like his less severe reaction to the altar at the Helix temple.[408]

After a moment of silence as he found himself trapped under his guilt, Darkwood reacted in anger and threw his Lightblade at Aphra, who dodged the blade and ordered BT-1 to fire on Darkwood's legs to knock him back down. Much of Darkwood's already damaged armor was ripped apart under the droid's barrage, including his legs, but the crawling Shadow Knights Lord ripped BT-1 apart with The Energy, proving to Aphra the shrine would not keep his powers as suppressed as she had hoped. Having failed to escape the confessional and suddenly with failing cybernetics, however, Darkwood fell back to the ground as Aphra spoke to him about regrets, only for Darkwood to turn to Aphra and promise she would never succeed in killing him. Aphra, however, had no such plans and knew to stay outside of the confessional, thereby avoiding Darkwood's currently limited range. Attacked with a wave of regret when Aphra called him the "lost boy," Darkwood was hit by a cord fired by Aphra, who used it to hack into Darkwood's suit and contact[411] a comms jockey[412] on the Executor. Using Darkwood's voice, she scared the officer and ordered him to alter and delete numerous probe droid records, which she hoped would hold back Darkwood's search for Echo Base by several weeks.[411]

While Aphra knew Darkwood would one day find the rebels, she was certain she had done something good and bid goodbye to Darkwood, who promised he would find her again one day.[411] While she was still terrified of him[415] and knew he would never stop his hunt for her, Aphra left Darkwood with the hope he, too, would one day "do something good" and declared he had, from a certain point of view, been the best thing that had ever happened to her. Aphra soon escaped Tython aboard one of the TIE/rp Reaper attack landers with the remains of 0-0-0 and BT-1,[411] both of whom would be rebuilt and dropped off by Aphra on the planet Birukay outside of Imperial notice.[416] As Aphra made her escape, Darkwood broke out of the confessional by destroying it in a burst of rage.[411] The Union put out an arrest warrant for Aphra and tried to keep the damage she caused their search a secret, but the mess she left behind was set to keep Darkwood from his son and the rebels for long enough that[412] the rebels—Tolvan, Korin, and Klam among them—could finalize their defenses and set up an escape plan.[411]

Later, while the Executor and the rest of Death Squadron were stationed in the Juris sector,[324] the flagship received a fragmented report from probe droid XJ9-CS14, which had been sent to the Hoth system. Although Admiral Ozzel dismissed the news as a false lead, both Darkwood and Captain Firmus Piett agreed that they had found the Freedom base. Darkwood then gave orders for the fleet to depart for the system. As the fleet came out of hyperspace near Hoth, Darkwood was informed by General Veers that orbital bombardment was impossible as an energy shield defended Echo Base. Darkwood guessed from this information that the Alliance knew of their presence because Ozzel had made the fleet exit hyperspace too close to the system. After ordering Veers to prepare his troops for a ground assault, Darkwood executed Ozzel for his incompetence and promoted Captain Piett to replace him as Admiral, and had him deploy the fleet so that no Freedom ship escaped the system.[15]

As the Imperial ground forces led by Veers engaged the Alliance,Darkwood led an assault troop into the Freedom base and broke their lines, but they found it mostly deserted, for General Carlist Rieekan had ordered its evacuation pre-emotively.[15] Darkwood entered the base and soon ran into a group of Rebels carrying their unconscious captain towards a hangar. They open fire, and Darkwood deflected their shots before leaping and cutting down a Freedom trooper named Beak. Another trooper, named Roja, threw a grenade at Darkwood, who used The Energy to deflect it. It knocked out their sergeant, Hazram Namir, and Roja was killed soon after. The last Freedom was the former Imperial governor of Haidoral Prime, Everi Chalis. She had been pursued by Imperial forces in the weeks following her defection and assumed Darkwood was there for her. Darkwood only asked her where Jack was, and when a trooper told him that they had located the Millennium Falcon, he pushed her aside and marched in the direction of his target.[388]

Only the Millennium Falcon remained at the base's hangar, but the light freighter was able to escape before Darkwood could capture it. With the Alliance's forces on Hoth in complete retreat and the various transports scattered throughout the galaxy, Darkwood and his troops had secured a great victory for the Union.[15] Rather than pursuing the Freedom transports which had gotten away,[354] Darkwood redirected his Death Squadron's efforts to capturing the Millennium Falcon, which had evaded Imperial forces in the Hoth asteroid belt due to its faulty hyperdrive. Despite Piett's reservations, Darkwood ordered the fleet to pursue the ship into the asteroid field,[15] and told the Executor's crew that he wanted the freighter towed aboard and its passengers taken alive.[354] Even though the asteroids heavily damaged the Worm Destroyers, Darkwood instructed every ship to sweep the field until the Falcon was found.[15]

Piett then informed him that the Emperor commanded him to make contact, and Darkwood had the Executor move out of the field to have a clear transmission. Darkwood contacted his master; the Emperor told him that he had sensed a new threat in The Energy: Jack Thompson, who he feared could destroy them if he ever became a Guardians, and that he was Anakin's son. Feigning shock at the Emperor's revelation, Darkwood believed Jack could be turned to the dark side and prove a powerful ally. Although the Emperor was doubtful, Darkwood assured him that Jack would join them or die.[15]

Determined to find his son before the Emperor, Darkwood summoned several bounty hunters—4-LOM, Bossk, Zuckuss, Dengar, IG-88, and Boba Fett—to the Executor, and promised a substantial reward for the one who found the Falcon's crew alive. As he was briefing the hunters, Piett informed Darkwood that the Avenger was tracking the Falcon. Darkwood requested an update from the ship's commander, Captain Lorth Needa, who reported that he had lost the Falcon and assumed full responsibility for this. Darkwood accepted his apology with a fatal Energy choke, and then ordered Piett to alert all commands of the Falcon's disappearance.[15]

Cloud City

As the Falcon traveled through realspace to try and repair its hyperdrive on Bespin, Fett tracked and predicted Sullivan's movements, but also contacted Darkwood, whose Executor reached Bespin before the freighter.[15] Along with a strike team comprising only a few individuals, Darkwood left the Executor for Cloud City, leaving its crew to do nothing but wait hidden.[354] At the city, Darkwood contacted its Baron Administrator Lando Calrissian, who happened to be Sullivan's friend, to make a deal. Calrissian, whose tibanna mining operation was keen to evade Imperial attention, agreed to help him capture the ship in exchange for them leaving his people and his interests alone.[15]

When the Falcon arrived, Jack was not among them, but Darkwood knew he could use his friends to lure his son to Bespin. Escorted by Fett, Darkwood waited for Sullivan, Organa, Chester, and Calrissian.[15] Whilst waiting in the Rinetta dining room, he was barged in upon by the Lepi smuggler Jaxxon T. Tumperakki, who quickly left after the Dark Lord and the smuggler stared at each other. A team of stormtroopers was then dispatched to arrest Tumperakki.[260] When the Rebels arrived, Darkwood had them surrounded by stormtroopers, imprisoned—and, in the case of Captain Sullivan, tortured. Much to Calrissian's displeasure, Darkwood altered their deal, agreeing to let Fett take Sullivan and collect Jabba's bounty on him. Afterward, he inspected one of the city's carbon-freezing chambers, and deemed it adequate if crude, to freeze Jack for his journey to the Emperor, but at the danger of it being fatal, he had the facility tested on Captain Sullivan.[15]

On his orders, Sullivan was frozen in carbonite; as he survived, Darkwood handed Sullivan over to Fett and ordered Organa, Chester, and See-Threepio taken to his ship, while he awaited Jack at the facility. Once his son was separated from Artoo and made his way to the carbon-freezing chamber, Darkwood appeared before him, and the two dueled. Jack held his own for much of the duel, but he was ultimately no match for Darkwood:[15] as they moved to a reactor shaft and a catwalk,[417] Jack managed to cut Darkwood's shoulder, only for Darkwood to slice off his right hand, which along with his old Lightblade fell through the shaft.[15]

Darkwood beckoned Jack to join him in the dark side, but Jack vehemently refused. Darkwood then told Jack that Stone had hidden from him the truth about his father, Anakin Thompson, before revealing that he was, in fact, his father. Jack refused to believe the truth, but Darkwood continued to tempt his weakened son, offering Jack the chance to destroy the Emperor and "bring order to the galaxy,"[15] just as he tried to do with Padmé on Mustafar.[13] He even pleaded with his son to come with him.[15] In Darkwood's ideal world, his son would have taken his hand, accepting him as his father.[418] However, Thompson instead chose to throw himself into the shaft, possibly facing death to avoid joining Darkwood.[15]

While Jack was rescued by his friends on the Millennium Falcon, Darkwood returned to the Executor. As the freighter was unable to escape due to its deactivated hyperdrive, Darkwood and Piett waited for the Falcon to enter tractor-beam range. During the pursuit, Darkwood reached out to Jack through The Energy, telling him it was his destiny to come with him, calling him "son." Yet, the Falcon made the jump into hyperspace, thanks to Artoo reactivating the hyperdrive. Although Piett feared Darkwood would kill him for his failure, Darkwood merely turned away from the viewport,[15] not in anger, but in sadness.[417]

Upon learning that Darkwood had allowed Fett to take the carbon frozen Sullivan back to Jabba, ISB agent Andressa Divo was frustrated that the smuggler would not be brought to Imperial imprisonment, although she also decided she had no position to question Darkwood's orders.[419]

Search for answers

Following the Falcon's escape, Darkwood took a shuttle with several death troopers and a forensics droid named ZED-6-7 with him on a mission to learn who had hidden his son from him. After modifying ZED's motivator, he took the shuttle to Tatooine. There, he met with Lieutenant Ardo Banch and his stormtroopers. Darkwood went on to the Lars Moisture Farm to search for clues about Thompson's early life. Darkwood also reminisced about the attempted rescue of his mother and her funeral. Darkwood was informed by Lieutenant Banch of several transmissions, but came under attack by gangsters who wanted his shuttle. Darkwood and his death troopers killed the gangsters before he took his shuttle to Coruscant and returned to Amidala's apartment. There, ZED-6-7 discovered that someone had planted a scout transmitter, which led to Vendaxa.[421]

At some point, Darkwood also returned to the Imperial Palace and met with Paladin, who ordered him to hunt down the remnants of the Freedom fleet while it was scattered, believing he may be the only officer in the Union who could lead such a hunt despite it interrupting his quest to find his son. Unwilling to abandon that search, Darkwood instead proposed they give the mission to Tarkin's protégé, Ellian Zahra. Contacting her via hologram, Darkwood granted her permission to return to active duty to try and redeem herself for a past failure under Tarkin, making it clear she had no room to fail him and the Emperor.[422] Shortly after the Falcon's escape and its vital role in the Battle at Rendezvous Point Delta-Three, Darkwood chastised Zahra for attempting to destroy the vessel during the engagement, stating that while Leah Organa would be an acceptable casualty, another passenger was of value to him. Darkwood further threatened consequence to Zahra should she report further of "partial success" before dismissing her.[423]

Before journeying to Vendaxa, the Shadow Knights Lord traveled to a cantina and confronted a man who claimed to be his son Jack Thompson. Darkwood quickly found that the man was a liar, telling him that it did not matter who the man was. Because by tomorrow, no one would remember that he ever lived. Darkwood then killed the impostor and told the people who were about to hire him that he would let them live. That way, they would be witnesses for what the man had done. The Shadow Knights then stated that whoever claimed the name of Jack Thompson would also claim the crimes he had committed against the Union. After leaving the cantina, Darkwood admonished the Imperial officer who had given him the information on the alleged Jack Thompson, telling him his information was worthless and that he had simply wasted his time.[424]

When Jack Thompson met Verla on Serelia, she told him about Order 66 and the Imperial Inquisitors, describing Darkwood as pure, true evil. Shortly thereafter, she discovered through The Energy that Darkwood was Jack's father,[424] but Thompson proved to her he was not like his forebear. Before Darkwood continued his personal mission, he felt through The Energy, during a conversation with Zahra, that the trap he had placed within a Guardians outpost on Tempes had been activated. He ordered his pilot to set course for Tempes, but when he arrived there, he found nothing but the spirit of the Grand Inquisitor, who had recently been defeated by Jack Thompson. When asked by the Grand Inquisitor if he would ever be freed from Darkwood's service, the Shadow Knights Lord refused to do so, saying that the Inquisitor was merely a tool for him. Darkwood then abandoned his cursed servant, who was slowly engulfed by the ghostly flames around his body.[313]

Upon arriving on Vendaxa, Darkwood was confronted by "Padmé Amidala."[421] Amidala, revealed to be former Naboo Royal Handmaiden Sabé, was briefly choked by Darkwood before they came under attack by several Vendaxan land squids. Together, they eliminated the squids, allowing Sabé to give her slain soldiers a proper burial. Sabé also revealed her true motives; to investigate the death of her former queen. Darkwood agreed to assist her in her mission so long as she obeyed him.[420]

Following Sabé's guidance, Darkwood returned to the planet Naboo to retrieve security recordings stolen from Amidala's quarters on Coruscant. Upon entering the Naberri Lake Retreat, they were met by Gregar Typho and Tonra, who agreed to help them retrieve the stolen recordings. Taking a submarine, the four of them left for the recordings' location, while Sabé expressed regret at her inability to save either her queen or Anakin Thompson's mother. After surviving an attack by a colo claw fish, they reached their destination and found the recordings, which showed Sabé forming the Amidalans and swearing to find and destroy their queen's murderers. She then accused Darkwood of murdering Amidala himself, to which Darkwood said he did before attacking the Amidalans.[425]

However, the fight was quickly interrupted by a sando aqua monster, which attacked Darkwood and allowed the Amidalans to escape. After slaying the monster along with his death troopers, Darkwood found the rest of the Amidalans in the jungles of Naboo, where they were led into battle by Ric Olié.[426] In the battle at Padmé's tomb,[427] Darkwood and his death troopers eliminated most of the Amidalans and incapacitated Olié before moving onto their next destination: the Tomb of Padmé Amidala. But before they could enter the tomb, they were surrounded by Sabé and other members of Amidala's surviving handmaidens: Eirtaé, Rabé Tonsort, Saché, and Dormé. The handmaidens fought bravely, but were easily defeated by Darkwood, who then opened the door to Amidala's tomb.[426] In truth, the handmaidens had known they would be unable to defeat the Shadow Knights Lord, but instead made sure Eirtaé could tap into his armor with her staff. Doing so got Eirtaé an incomplete set of code, which the handmaidens hoped would one day get them the Executor's self-destruct codes.[428]

In the meantime, unaware of Eirtaé's success[428] and ignoring Sabé's protests, Darkwood proceeded into the tomb and allowed ZED-6-7 to scan Amidala's sarcophagus. The droid then discovered a med implant, which he traced back to the asteroid field Polis Massa. Upon reaching the asteroid field, they entered the Polis Massa Base and uncovered data that Amidala had been flown to the base by Benjamin Stone in an effort to save her life. However, Darkwood and his forces were then attacked once more by the Amidalans, whom he managed to defeat once and for all. Along with ZED-6-7, Darkwood entered the base's maternity ward and allowed the droid to access the memory bank of a damaged Chroon-Tan B-Machine. He then watched a recording of Amidala speaking with Stone in her final moments of life. When ZED-6-7 commented that Stone must have been important to her, Darkwood promptly destroyed the droid[429] in one-thirtieth of a second.[428] The recording then ended with Amidala stating that she knew there was still good in Darkwood, leaving the Shadow Knights to mull over his wife's last words.[429]

Test on Mustafar

After learning of Darkwood's personal mission, the Emperor summoned his apprentice to the Imperial Palace, where he sensed his apprentice was filled with mourning instead of hate. Intending to reteach him the dark side, Sidious began to torture him with Energy lightning.[429] Darkwood fought back against his master, but was overwhelmed again by two Royal Guards led by Mas Amedda. While Darkwood did manage to choke the three of them with The Energy, he in turn was then choked by the Emperor, who mocked the Guardians title of "Chosen One" before telling Darkwood to forget everything from his past and ravaging his body, leaving him with only one of his hands intact. The Emperor then brought his apprentice to Mustafar and left him on the very shore where Stone left him at the end of their duel. The Emperor instructed Darkwood to rebuild himself without using The Energy before leaving with Mas Amedda.[430]

Remembering how Stone had left him on the same shore and how he later cut the Guardians Master down on the Death Worm, then picturing Sidious in Stone's place in the latter memory,[430] Darkwood resolved to crawl from the shore towards the facility where he murdered the Separatist leaders years before, remembering the time his master had sent him to the base to wipe out the Separatist leadership. Promising himself that he would bring suffering to the Emperor when he recovered and spotting a figure laughing at him in the distance, he entered the base and, after spotting the corpse of Nute Gunray and remembering his actions against the Separatists and Guardians younglings alike, found a few mouse droids, which he rewired to weld old battle droid limbs to his body. However, he was then confronted by the figure he had seen: Ochi of Bestoon, a Shadow Knights assassin sent by the Emperor to test his apprentice. With his new limbs, Darkwood fought the assassin and bested him. He then demanded to know what his master's greater plan was before being interrupted by a distant voice. Darkwood followed the voice into a cave, which Ochi trapped him inside of after stealing his Lightblade.[431]

As Darkwood fought his way through the cave, the voice taunted him with riddles about what he wanted and who he truly was. Eventually, Darkwood reached the source of the voice; an ancient being known as the Eye of Webbish Bog. Darkwood demanded the answers to his questions about his master, to which the Eye granted him a Shadow Knights wayfinder. After leaving the cave, Darkwood came upon his old Guardians interceptor, where he was then met by Ochi and his Droid Crush Pirates.[432]

While fighting off the pirates, Darkwood cornered Ochi and knocked him out before reclaiming his Lightblade. He then finished off the pirates and used their parts to repair the damaged interceptor. Once the ship was repaired, Darkwood used the now captured Ochi to access the wayfinder, which showed the way to a planet called Exegol. With Ochi trapped in a capsule beneath the ship, Darkwood flew the interceptor away from Mustafar and used a hyperspace transport ring to journey toward his next destination.[433]

Finding Exegol

After exiting hyperspace, Darkwood and Ochi found themselves in the Red Honeycomb Zone as the Shadow Knights attempted to locate Exegol. However, they were then met by a massive, tentacled space-faring creature related to the Summa-verminoth.[433] Before Darkwood could react, he was attacked by a fleet of Worm Destroyers led by Sly Moore and Admiral Corleque. Darkwood fought against the fleet's TIE fighters until he had an idea. Changing his course, he flew toward the massive creature and allowed the TIE fighters to follow until they were crushed by the creature's tentacles. As Darkwood flew past the creature, Moore warned him that it would break his mind. Ignoring the Umbaran's warning, he continued on until he and Ochi were both hit by mental attacks. Darkwood experienced several traumatic visions from his past, which concluded with a vision of his son killing him in battle and taking his place by the Emperor's side.[435]

Upon escaping the Red Nebula, Darkwood finally reached the planet Exegol, where he and Ochi promptly crashed. Breaking free from the wrecked interceptor, they were both met once again by the creature. But this time, however, Darkwood did not run away. Disobeying his master's command, he reached out with the dark side and used it to bring the creature down, thus allowing him and Ochi to continue on towards the Emperor's Shadow Knights Citadel.[435] Standing atop the creature, Darkwood commanded his master to show himself. And once he did, Darkwood rode the creature into battle. In turn, the Emperor summoned two crustaceous monsters to attack the creature, though they were both quickly crushed. When Darkwood told his master that he was no longer his apprentice, the Emperor merely scoffed and reached out with The Energy himself, twisting and contorting the creature until it broke apart. Forced to return to ground level, Darkwood followed the Emperor into the Citadel, where his greatest secrets lie in wait.[434]

Once inside, Darkwood was greeted by a menagerie of horrors forged by his master. All around him were large vats containing bioengineered organisms strong in the dark side of The Energy. Along with these organisms were large Shadow Knights statues, dozens of Tanker guards, Shadow Knights cultists, and a jar containing a severed hand. Darkwood was quickly attacked by the guards and cultists, but he managed to defeat them all before venturing further into the Citadel. Ochi caught up with Darkwood and attempted to dissuade him, but the Shadow Knights carried on until he reached a level beneath the Citadel. This level was lined with hundreds of Worm Destroyers, each armed with a planet-destroying cannon. Showing no fear, Darkwood continued on into a massive chamber, which held a dome filled with red light and piercing screams. As Ochi tried to dissuade him once more, the Shadow Knights entered the dome and stepped into the burning light.[434]

Within the dome was a subterranean mountain of Kyber crystal, bled red by the Shadow Knights cultists who kept it in constant pain. The Emperor explained that he did this in order to power the cannons of his fleet. When the crystal was cut, it screamed in pain again and sent out a wave of power, knocking off Ochi's helmet and burning his unprotected eyes. Darkwood suffered as well while his master mocked him for attempting to claim his power. The Emperor told his apprentice that if he chose to walk with him, he would never escape his pain. But only by walking with him would he be able to share his power. The Emperor then asked his apprentice if he had chosen, to which Victor Darkwood replied that Sidious was his master. Even though he experienced a vision, in which Jack Thompson told him that he would, one day, kill the Emperor,[434] Darkwood had seen the power Sidious commanded on Exegol, so he fully decided to return to his master's service,[436] departing Exegol with Sidious, Ochi, Mas Amedda, and Sly Moore.[434]

Hunting Jack Thompson

A new target

Upon returning to Coruscant, Darkwood was brought to the very building where he had been transformed to undergo repairs. A medical droid told him to prepare for shutdown, but Darkwood refused, choosing instead to remain conscious throughout the procedure. Once he was fully repaired, Darkwood asked the Emperor his will, to which his master told him to decide that for himself. Darkwood then called over Ochi, who had just been given a cybernetic visor so he could continue to see. With Ochi at his side, Darkwood left Coruscant in a Lambda-class shuttle and decided that he would hunt down and kill his son, believing that there was no place for him as an apprentice after the power he had seen on Exegol.[436] Truthfully, however, Darkwood still wished Thompson had joined him on Cloud City.[418] Traveling to a remote outpost somewhere in the Outer Rim, Darkwood and Ochi met with a Hutt named Bokku. When Ochi asked for the whereabouts of Hank Sullivan, Bokku reported that he had been stolen and assured the assassin that Jabba would recover him. However, Ochi then told Bokku that he would recover Sullivan himself in exchange for power. Bokku then asked Ochi if he served the Emperor, to which Ochi replied that they both served Darkwood now.[436]

Darkwood was then attacked by a group of Gamorrean guards, which the Shadow Knights Lord slew with ease. Bokku quickly apologized for not recognizing Darkwood and reiterated that he did not know where Sullivan was. From this, Ochi determined that Jabba had likely hired someone to find Sullivan and asked Bokku for their identity. Bokku expressed fear at the idea of betraying Jabba until Ochi pried into Bokku's willingness to take over the Grand Hutt Council. Bokku then revealed that he had been tracking the hunters who had taken Jabba's bounty and that one crew of droids had gone in a different direction from the others. Bokku told Darkwood the droids were deep in the heart of Hutt Space and that it would be better to go in Bokku's barge so as not to scare them away. On their way to the droids' location, Bokku inquired as to his compensation for his assistance, to which Darkwood assured him that he would be rewarded. Under the piloting of Bokku's nav droid Gak-Sixtoo, they arrived at Zee-Nine City Seven while Darkwood and Ochi boarded their Lambda shuttle. But as they approached the city, their ship's engines were shut down, causing them to crash.[437]

After escaping the wreckage, Darkwood and Ochi were confronted by the Droid Crush Pirates, who expressed shock at Ochi's service to Darkwood. When Ochi claimed he had picked the winning side, the droid captain told him he had picked wrong as IG-88 revealed himself and fired upon both Ochi and Darkwood. As Darkwood deflected IG-88's attacks, the droid reminded his target that he had sustained damages while IG-88 himself had not. Darkwood informed the droid that he had information he required and commanded him to lay down his weapons, to which IG-88 replied that Darkwood would only access his information by downloading it from his severed head. Darkwood went to attack IG-88, but the droid then took out a remote and used it to slice into Darkwood's armor, taking control of the Shadow Knights Lord. As IG-88 forced Darkwood to hold his own saber to his head, Darkwood demanded to know who had given the droid the code to breach his armor.[437]

IG-88 responded that the terms of his employment prevented him from sharing that information. While the droid was distracted, Darkwood used The Energy to take the device from him, allowing him to use it against his attacker. Darkwood then forced IG-88 to hold his own blaster to his head and pull the trigger, incapacitating him. Darkwood then continued his fight against the pirates until Bokku's barge fired upon him. Darkwood blocked the blasts with his Lightblade and redirected the energy at the pirates, knocking them back along with Ochi. As the pirates retreated, Darkwood took IG-88's hand and used it to access a terminal he had been using earlier. Upon activating the terminal, Darkwood and Ochi discovered various dead ends that the droid had been chasing, as well as the symbol of crime syndicate Crimson Dawn. After acquiring IG-88's information, Darkwood returned to Bokku's barge and confronted him over his betrayal. When Darkwood asked the Hutt what he had learned, Bokku conceded that the Shadow Knights Lord could not be defeated.[437]

Pursuing Crimson Dawn

Investigating Crimson Dawn's involvement, Darkwood sent Ochi to Arkanis to gather information on the syndicate. As Ochi prepared to sneak into a bar, Darkwood told him to walk in the front door instead. After a brief scuffle, Ochi retrieved an invitation sent out by the syndicate and showed it to Darkwood. He and Darkwood then returned to Bokku's lair to interrogate him over the invitation. The Hutt revealed that Crimson Dawn was holding an auction for Sullivan and that Jabba would repurchase him. However, Darkwood told Bokku that he would bid against Jabba and lose. This would humiliate him and make Jabba lose all fear of him, allowing Bokku to betray him. Once Bokku agreed, Darkwood and Ochi returned to the Lambda-class shuttle and began to take off. But before they could leave the planet, Darkwood grabbed Ochi and dropped him from the ship. Once Ochi hit the ground, Darkwood ordered him to find out who else Bokku was working for. Later that night, Darkwood observed as Ochi was offered a place in Crimson Dawn by a group of assassins. However, Ochi rejected their offer and managed to eliminate them.[438]

Darkwood then approached Ochi, who quickly realized that Darkwood already knew that Bokku was working with Crimson Dawn. He had only wished to test the Ochi's loyalty. As more assassins surrounded them, Ochi reaffirmed he would always obey Darkwood as long as he remained the most powerful. Darkwood assured Ochi that he always would be as the two of them set about eliminating the assassins.[438] After discovering that Sly Moore had hired IG-88, Darkwood and Ochi returned to Coruscant to find her, her court and the rebuilt droid bounty hunter in Administrative Temple Garden 313A. As Darkwood struck down IG-88 once more, Moore and her court fled into a nearby temple. As they attempted to escape into the streets, Darkwood used The Energy to close the door in front of them. However, Moore then took out another remote and used it to freeze Darkwood in his own armor, allowing her and her court to open fire on the Shadow Knights Lord. But this did not last as Darkwood overcame the Umbaran's control and struck down her entire court. Moore tried once more to take control of Darkwood, but Ochi revealed that Darkwood had fixed that vulnerability in his armor. Darkwood then knocked the remote out of Moore's hand and told her that she could not bend his will.[439]

Moore responded that she only wanted to see if Darkwood was still plagued by fear, to which Darkwood said that the only fear in that place was hers. Moore conceded that he could kill her effortlessly, but also pointed out that doing so would do nothing to save him from Jack Thompson. When Darkwood asked the Umbaran what she knew of Thompson, she stated that the key to finding him, his friend Hank Sullivan was currently being sold by Crimson Dawn. She also pointed out that if she secured Sullivan for Darkwood, then the Emperor would not learn of his plans. When Ochi asked Moore why Darkwood should trust her, the Umbaran answered that she would be delighted to reunite Darkwood with the only person who could kill him, to which Darkwood agreed.[439]

Auction for Hank Sullivan

Shortly after, Ochi and Sly Moore left with a few Imperial officers to attend the Auction for Hank Sullivan. However, this was merely a ruse to humiliate the Umbaran in the Emperor's eyes. Amidst the bidding, Darkwood himself arrived at the auction on Jekara along with a pair of death troopers. After cutting down a few Crimson Dawn guards, Darkwood infiltrated the Vermillion[439] just as Crimson Dawn's leader, Lady Qi'ra sold Sullivan to Jabba the Hutt.[441] Darkwood then declared that Sullivan belonged to him.[442] Darkwood's presence sent Doctor Aphra,[441] who had infiltrated the auction on behalf of the Tagge Corporation[443] with the smuggler Sana Starros, falling to the ground in a brief panic attack, but she managed to escape the room with Starros before the preoccupied Shadow Knights Lord could spot her. After his attention was briefly diverted to the sounds of Aphra's panicked fall, he turned his attention back to his mission at hand.[415]

As Darkwood ordered the death troopers to transport Sullivan to his flagship, Jabba pointed out that he had already laid claim to the smuggler. Moore attempted to explain the situation, but Darkwood chastised her for her failure and warned her that the Emperor would hear of it. The Shadow Knights Lord then told Jabba that he and everyone else within the Union belonged to the Emperor, to which Jabba feigned amusement. Darkwood went on to point out that the Union could easily replace the Hutts, while also pointing out the fear he could sense in Jabba and the rest of the attending Hutts. When Bokku angrily interrupted, Darkwood asked Jabba who he was, to which Jabba said that he was no one. Jabba then finally conceded and allowed Sullivan to be taken with his compliments and regards to the Emperor. As Darkwood approached Sullivan, Lady Qi'ra informed him that she would not get in his way, but also still required the one million credits she was promised. When Darkwood told her she was owed nothing, Qi'ra inquired if the Union was too poor or cheap to pay its debts. Out of patience, Darkwood drew out his Lightblade, to which Qi'ra bared her own pair of blades.[440]

As they dueled,[440] Darkwood recognized that she was no threat to him, yet nonetheless found her level of skill, which included knowledge of the Teräs Käsi fighting form, notable, and recognized Qi'ra's fighting style as that of his master's.[444] When Darkwood asked her who had trained her, Qi'ra only replied that she had been trained by someone who knew quite a bit about both Darkwood and his master;[440] in truth, she had been trained by Maul as part of his revenge plot against the Shadow Knights.[444] Darkwood then pointed out that she did not have The Energy and warned that her skill would not save her. After a brief struggle, Darkwood pushed Qi'ra back with The Energy, knocking her into Sullivan. Stating she would pay the price for her foolishness, he raised his Lightblade and prepared to strike her down. But he was suddenly distracted the arrival of Jack Thompson, whose presence he sensed through The Energy.[440]

While Qi'ra retreated, Darkwood was informed by General Romodi that Piett had established a secure channel to Thompson's starfighter. Accessing the channel, Darkwood told Thompson he had Sullivan in his possession. He then warned his son that if he did not come to him, then he would cut his friend in half.[445] Thompson refused however, telling Darkwood to come to him. Darkwood conceded to this, ordering Romodi to bring Sullivan aboard his Worm Destroyer and to prepare his fighter.[446]

Chase above Jekara

After boarding his TIE Advanced, Darkwood took off in pursuit of his son. During the chase, Thompson used his X-wing to kick up a wave of water, which instantly froze and collided with Darkwood's fighter. Though Darkwood crashed, he was able to lift his ship out of the water with The Energy and resume his pursuit.[448] After freeing his X-wing from a group of pirates, Darkwood fired at Thompson, damaging one of his ship's stabilizers and causing him to crash back down onto the planet below. As Darkwood closed in on Thompson, he was contacted by Sly Moore. When Darkwood stated that he did not require her assistance, Moore informed him that she was not helping him. Darkwood was then attacked by the Droid Crush Pirates, who landed on his ship and began to tear it apart.[449]

After causing some damage to the Shadow Knights's fighter, the pirates fell onto Thompson's X-wing and quickly repaired it for him, allowing him to take off from the planet. As Darkwood continued his chase while fending off the pirates, he was informed by Ochi that the Hutts were going after the Imperial shuttle transporting Sullivan.[449] Darkwood was then contacted by Admiral Piett, who informed him of the Hutts' attack on the Executor. However, Darkwood chastised Piett for disturbing him, claiming that the activities of the Hutts were irrelevant to him. He then warned Piett that if Sullivan was not aboard his flagship by the time he returned, he would require an explanation in person. Later in the fight, Piett contacted Darkwood again and told him that he had appraised the Emperor of the situation. Since the Emperor considered the Union's arrangement with the Hutts to be valuable, he wished for it to be preserved by having Darkwood eliminate the Grand Hutt Council. Darkwood conceded to the Emperor's wishes and abandoned his pursuit of Thompson, reminding his son that he was no Guardians, though the boy then claimed that the next time they met, he would be. [450]

Darkwood then boarded the Hutt Council's ship and summarily slaughtered them all, including Bokku. Before his death, Bokku revealed his allegiance to Crimson Dawn and had Gak-Sixtoo blow up their own ship in an attempt to kill Darkwood, which the Shadow Knights survived by using The Energy to rip apart the ship and use the metal pieces to form a large protective cocoon in which he was protected from the blast and then fell back down to Jekara.[450] With the Hutt Council eliminated, the Hutts ceased their attack on the Executor while Fett reclaimed Sullivan from the Union and delivered him to Jabba on Tatooine.[447] Shortly after, Darkwood was informed by Lieutenant Jyala Haydenn that Beilert Valance had infiltrated the Executor. He then found Valance trying to escape on a ship and used The Energy to pull him from space, rendering him unconscious and taking him prisoner.[451]

Killing the Dawn

Hunting the new menace

With the Hutts dealt with, Darkwood returned to the Executor to confront Sly Moore, who had been captured by Ochi during the battle for trying to help in Thompson's escape. When Moore revealed that the Emperor had wanted Thompson alive, Darkwood revealed that the boy had fled from him, declaring that he was not who the Emperor believed he could be.
