When Moore pointed out that Thompson was still free and could still fight, the Shadow Knights Lord choked her and stated that his hope would not be enough. Darkwood was then contacted by Victor Sidious, prompting him to release the Umbaran and kneel before him. After restating his loyalty to his master, Darkwood was instructed by Sidious to hunt down and eliminate Crimson Dawn's agents within the Union. Shortly after, Darkwood was met on the Executor by Romodi, who presented him with all of the troops he would need to destroy Crimson Dawn. Darkwood then instructed Ochi to meet with the Assassin's Guild and inform them that they would be serving him now.[450]

In secret, Ochi swore himself to Qi'ra's service after he arrived at the Assassin's Guild base,[450] although the assassin would later claim he was acting as an Imperial double agent.[452] Following Ochi's departure, Darkwood had Valance repaired and waited for him to awaken. Once he did, he was shocked to find himself restored to his former appearance. When Valance asked Darkwood what he had done to him, Darkwood explained that he had had Valance fixed since he would now be serving him.[451] Valance then realized that his heart was gone, to which the Shadow Knights informed him that it had been removed and replaced with cybernetics, along with further upgrades to his body. Valance attacked Darkwood, but Darkwood cut him in half before leaving him and telling a medical droid to put him back together. Once Valance was repaired, Darkwood, alongside Haydenn, informed him that he would be assassinating Crimson Dawn's agents within the Union. For if he did not, the Union would kill those he cared about.[453]

While he meditated aboard the Executor, Darkwood was informed by Haydenn that Valance had completed his first assignment in killing the alleged Crimson Dawn mole Tarl Sokoli. Darkwood then revealed that there was no connection between Sokoli and Crimson Dawn. Sokoli had been publicly dissatisfied with the Union and Darkwood had decided that killing someone from Valance's childhood would be a good test for the bounty hunter. The Shadow Knights then told Haydenn to alert Ochi that Valance was ready to join them.[454] Darkwood also came to realize that Ochi had joined with Crimson Dawn but, deciding he could use it to his advantage in the hunt for the syndicate, kept his deduction a secret.[455]

Confronting the Knights of Ren

Darkwood learned that Zahra's campaign against the Rebellion ended in failure[444] during the Battle of Panisia.[457] Sometime after that engagement,[458] Darkwood investigated the assassination of the Emperor's Royal Guard on Coruscant,[456] which was secretly carried out in part by Ochi.[458] During the investigation however, he was informed by Vaneé that a band of thieves had infiltrated Fortress Darkwood. The Dark Lord quickly returned to Mustafar and confronted the intruders in his castle; a group of dark side marauders known as the Knights of Ren, who were secretly working with Crimson Dawn. After Darkwood killed one of them, Fyodor with a Energy choke, their leader fought him with a Lightblade while the rest of them retreated. The leader said he had taken the blade from his predecessor, from whom he also inherited his name; Ren.[456]

Darkwood expressed pity for how far the Knights of Ren had fallen before Ren leapt from a window with a device known as the Screaming Key. As Ren gloated below, Darkwood used The Energy to pull the key back up toward his hand, prompting the knights to open fire on him. Ren then pulled the key back down into his own hand before escaping with the knights on speeders. As the knights sped away, Darkwood stepped outside to study the remains of his slaughtered troops.[456]

Rooting out the Dawn

While thinking in his mediation chamber, Darkwood reflected on his promise to his son on Cloud City that they would bring order to the galaxy. Darkwood concluded that his master had only brought about chaos and decided to set out on a new quest to establish order, whether or not he had his son by his side. Resolving to destroy Crimson Dawn as the first step, Darkwood left with Ochi to assist the Union in hunting down Dawn agents on various planets, including Vincorba and Calior. While on Vincorba, they fought alongside a group of mercenaries known as the Revengers. With Darkwood's crew established, Ochi commented that they would serve well as expendable assets, to which Darkwood reminded him that he was expendable as well.[459] After finishing on Calior, Darkwood, Ochi, and the Revengers rendezvoused with Valance and a few other assassins on Laecor to continue their hunt. When Valance asked Darkwood why he was there instead of leading Dark Squadron, the Shadow Knights Lord simply told him to do as he was told. As a squad of stormtroopers attempted to move a group of civilians, the civilians refused to comply, prompting the stormtroopers to fire on them. However, Darkwood then jumped in to deflect their blaster bolts and slaughter them. When Ochi showed confusion over his master's actions, Darkwood revealed that the stormtroopers served Crimson Dawn. As the group carried on, Darkwood came upon a statue, which the locals said was of their village guardian who was killed fighting Crimson Dawn.[25]

Upon locating more of the Dawn's agents, Ochi opted to use the Revengers as bait to lure them out of their stronghold. Darkwood then used The Energy to grab Ochi and throw him toward the Revengers, using him as bait as well. As the agents surrounded them, Ochi requested that Darkwood bomb the stronghold. But Darkwood refused and instead mounted Gee-Ninety, balancing himself atop the droid as they rolled themselves to the stronghold's front entrance. Darkwood then stormed the stronghold along with Ochi, who the Shadow Knights stopped from killing all of the agents inside. When Ochi expressed more confusion, Darkwood explained that Crimson Dawn was just a symptom and that he would destroy the disease. He then discovered a layout of every Dawn agent within the Union, which Ochi and Valance noted went all the way to the top.[25] Following this, Darkwood and his followers returned to Coruscant. Valance, the assassins, and the Revengers left for the Zanchal Estate while Darkwood and Ochi left for the Imperial Palace.[460]

Upon entering the Palace, they struck down all of the Royal Guards and aides in the Emperor's throne room, except for Sly Moore. Unfazed by this, Sidious pointed out Darkwood should have kept one of them alive for questioning. When Darkwood asked him if they were loyal, Sidious said they were loyal to strength, which Darkwood showed them. However, only one of them was Crimson Dawn, meaning that Darkwood's list was flawed. When Sidious questioned if his apprentice already knew that, Darkwood hesitated before assuring his master that he would bring him order. Darkwood and Ochi then left for the Zanchal Estate to confront Baron Espis Zanchal for his loyalty to the Dawn. Upon arriving, Darkwood was thanked by a pair of hostages who the Revengers had rescued from the Baron. As he returned to his shuttle, Darkwood informed Ochi that the list was a plant, which Darkwood himself had deduced when Ochi could only confirm that half of the names on it were Crimson Dawn. Darkwood told Ochi to find out what connected every other person on the list. That way, they would find out who planted it and why.[460]

Hidden enemy

Later on the Executor, Darkwood was informed by Ochi that Romodi and his soldiers were not Crimson Dawn after all. However, Ochi warned him that Romodi did not understand the Shadow Knights and would come for Darkwood someday. Stepping out of his chamber, Darkwood asked Ochi what connected the false names on the list. Ochi revealed that they had all undertaken a mission for Romodi to execute the Amidalans on Naboo. Following this development, Darkwood and his team of assassins traveled to Tranchar to capture and interrogate Lieutenant Grapa about the mission. After fighting their way through stormtroopers and DT-series sentry droids, the assassins discovered civilians being tortured by the Imperials. Gee-Ninety broke from the mission to free the civilians, prompting Darkwood to destroy the droid as punishment. Darkwood went on to interrogate Grapa about her mission to kill the Amidalans.[455]

As she claimed to Darkwood that they were all dead, Tanka blasted her in the head for revenge. Darkwood then realized that the Imperials had known they were coming. When Darkwood asked Valance who had sent the droids, Valance confirmed they were from Romodi's command. Darkwood then remembered that Ochi had spoken with Romodi prior to their departure. When he confronted Ochi with that fact, Ochi tried to stammer out an explanation before giving up and running away while Valance fired at him. After stealing a spare ship, Ochi escaped from Tranchar and eventually arrived on Naboo to meet with Sabé. Darkwood followed the assassin in his TIE Advanced. As soon as Ochi stepped out of his ship, Darkwood revealed himself and destroyed it with his TIE. After landing in front of Ochi, Darkwood revealed he had known of his allegiance to the Dawn and gave the assassin an out by commending him for leading him to Sabé, which Ochi went along with and claimed was his plan all along. As Darkwood confronted Sabé, he took her Crimson Dawn badge from her and claimed that he was the Dawn.[455]

Recruiting Sabé

Following this, Darkwood took Ochi, Sabé, and ZED-6-7 to the carcass of the sando aqua monster he had slain. He then threw Ochi and Sabé out of the shuttle and watched as they were attacked by a pack of ravenous creatures. Ochi attempted to assure Darkwood of his loyalty, but Darkwood silenced him and asked Sabé why she believed in Crimson Dawn. As she defended herself, she said that she had merely infiltrated the syndicate. Ochi fought alongside her and claimed he had done the same for the Union. When Darkwood asked Sabé if she also intended to serve the Union, Sabé said she did not as long as the Union served chaos. Sabé went on to claim that she and Darkwood wanted the same thing: order.[452]

To this, Darkwood levitated the creatures with The Energy and told Sabé that if she killed one monster, a thousand more would replace it. He then explained that one would have to either make the monsters agree or deal with them all. With that, Darkwood threw the creatures at a group of long-necked beasts as they sprouted from the ground. Understanding Darkwood's plan, Sabé brought him to a Crimson Dawn outpost while Ochi returned to the Executor to speak with Romodi. Upon arriving at the outpost, Sabé informed the Dawners there that they served Darkwood now. To this, the Dawners attacked Darkwood and were easily cut down by the Shadow Knights Lord. Darkwood then had Ochi use Valance and the assassins as bait to draw the remaining Dawners and Romodi's forces to Oro Sankeria. As the Dawners and Imperials fought one another, they were suddenly wiped out by a pulse bomb, thus eliminating many of Darkwood's enemies. Darkwood informed Sabé that Crimson Dawn and Romodi feared him now. When he asked Sabé if she was scared, Sabé asked him why she should be scared of his true self, Anakin Thompson.[452]

Darkwood responded that Anakin Thompson was dead. But Sabé revealed that she had deduced Darkwood's identity from his obsession with order, his investigation into Padmé's death, and Padmé's final words at Polis Massa. When Darkwood drew his Lightblade and asked Sabé what she wanted, she claimed she wanted to help him achieve order. She went on to explain that when Thompson never returned to Tatooine to free his mother, she and Tonra secretly worked in her honor to free enslaved people and smuggle them from Tatooine to a colony on Gabredor III. Now, the colony was being exploited and neglected by the corrupt Governor Tauntaza, who was in league with Crimson Dawn. Sabé told Darkwood that if he helped her save the refugees, he would get the chance to destroy another Crimson Dawn cell and prove that Padmé was right about him. To this, Darkwood withdrew his Lightblade and accepted Sabé's proposal. He then warned her that she alone was responsible for where her path took her before they silently boarded the shuttle and left for Gabredor III.[461]

Familiar faces

As they approached the planet, they were met by Tauntaza's flagship. When Darkwood asked ZED-6-7 if the ship's crew had detected them, the droid said they had not yet. Darkwood then told him to take them to the planet's surface. Sabé suggested summoning the Executor, but Darkwood said they would first visit the colony to see if she was telling the truth. Once they landed at the colony, they stepped out of the ship as ZED-6-7 introduced Darkwood to the refugees and asked to see their protocol droid. Since their protocol droid had long broken down, ZED-6-7 instead spoke with their astromech droid Em-Sevenbee. The droid and two of the refugees then confirmed Sabé's story. The pair of refugees then showed off a terrestrial vehicle they had built, inspired by their earlier lives on Tatooine. When Darkwood asked the refugees for their names, they introduced themselves as Kitster and Wald; Thompson's childhood friends.[461]

When Kitster asked Darkwood if they could do anything for him, Darkwood told them they could do nothing. He then sensed that the surrounding land was disturbed, to which ZED-6-7 explained that it was currently suffering from drought. When another refugee claimed things would get better once the clouds came back, Darkwood said they would only get worse as they were attacked by a pack of clawed beasts. While Darkwood and Sabé fought off the creatures, a few of them surrounded Kitster and Wald. Darkwood then leapt in and cut them down, saving the refugees. Kitster thanked Darkwood as the Shadow Knights Lord silently walked away. Sabé then asked Darkwood if he had seen enough and told him it was time to take out Tauntaza. However, Darkwood told Sabé she was not ready, since the refugees had ignored her order to stay back and put themselves in danger. He then told her that if she wanted them to live, they would have to obey her.[461] Afterwards, Darkwood had Sabé don a set of Imperial equipment and recruited her into the Imperial Military[462] under the rank of Lieutenant Commander, granting her authority over all the forces he gathered.[463]

He then ordered an Imperial officer to open a channel to the Executor. When the officer reported that Tauntaza's ship was blocking their communications, Darkwood ordered for his shuttle to be prepared. He and his team then traveled to the governor's facility and proceeded to fight their way towards the governor. After cutting down a few repurposed battle droids, Darkwood was confronted by Tauntaza, who briefly subdued the Shadow Knights Lord with a life-draining weapon. Darkwood's team was then attacked by more battle droids and a few Crimson Dawn assassins. Once Darkwood recovered, he Energy-choked Ochi for allowing the governor to escape. Ochi explained that he was assisting Darkwood, to which Darkwood asked Ochi if he thought Tauntaza's weapon could destroy him. Sabé then told Darkwood that it might have, prompting Darkwood to release Ochi and meet with one of the weapon's designers, Doctor Aira. Aira explained that the technology had been used to raze the planet Karolia and expressed remorse over not having known what the technology would be used for. As Sabé reassured the rest of the regretful designers, Darkwood seized Ochi's speeder and left to find the governor.[462]

Catching up to Tauntaza and her escorts, Darkwood blocked a blast from one of her battle droids before reaching her at her escape ship. As the ship took off, Darkwood used The Energy to hold it and destroy its interior controls. However, the ship's powerful engines ultimately allowed the governor to make her escape. Using a pair of Imperials as a diversion, Darkwood boarded Tauntaza's flagship and fought off more battle droids and Imperials before confronting the one in charge. When Darkwood asked where Tauntaza was, the Imperial claimed there had been a misunderstanding and recommended that the Shadow Knights reconsider his "hostile stance". Darkwood then had the ship's crew open a channel to his own ship and ordered Piett to lock onto the governor's flagship. After giving Piett the order to destroy the ship unless countermanded, Darkwood asked the Imperial again for the governor's location. Upon learning the location, Darkwood returned to his shuttle and flew toward the facility where she was hiding, only to be met by a sandstorm generated by the facility.[463]

Despite the pilots' warnings, Darkwood told them to continue approaching the facility while he held the ship together with The Energy. A lightning bolt then struck the ship, causing it to crash into the forest. Once Darkwood stepped out of the wreckage, he was met by Ochi, who he asked for Sabé's location. Ochi reported that the storm had taken her and a colonist claimed that the storm would kill them all. When Darkwood asked the colonist if they had any ships, he said the only ship that had not been confiscated or destroyed was Kitster's racer. When the colonist said that only Kitster could drive it, Darkwood said they would see as he approached the racer.[463] ZED-6-7 told Darkwood that he could not trace Sabé or the other colonists, to which Darkwood told the droid he would command his assistance if he required it. Faced with numerous flashbacks to his past, Darkwood then boarded the racer and sped off into the storm. While deflecting debris with his Lightblade, Darkwood located Kitster on the ground alongside a large tank. Landing next to him, Darkwood asked Kitster where Sabé was. Kitster explained that she was trapped beneath the tank and that he had been trying to move it. Once Darkwood lifted the tank off of Sabé, the three of them were overwhelmed by the surrounding storm. Darkwood then contacted Piett and ordered him to have the Executor fire on his location.[34]

When Piett warned that would incinerate everyone and everything within range, Darkwood choked him with The Energy, prompting him to open fire. The resulting bombardment destroyed the tank and dissipated the storm. After cutting himself out from the remains of the tank, Darkwood took Sabé from Kitster and carried her into the distance.[34] Eventually, the three of them returned to the colonists and Darkwood ordered Piett to open fire on Tauntaza's facility. However, the blasts were deflected by a shield, which ZED-6-7 explained was drawing energy from the planet. Despite Ochi's doubts, Darkwood remained certain the fortress possessed a weakness and, when ZED-6-7 revealed that the facility's underside was unshielded, took Em-Sevenbee with him to the facility. While he defeated more monsters on his way there, Darkwood placed his Lightblade within the astromech's dome. Once they arrived at the facility, he had the droid fire the Lightblade upwards into the unshielded underbelly. Darkwood then used The Energy to guide the Lightblade through the facility, disabling the weapon and carving an entrance for himself. With the shield down, Darkwood entered the facility and struck down Tauntaza's soldiers.[464]

However, he was then met by Tauntaza herself, wearing a suit of powerful armor. Tauntaza used the suit's arm cannon to fire at Darkwood. As he blocked the beam with his Lightblade, Darkwood told the governor she could not contain the power she was wielding. To this, Tauntaza accused Darkwood of having no control over his own power before he destroyed her arm cannon. Tauntaza then flew away as the facility exploded, leaving Darkwood severely injured without any power left in his suit. Sabé soon found Darkwood and begrudgingly restored his suit's power, allowing them to walk back to the colony. Once they returned, they learned from ZED-6-7 that Tauntaza had been receiving her orders from the Emperor himself. When Darkwood asked Sabé why she was smiling, she said she now knew why she had saved him.[464] Returning to his fortress, Darkwood healed in his bacta tank, but broke it in anger upon remembering Sabé's words about the good still in him.[269]

Later, Darkwood boarded the Executor and was met by Valance, Haydenn, and the Revengers. Darkwood chastised them for being early, to which Valance demanded to know why they were there. Darkwood then used The Energy to fix Valance's collar, saying he expected those who served him to be held to a certain standard. Haydenn claimed that Valance had the team operating at peak efficiency. To this, Darkwood said they would get a chance to prove their loyalty and advised Valance to channel his energies in a less self-destructive manner. When Valance asked what their mission was, Piett explained that they had been selected to protect a medicine shipment to Bestine IV from Crimson Dawn. Darkwood warned the mercenaries not to disappoint him as he would be watching before they left for their mission.[465] Darkwood then met with Sabé and asked her about Tauntaza's location. When she said Ochi was searching for the governor, Darkwood asked her what she advised they do. To this, Sabé told Darkwood he should do what he always did: directly confront the problem.[269]

Return to Skako Minor

Following Sabé's counsel, Darkwood and Sabé arrived at the Imperial Palace on Coruscant aboard a Lambda-class shuttle to confront Sidious. As the two were unexpected, one of Paladin's advisors tried to stop the Dark Lord, but Darkwood only reacted by throwing the advisor against a wall before making his way into the Emperor's throne room. Although his two present Royal Guards pointed their Energy pikes at Darkwood, Sidious reacted by calmly asking Darkwood what his heart told him. Darkwood proceeded to hold Sabé in the air with The Energy as Sidious laughed and claimed to her that she had only reminded Darkwood, who was remembering moments of his past at the same time, of his weakness. Dropping her, Darkwood refused to assist Sabé as the Emperor had his Royal Guards attempt to destroy her. Once she outwitted them in combat, Sidious killed the guards in anger and, seeing her worth as an agent, allowed Sabé to continue working with Darkwood.[269]

As they returned to their shuttle, Darkwood and Sabé were informed by ZED-6-7 of their next mission. Jul Tambor, the grandson of Wat Tambor, was working to overthrow the pro-Imperial regime on Skako Minor through a nationalist uprising. According to Imperial intelligence, Tambor had also enlisted pirates to blockade the planet and stockpile technology to be used against the Union. As such, their mission was to end the Skakoan's uprising and restore Imperial control over Skako Minor.[269] In truth, Tambor had known he would have to face Darkwood as part of his crusade. Over the course of the last six years, the Skakoan had worked to collect and reassemble droids who had been killed by Darkwood, thereby allowing him to analyze their databanks to study Darkwood's moves. Amongst the droids he reassembled were B1 battle droids, B2 super battle droids, and Gee-Ninety.[428] Tambor was very confident that he would be able to kill Darkwood.[270]

Upon reaching the planet, Darkwood and Sabé discovered a crashed commercial transport from Corellia. ZED-6-7 explained that the ship had been boarded and those inside were killed by the planet's poisonous atmosphere. As they traversed the terrain, Darkwood killed a pack of cliff worms, saving a group of pirates. When he noticed a pile of corpses on the ground, he struck down the pirates, while Sabé hesitated and allowed one of them to escape. When Darkwood asked her if the pirates deserved mercy, Sabé admitted she didn't know who did anymore. Darkwood reminded her that she was his shadow now and she said they would need one of the pirates alive in order to locate Tambor. To this, Darkwood instructed Sabé to follow the surviving pirate and find Tambor while he took care of the rest.[269]

Confronting Valance

Upon learning that Valance had betrayed the Union, Darkwood boarded the Righteous Fist and had Haydenn accompany him as he approached the surface of Bestine IV in his shuttle. Once they landed, Darkwood and Haydenn confronted Valance and the rest of the Edgehawk's crew. Drawing his Lightblade, Darkwood told Valance that his desertion would not be tolerated.[466] Charging into battle, Valance told Darkwood that he regretted ever serving the Union. As they fought, Darkwood took note of Valance's power, which he regarded as proof Valance was a waste of potential. Once he was beaten into submission, Valance said that the planet's people would still be free of the Union. However, Darkwood revealed that Valance had doomed them as his forces bombed the locals, as well as his troops.[467]

Ignoring Valance's chastisation of his lack of care for his own, Darkwood prepared to strike Valance down, but Haydenn insisted on finishing the job herself to make up for her previous failures, which Darkwood allowed. Valance tried to convince Haydenn to leave the Union with him, but Haydenn blasted him in the face and sent him falling over the edge of the cliff. Before leaving the planet, Darkwood contacted Haydenn and ordered her to send in a group of Imperials to hunt Valance down in case he survived. To accomplish this task, Darkwood personally selected Inferno Squad.[467]

Unmasking the Dawn

As Sabé searched for Jul Tambor,[468] Darkwood was called to the Imperial Palace by Sidious. Upon meeting with his master, Darkwood was informed by Sidious that the Shadow Knights had an enemy, claiming that the recent attacks were part of a coordinated campaign of diversion. Sidious then called in ISB Director Sliro Barsha to report on his current investigation. Barsha reported that his division within the ISB[444]—the undercover criminal syndicate Zerek Besh[469]—had learned of a rumor that the major crime syndicates were vying to obtain the Hutts' long-standing arrangement with the Union. However, the rumor was coming from a single source: Crimson Dawn. As they walked through the Palace halls, Darkwood agreed that Crimson Dawn had orchestrated the Syndicate War but pondered how deep its influence went.[444]

Sidious went on to state that they had orchestrated the other attacks as well, saying that Crimson Dawn had targeted the Shadow Knights. He then asked Darkwood about the syndicate's leader, Qi'ra, who Darkwood fought and failed to acquire Sullivan from. Darkwood told Sidious that while Qi'ra had not been a true threat to him, she was trained in Teräs Käsi and possessed a fighting style which reminded Darkwood of his master's. From this, Sidious and Darkwood deduced that, since Qi'ra wallowed in the criminal underworld, she must have been trained by Maul. Sidious then concluded that Maul must have trained Qi'ra to use her as an instrument of revenge and went on to ponder what else he had taught her. With the Shadow Knights aware of Crimson Dawn's role in recent events, an alert was sent out the rest of the Imperial Military to track down the Vermillion, which made Qi'ra order her assets in the Union and other major factions to sow chaos across the galaxy to further distract the Shadow Knights. With the Screaming Key they had stolen from Darkwood, the Knights of Ren, meanwhile, traveled to a location known as the dark side hellscape,[444] a location unknown to Darkwood and Sidious,[456] alongside "the Archivist" on orders from Qi'ra to retrieve an artifact[444] known as the Fermata Cage.[470]

Dealing with Zerek Besh

After Director Barsha and Zerek Besh failed to bring in a single member of the Freedom Alliance, Darkwood confronted him on the ISB space station Derosha Six. There Barsha tried to appeal to Darkwood by announcing the successes of Zerek Besh, but Darkwood countered that, because of the criminal syndicate's inability to bring in any rebels, Zerek Besh and Barsha by extension were failures. Barsha continued to boast about the size of the network the ISB had created under Zerek Besh, and insulted Darkwood about his and the Imperial army's failures on Hoth. In his anger, Darkwood destroyed a glass display behind him using The Energy, and surrounded Barsha in the shards. Forced to ground, Barsha pleaded with Darkwood, saying that he was close. Darkwood simply replied that he needed to find rebels then.[469]

Shortly after his departure, thiefs Kay and Riko Vess were able to steal access codes to Director Barsha's palace vault on Canto Bight. From there, Kay Vess' crew working under Barsha's brother Jaylen Vrax were able to steal a secret codex Barsha had that detailed sensitive information about almost all ranking Imperial officers. Vrax contacted Darkwood and informed him of the database and his intentions to take control of Zerek Besh. They made a deal, in exchange for the codex and the coordinates to a Freedom base on Akiva, Vrax would be given Zerek Besh. Later, Darkwood ordered Barsha onto the bridge of the Revelator, where he condemned Barsha for letting such critical Imperial intelligence go. Darkwood then looked to Vrax and said the Union had accepted his terms. He ended the call and gave up Barsha to the mercy of Vrax and his associates.[469]

Finding the Fermata Cage

Darkwood was amongst Paladin's tools in the crusade to root out Dawn agents across the Union. During his effort to destroy the Dawn, Darkwood bisected a traitorous stormtrooper in an industrial environment.[470] At one point, Darkwood nevertheless returned to his castle on Mustafar, where he was called by the Eye of Webbish Bog. Upon entering the Eye's cave, the Eye showed Darkwood a moment from the Clone Copulation, where he, Yorin, and their clones had fought B1 battle droids, and glimpses of the future, including one of him being confronted by Amidala's surviving handmaidens. Darkwood then realized that he had been lured there as a test before being attacked by Mustafarians. After killing them, Darkwood claimed that the Eye had wasted his time with puzzles. To this, the Eye explained that the visions were warnings for the end of everything. When Darkwood asked why he should care, the Eye reminded Darkwood that he was a part of everything, but, if he refused to accept this, he could not be helped. Just as the Eye had shown him,[471] Darkwood, with his helmet slightly damaged, returned to his fortress and, after sensing a great disturbance[470] in the dark side,[472] was contacted by Sidious to discuss the disturbance.[470]

Informed by Sidious that Qi'ra claimed to possess the Fermata Cage, Darkwood was ordered to find the artifact and kill anyone who knew of it.[470] Sidious also told Darkwood about Qi'ra's central claim about the artifact: it contained an ancient Shadow Knights Lord[472] who was so powerful they had needed to be locked away.[473] In truth, no such Shadow Knights Lord existed; Qi'ra simply needed a claim that would draw both Shadow Knights to the Cage, allowing her to lock them both into it and free the galaxy from their rule.[474] Her initial effort to open the cage, however, had resulted in the Knights of Ren abandoning Crimson Dawn out anger at being hurt in the attempt[470] and out of fear of the ripple it sent out.[475] Darkwood made his way to the site of the disturbance, where he only found wreckage from the experiment. Although he was certain it was the origin of the disturbance, he deduced, due to a lack of bodies, that no Shadow Knights Lord had been released. Darkwood then returned to the Executor and reported his findings to Sidious via its holoprojection chamber.[472]

Darkwood asked Sidious if there truly was an ancient Shadow Knights within the Cage, which Sidious explained was possible: built by Victor Momin himself, the Cage could imprison people and places outside of time[472] and had never been used lightly.[257] Sidious further warned Darkwood that, because of the urge within every Shadow Knights to dominate others, the mere possibility was the greatest danger to everything they had built together. Amid their conversation, the two suddenly felt the Cage begin to open once more as the Archivist and her assistant, archaeologist Kho Phon Farrus, tried to use Ascendant-designed technology to focus nearby dark side energy into it. Although Farrus shut off their technology as the Cage began to[472] leach life energy[473] from each of them, the experiment had carried on for long enough that Sidious was able to track the disturbance to[472] the planet[244] they were on. With this, Sidious sent Darkwood to retrieve the Cage and destroy those present.[472]

The Orphans

Once the Executor arrived at[472] the planet,[244] Darkwood, intending to handle the situation on his own, ordered Piett to prepare his Lambda-class T-4a shuttle and landed at the seemingly-deserted site with two stormtroopers.[472] In the vicinity of the artifact, where he could not single out the presence of the supposed ancient Shadow Knights, Darkwood sensed a great deal of dark side power from the Fermata Cage.[473] Darkwood discovered the Archivist and Kho Phon Farrus trying to hide beneath the remains of their machine.[472] In Darkwood's presence, the Archivist found that the Shadow Knights Lord radiated a dark energy.[244] While Farrus, having been warned of Darkwood by their rival Doctor Aphra, quickly surrendered, the Archivist refused, to which Darkwood began to choke the agents with The Energy. However, he was interrupted by the arrival of Chanath Cha and her Orphans, consisting of Sear, Ladybright, and Imara Vex. When Cha asked Darkwood if he remembered her, Darkwood simply stated that he would kill her as he bared his Lightblade.[472]

Cha went on to introduce herself, explaining that Darkwood had slaughtered her family and that she had spent every day since then plotting her revenge. Darkwood quickly forgot her name once more before the Orphans attacked. After being electrocuted by Ladybright and burned by one of Cha's acid bombs, Darkwood pushed the Orphans back and walked into the ocean to recover. Once he did, he returned to the shore and saw Ladybright, Vex, Pharrus and the Archivist fleeing for the Siroto. When Darkwood mentioned Qi'ra's death to Cha and Sear, he sensed their confusion and lack of fear, indicating that Qi'ra was still alive. As he fought Cha and Sear, he chastised them for their arrogance and prepared to strike Cha down, only for Vex to snipe off one of his hands, prompting him to tip over the Siroto with The Energy and throw Vex into the forest. With Vex severely injured, Ladybright, Pharrus and the Archivist fled in the Siroto before returning to assist Cha and Sear. However, the Siroto then turned back around and left the planet, much to Cha's relief. With Sear and Vex defeated, Darkwood twisted Cha's introduction of herself by re-introducing himself and stabbed her through the chest.[244]

With Cha dead, Darkwood left the planet to find Qi'ra[244] and reunited with his master. Both traveled a dark, smoky location, where Sidious asked Darkwood if he actually felt the presence of an ancient Shadow Knights Lord in the Cage. With Darkwood admitting he only felt intense dark side power from it, he engaged Sidious in a sparring duel, the first they had engaged in in some time, at his master's request, allowing Sidious to explain how he would enforce the Rule of Two if another Shadow Knights Lord indeed existed. After Sidious pushed back Darkwood with a final strike and declared the duel over, Darkwood made one last rush at his master before stopping when his master repeated himself. Feeling they had no choice but to follow the ripple in The Energy caused by the next Cage opening, Sidious took the moment to admit Qi'ra would be missed as he and Darkwood looked across the location.[473]

Qi'ra attempted to sure up her chances by reaching out the Knights of Ren, reminding them that they had attacked Darkwood's fortress and thus would be hunted by the Shadow Knights if they did not help her destroy Darkwood and Paladin.[473] Around the same time,[475] the Spark Eternal artificial intelligence, created thousands of years prior for use against the Shadow Knights by the Ascendant cult,[476] resolved to hunt down Darkwood. The AI had taken over Doctor Aphra's body to carry out its mission, further collecting 0-0-0 and BT-1 to help in its quest. When Aphra used a virus she had installed into the two droids to regain control of her body, the Spark introduced, much to Aphra's horror, their Shadow Knights hunting mission against Darkwood.[475] By possessing Aphra, it had access to her memories of Darkwood and saw how she considered the Shadow Knights Lord a monster, but the AI believed itself ready to take on the Shadow Knights Lord and his master. Because Aphra had once outmaneuvered Darkwood, the overconfident Spark thought itself skilled enough to engage him in one-on-one combat.[477] In fact, Qi'ra contacted[473] the Spark to request its help in destroying the Shadow Knights.[257]

Destroying the Dawn

The Archivist moved the Fermata Cage and the equipment needed to finally open it to[473] an ancient Amaxine Space Station in the Outer Rim,[478] where the Cage could feed off the plant life and dark side energy left behind by[473] the ancient Drengir beings.[478] Sensing the disturbance in The Energy caused[257] by the opening of the Cage, Sidious and Darkwood, just as Sidious said they would need to in the name of enforcing the Rule of Two,[473] followed the disturbance[257] aboard the Executor[473] with a fleet of Worm Destroyers at their back.[477] The Shadow Knights Lords and their forces reached the station shortly into the opening process. Overlooking the station from the Executor's bridge, Darkwood found himself with overwhelming desire to board the station and meet the possible Shadow Knights, hoping to test himself against that Shadow Knights Lord's power no matter if he survived the encounter. Able to sense his apprentice's wish, Sidious felt the same but declared it a foolish desire and ordered the Executor to destroy the station.[473]

Before the Executor could fire, the warship's crew detected an entire enemy fleet emerging from hyperspace. While Darkwood initially assumed it was the Freedom Alliance, The Energy was[473] Qi'ra's secret Dawn Fleet,[472] which targeted the Executor's long-range weapons, thereby forcing the Executor to divert power to its shield batteries and delay the Emperor's order to destroy the station. With his master unwilling to take any chances, Darkwood boarded a Lambda-class T-4a shuttle with Sidious and[473] an escort[257] of death troopers and Emperor's Royal Guards, flying through the space battle under the escort of crimson TIE interceptors. As their troops engaged with Dawn soldiers who had set up a parameter in the hangar, Sidious and Darkwood disembarked from their shuttle into the station, which was filled with dark side energy that Sidious could tell was not all related to the Fermata Cage. Unsure of what they would find,[473] the two Shadow Knights Lords led the charge[257] with the mission to totally destroy the Dawn.[473]

Leading from the front, Darkwood and Sidious fought their way through the station. As the two Shadow Knights could deflect blaster bolts with their lightsabers, Dawn Squads Two and Three attempted to engage Sidious and Darkwood with electrified melee weapons, but both Shadow Knights effortlessly fought through the guards. Still, Darkwood toyed with one soldier until Sidious ordered him to stop and simply kill them. As they journeyed closer to the Cage, they were confronted by the Spark Eternal-possessed Aphra, who mocked the two Shadow Knights. Darkwood quickly deduced Aphra was under some kind of possession and stayed behind to deal with her while Sidious carried on. Believing she could weaken him, the overconfident Spark engaged Darkwood with the[257] cortosis alloy-based Null Blade, which would temporarily shut down lightsabers.[416] To the Spark, the battle represented a chance to finally fulfill the dream of its long-dead creator, Miril, by destroying the Shadow Knights Order, even if the battle proved too much for Aphra's body.[477]

The blades of Darkwood and the possessed Aphra clashed several times, thereby shorting out the Shadow Knights Lord's Lightblade.[257] While the Spark at that moment thought it was ready to destroy Darkwood and jumped at him,[477] Darkwood effortlessly excised the Spark from Aphra's body with a Energy push[257] as a group of new arrivals watched: Aphra's former lover Sana Starros had gathered a crew of Aphra's friends and loved ones to rescue her from the Spark.[477] Having abandoned the Dawn in fear after their run in with Darkwood,[244] Farrus was also among the group.[477] Unknown to Darkwood, who declared the Spark to be a weak enemy, he had effectively[257] ended the dream of the Ascendant cult by defeating its main weapon[477] without much effort. After a terrified Aphra awoke under Darkwood and escaped as the opening Cage sent out a ripple, Darkwood elected to return to his master's side rather than hunt down his former ally.[257] With Darkwood out of sight, the Spark attempted to possess Aphra once more, but the intervention of Farrus, Starros, and another one of Aphra's ex-lovers, Magna Tolvan, saved her.[477]

Darkwood took his place at an eager Sidious's side as the Cage finally opened. However, only a small disc without any connection to The Energy fell out: inside, there had been no secret Shadow Knights Lord after all, and Sidious believed the disc was nothing important. Unknown to both,[257] the disc was actually a droid locked away[479] by the ancient Shadow Knights.[480] Watching from afar aboard each of their starships, the Archivist and Qi'ra remotely re-activated the Cage, encasing Darkwood and Sidious in a time-lock. Qi'ra intended to recover the Cage with the two Shadow Knights trapped inside and throw their prison into a Worm. However, it was at that moment that cannon shots from beyond the space battle suddenly broke into the station and[257] destroyed the Cage:[481] while it remained physically together, the Cage's time-lock collapsed, freeing the two Shadow Knights from its grasp[257] but sending a Energy Wave out across the galaxy.[481] The cannon shots had come from the Night Buzzard, personal transport of the Knights of Ren: although Ren had watched Darkwood and Sidious fall victim to Qi'ra's plan from a distance, he believed Sidious would ultimately find his way out of the Cage and thus believed the only way to save his crew was to align themselves with the Shadow Knights.[257]

The wave sent out by the collapse of the Cage wiped out much of the Dawn Fleet, giving the Imperials a chance to wipe out the Dawn forces as the escaping Qi'ra gave a retreat order. As they collected[257] the destroyed[481] but intact Fermata Cage, Sidious and Darkwood sensed a great rending in The Energy sent out by its collapse, with Sidious admitting it made his grasp on The Energy tenuous. Sidious and Darkwood then came face-to-face with the Knights of Ren, who revealed themselves in return for mercy. Sidious, however, responded with a blast of Energy lightning to punish the Knights, promising that whatever was left of the group would be put to excellent use. Still, Sidious declared the Knights' future a small matter and ordered Darkwood to depart with him. In the aftermath of the Crimson Dawn war, the syndicate was shattered while the defeated Qi'ra fled into hiding.[257] The effects of The Energy Wave[468] varied at different times: Energy-sensitives could find themselves with a limited connection to The Energy at one time but could wield great power[482] when the Wave returned. Darkwood came to fear the great energy provided by The Energy Wave because he could not control it.[468]

Fighting the past and The Energy

Reunion with the Handmaidens

As The Energy Wave vanished for the time being,[468] Darkwood returned to the Executor and meditated, reflecting on Stone's words that only a Shadow Knights dealt in absolutes and declaring that no one in the galaxy should forget his allegiance. Afterward, Darkwood had Piett contact the recently-returned Ochi, who had returned to the Executor with Sabé, to bring Sabé into a room aboard the ship. Within, Darkwood had also summoned Sabé's recently assigned personal guards, stormtroopers KA-336 and KV-222; both troopers had broken protocol by openly speaking about her classified assignment, with Darkwood demanding the woman before him kill them both with a nearby E-11 blaster rifle as punishment. In truth, Darkwood suspected that the woman was not Sabé, but another of Amidala's surviving handmaidens, and was testing that theory. Darkwood also created a struggle when he urged the troopers to also run for the blaster. "Sabé" grabbed it first and fired at them, injuring both but leaving them alive. This confirmed Darkwood's suspicion that this was not Sabé and instead another handmaiden, Dormé. Lifted into the air via a Energy choke, Dormé claimed that Sabé wouldn't have killed them, which Darkwood declared meant Dormé did not know Sabé at all.[270]

Upon realizing that Ochi had helped her get inside as part of a scheme get rid of Sabé, whom Ochi saw as a rival, Darkwood threw Ochi into a wall and told Dormé that Sabé served him willingly. Dormé denied Darkwood's claim, saying Sabé was the best of them. To this, Darkwood brought her to a hangar and told her she should follow Sabé's lead by claiming the same power. When Ochi advised against this, Darkwood tossed him aside, saying he simply needed her to serve his purpose. Dormé stated once again that neither she nor Sabé would serve Darkwood. Darkwood then said they would see before he took her aboard a Lambda-class T-4a shuttle and traveled to[428] the planet[262] Fedalle.[428]

Shortly after, Dormé's fellow handmaidens Eirtaé, Saché, and Rabé Tonsort arrived on the planet. When Dormé insisted they go after Sabé instead, the handmaidens told her they wouldn't leave her as they engaged Darkwood in combat. Despite their coordination, the handmaidens were subdued by Darkwood, who gave them one final chance to join him. However, Saché revealed that Eirtaé had seized codes from Darkwood's suit during the fight, allowing the handmaidens to complete Dormé's sabotage of the Executor. Before they could destroy Darkwood's flagship, which would kill thousands in the name of rescuing Sabé and defeating Darkwood, Darkwood declared they were just as bloodthirsty as he needed them to be to join him. However, he was contacted by Piett, who showed him a transmission from Tambor.[428] After arriving on[270] the planet[483] Brentaal IV, Sabé had confronted Tambor while he was helping out a village of exiled Skakoans, urging him not to go up against Darkwood and declaring that the Shadow Knights Lord had only grown more dangerous since her own failed efforts on Polis Massa. She was certain that Tambor's efforts against Darkwood would fail and that the Shadow Knights Lord would slaughter[270] the hundreds of Skakoans on Brentaal IV[428] in retaliation.[270]

Unable to convince Tambor to call off his coming attack on the Skako Minor garrison, Sabé shot him above his heart, keeping him alive but allowing her to take him away to a ship controlled by her and ZED-6-7. Aboard, Tambor listened to her reasoning about the danger Darkwood posed and declared that it was time to kill the Shadow Knights Lord, capturing Sabé after his rebuilt droids reached the starship. After showing Sabé video footage of Darkwood, Tambor sent his hologram to Darkwood, in which he revealed her capture and baited him to come after him.[428] While fully aware of the Shadow Knights Lord's ruthlessness and not doubting Darkwood's willingness to slaughter his refugee camp in retaliation,[227] the Skakoan had come to believe that Sabé was Darkwood's weakness, which would finally give him an advantage over the Dark Lord. On Fedalle, realizing they would need Darkwood and his forces[428] to stage a daring rescue,[418] the handmaidens reluctantly joined Darkwood to go after Tambor, much to Ochi's annoyance. The group returned returned to the Executor aboard his Lambda shuttle[428] and eventually left for Brentaal IV.[227]

Corrupting Sabé

Upon reaching Brentaal IV via a Lambda-class shuttle with the handmaidens and ZED-6-7, Darkwood reflected on his last meeting with Amidala on Mustafar and how he wished it had gone, privately declaring to himself he would finally have his idealized outcome.[227] He also saw it as a way to have his idealized version of the duel on Bespin, privately comparing the situation to him reaching out to Jack Thompson.[418] Entering a building on-world, Darkwood and the handmaidens were met by Moore, who explained with Darkwood how each of the handmaidens could benefit from serving the Union and the Shadow Knights Lord. When none of the four agreed to his demands, he telekinetically threw all four outside through an opening created by Tambor's forces, who had tracked down the Shadow Knights Lord and his reluctant allies, and forced them to fight the B1 battle droid horde alone. To Darkwood, the battle was a chance for the handmaidens to earn their places at his side and show them their own power. Overlooking their victory, Darkwood remained silent as Moore declared it an example of their skills, which she promised would only grow under Darkwood's service.[227]

Shortly thereafter, Eirtaé discovered from one of the B1's memory banks that Tambor's command ship, in which she learned Sabé was being held, was three klicks to the south of their position. However, Tambor, ignoring Sabé's continued pleas that Darkwood would wipe out his refugee camp if he continued, sent three platoons of B1s and a Low Altitude Assault Transport/infantry to attack a civilian village, hoping it would trap his enemies into the village's canyon and let him escape with Sabé. Both Darkwood and Moore understood the attack was a trap, with Darkwood declaring it an example of the weakness that the handmaidens would need to overcome. When the handmaidens demanded that Darkwood assist them in aiding the populace, Darkwood elected to use them as a distraction,[227] effectively turning them into his pawns so he could get to and corrupt Sabé,[418] and teach them another lesson: as they fought the B1 horde, he could slip past the droids' lines, leaving the handmaidens to seize their true potential against a major enemy. Riding into battle on speeder bikes, Darkwood cut down the gunship but left the handmaidens to the droids, cutting into Tambor's ship to save Sabé and confront the revolutionary.[227]

Inside Tambor's ship, Darkwood took position in front of Sabé's cage but was met by the rebuilt Gee-Ninety, who fired upon the Dark Lord before releasing magnetic spheres. Having deduced that Darkwood would attack with brute Energy, Tambor and the droid had created the spheres specifically to immobilize him via electrocution. However, the handmaidens then arrived and used Eirtaé's own magnetic devices to Energy down the spheres, although Tambor and Gee-Ninety managed to flee by detaching the room from the rest of the ship while Darkwood recovered. The Shadow Knights Lord maintained that he had not betrayed the handmaidens, instead declaring that he had granted them a chance to claim their true power, before Energy choking Eirtaé and Dormé against the wall. Both had just released Sabé from her cage and begged her to leave Darkwood's service, but Darkwood offered her a chance at creating order instead of allowing the innocent-attacking Tambor to run free. As he made the offer[227] and the handmaidens begged Sabé to turn back, Darkwood remembered his lived and idealized versions of the meeting with Amidala on Mustafar and with his son on Bespin.[418]

Sabé almost took Darkwood's hand, but was tackled and pulled out of the room by Dormé while Eirtaé and Saché provided covering fire against Darkwood. Darkwood drew his Lightblade and pursued the handmaidens outside, only for the group to take Sabé into the air with Eirtaé's antigravity devices to an overhead Worm Commuter 2000 as Darkwood watched on. The ship was in fact being piloted by Ochi, who had made a deal with the handmaidens that they would convince Sabé to abandon Darkwood to make sure he would never see any of them again. However, Sabé believed her handmaiden sisters did not have the tools needed to put a stop to Tambor's campaign, deciding the only way to save the civilians Tambor was on the cusp of attacking was to join Darkwood's violent crusade. She also decided that, by staying with Darkwood, she could continue to bring about Amidala's declaration that good existed in Anakin Thompson. Bidding her fellow handmaidens goodbye, Sabé detached her antigrav device to fall back to Darkwood, who guided her to the ground with The Energy. While a Energy of stormtroopers had arrived via a Lambda-class T-4a shuttle and were firing upon the escaping Worm Commuter, Darkwood called their assault off, declaring that the incident was over before walking back to the shuttle with Sabé at his side.[418]

The following day, returning to the Executor and receiving an update on Tambor's gunships, Darkwood made it clear to Piett that the campaign against Tambor was under Sabé's command. Ordering her own shuttle be prepared for departure, Sabé enginneered the downfall of Tambor without even killing the Skakoan; contacting the revolutionary, she claimed that twelve Skakoan refugees, whom were actually in the same room as her, were imprisoned with Darkwood and his forces in the abandoned village of Kantiema, which Tambor quickly bombed. Seeing that the man they thought would protect them was willing to throw away their lives and then brag about his coming rule, the people of Skako Minor quickly abandoned[418] and revolted against[484] Tambor's crusade. With that, the Union granted all his refugees amnesty and began an armistice process on Skako Minor. After Tambor's droid army was wiped out, Sabé reported her success to Darkwood, who meditated and accepted her rational for not killing Tambor, as doing so would have made the Skakoan a martyr and cost hundreds of lives. Still, Darkwood felt the need to correct her by claiming they were bringing order, not mere peace.[418]

He also inquired to her about how she felt about the power at her fingers, telling her she would continue to grow stronger as she remained in his service. However, Sabé remained convinced that her fellow handmaidens, dismissed by Darkwood as weak, had only left because they did not know he was Anakin Thompson. Before leaving the room, Sabé declared it meant she was the only one present who had to lose their soul by staying under his command. Feeling a pain[418] and losing control of The Energy[485] as The Energy Wave returned,[468] Darkwood declared to himself that Sabé was wrong in thinking she was the only one who had to lose their soul in return for the future they were working towards.[418] Darkwood was unable to control The Energy[485] as his powers began to tear apart the Executor, thereby presenting another threat to the Union: himself.[468]

Facing The Energy Wave

Darkwood's fear at his out-of-control abilities could be felt by Sidious all the way away on Coruscant, where the Shadow Knights Master was delighting in the uncontrolled powers afforded to him by The Energy Wave. Through The Energy, Sidious had reached out to Darkwood to speak of the great power but, noticing the fear, urged Darkwood to accept his true self. Darkwood managed to exit[468] his chamber[418] but still failed to control The Energy, crumbling parts of the Executor around him as Sabé, who quickly understood something had gone wrong with Darkwood's Energy powers, marshalled nearby Imperial officers and stormtroopers away from the out-of-control Shadow Knights Lord. Darkwood's powers began to breach the Executor's hull in the hallway just as Sabé cleared the room. Despite the objections of one of the officers, Sabé sealed off the hallway from the rest of the ship, leaving Darkwood liable to be sucked out into the vacuum of space. She attempted to contact Darkwood that the inconvenience would only last a brief moment, but she was Energy choked by Darkwood as Sidious spoke in his mind: Sidious offered that fear paved the way for anger and hate, but it would be up to Darkwood whether he chose to turn that hatred into either power or suffering.[468]

As Sidious spoke to him, Darkwood was thrown out into the vacuum of space. Darkwood reached out towards his flagship but only managed in carving a line of destruction in the Executor's hull. However, a motionless Darkwood was soon found by Sabé aboard a Lambda-class T-4a shuttle, from which she deployed two Prowler 1000 seeker droids to collect the Shadow Knights Lord and bring him aboard. Although Sabé asked him to hold back his destructive power for a single minute longer, Darkwood was unable to and quickly began to rip apart the Lambda and crush the two droids as Sabé launched them into hyperspace, coming out of lightspeed above an uninhabited astronomical object: taking Darkwood to the astronomical object had been Sabé's intention as it would give the Shadow Knights Lord a place to practice and rediscover control over his powers. As the crippled Lambda crashed towards its surface, Darkwood was thrown from the ship.[468]

He was followed by Sabé, who donned a wing backpack and reached out. Both managed to grip the other's arm before tumbling into the sea, causing Darkwood to lose his grip on Sabé. Reflecting on Paladin's declaration of power and suffering as he fell, Darkwood decided he would continue inland on an island on his own, leaving Sabé unconscious on the shore. Darkwood left a trail of footprints and destruction in his wake, through which Sabé followed him when she awoke. Darkwood pushed the Handmaiden back when she approached and demanded he be left alone because of his out-of-control powers. When Sabé—who called Darkwood "Anakin"[468] once more[418] in the process—offered the astronomical object as a chance for Darkwood to take control of his powers and life as a whole, reasoning that he had never truly controlled his own destiny, another blast of his power ripped apart the land around her and forced her to run. Remembering moments from the past as he again reflected on Sidious's offer, Darkwood decided the test was no longer for himself, but for Sabé: Darkwood raised the confused Handmaiden into the air and gave her a choice to turn hatred into power or suffering, making Sabé experience a vision[468] before asking her to choose.[484]

Sabé ignored the question and tried to focus their efforts to regaining control of Darkwood's powers, only for Darkwood to pull her close as a way of showing that the only safe place was to stand at the center of the storm. Darkwood then showed Sabé what he claimed to be her own desires, whereas she believed they were his, in a vision where she joined the Union and brought order to the galaxy by turning suffering into power. As Sabé rejected the possible future and demanded to know what "Anakin" wanted, a vision of one of the Handmaidens—as Saché, Tonsort, Rabé Dormé and Eirtaé were all present in the vision—tried to argue Darkwood and Thompson were different, which she vehemently denied before chasing after Darkwood in the vision: within the vision, Darkwood appeared in the shadows of the forest where they stood, with Thompson's face visible when he turned back to her. Chasing after Darkwood brought Sabé into contact with visions of Amidala and Jack Thompson, which Sabé took as proof that the light did indeed still exist in Darkwood, before the Shadow Knights Lord emerged and cut down both his wife and son, claiming once more the vision was not his own. The visions of the Handmaidens appeared to defend Sabé, but Darkwood cut all four down as he declared the vision was full of lies.[484]

Darkwood demanded Sabé embrace a future with him, but she threw herself back and exited the vision. As Darkwood's powers again lost control and threw Sabé back, the Shadow Knights Lord gave Sabé a final chance to forge either suffering and power out of hate, warning her that she would die should she reject him, simply because he would not be able to control his abilities. At that moment, Darkwood believed his abilities in The Energy were more powerful than any point prior in his life.[484] Having gone through an introspection into herself[414] through the vision, specifically remembering how Darkwood had cut down his own wife and son,[484] Sabé decided she could not join Darkwood after all: she rejected him[414] and the question all together by declaring she would not give into hatred.[484] Using his remaining control of The Energy[414] but telling himself it was his lack of control that did so, Darkwood responded by throwing Sabé[484] far out into the ocean.[414]

Taking back control

Attempting to take control of his powers, Darkwood then raised their Lambda shuttle from the waters and entered it, crafting[484] an electrostaff[486] to serve as a cane and laser weapon. He also donned an energy shield on his exposed electronic left hand. Sometime later, a group of human operatives, having learned from a source that Darkwood had lost control of his abilities and damaged his own flagship, arrived at the island with the intention of killing Darkwood while he was unable to control The Energy, operating under the belief it was their best chance to destroy the Shadow Knights Lord. Darkwood was currently standing on the beach and leaning on his staff, making the captain underestimate him as a weakened opponent. Darkwood raised the wreck of the Lambda to block their ship's laser fire. As their ship circled around and fired a blast of fire, Darkwood protected himself with his energy shield and, as the ship passed over him, destroyed it with his laser staff. Darkwood approached the crashed ship and informed the leader that, so long as he had hatred, he had power.[484]

The Dark Lord and the droids

Reunion with Aphra

Desperate to regain control of his abilities, Darkwood sent Ochi to locate the only remaining person who could help him; Aphra. Locating Aphra in a cantina on Sesid, Ochi played against her in a game of Sabacc while Imperial forces arrived. Realizing this too late, Aphra attempted to escape, only to be met by Darkwood himself. Overcome with anxiety, Ochi helped Aphra to relax before she reminded Darkwood of everything she'd done for him and offered her services once more. However, they were interrupted by bounty hunters sent by the Emperor to test Darkwood, who the three of them took care of. Upon learning of Darkwood's current state from Ochi, Aphra inquired Darkwood about her next assignment. Darkwood reiterated that she had last left him for dead on Tython, to which Aphra reminded him that he was still unharmed. Allowing Aphra to return to his service, Darkwood took her along as he and Ochi followed a map provided by her, meditating in private on the way there.[414]

Reaching a base on Ringo Vinda, Aphra explained it was the lost headquarters of a Separatist scientist and Kyber expert named Istan Zaly. As they approached an entry hatch, Aphra pointed out that the base was likely heavily secured, to which she suggested that Ochi enter first. Despite Ochi's refusal, Darkwood threw him down the hatch, after which he was subjected to all of the base's security weaponry. Once he had taken the brunt of the fire, Darkwood and Aphra followed him inside without resistance. Aphra then took Darkwood and Ochi to the Zaly Shield, which Aphra explained was made of Kyberite; a mineral that channels and guides The Energy emitted by the Kyber crystals that grow in it. Aphra also said that though the Separatists were unable to use the shield effectively against the Guardians, Darkwood could use it to harness his unstable Energy abilities into concentrated attacks. Darkwood went on to test the shield on Ochi, whom he blew away with a powerful blast of Energy energy. As Aphra assured Darkwood that she was there to help him, the three of them were suddenly confronted by an army of droids, including ZED-6-7, Ought-Six, Gee-Ninety, 0-0-0 and BT-1.[414]

Darkwood fought alongside Ochi and Aphra against the rebuilt droids. After a lapse in the fighting, the droid Arex came back online and led another strike against Darkwood by taking control of the knee joint that Darkwood had once claimed from the droid. But as they did, Darkwood took control of Arex and made them attack their fellow droids. ZED-6-7 then disabled Gee-Ninety and revealed his loyalty to Darkwood. Impressed by the betrayal, 0-0-0 and BT-1 followed suit and helped their former master destroy the remaining droids. Once the fighting ended, Aphra left with her droids aboard an extra ship, which Ochi reported to Darkwood. When Ochi asked if they should hunt them down, Darkwood said he had what he needed and ordered ZED-6-7 to rebuild all of the destroyed droids and reprogram them to be under his command.[239]

Scourge of the Shadow Knights

Upon assembling his army, Darkwood returned to Mustafar with several of the droids, including ZED-6-7, Arex, and members of the Crush Pirates. Other droids from his new army were brought to the Executor for repairs.[26] In his castle, he tested himself against a training droid and requested another when he destroyed it.[479] He also tested his control over The Energy, but one of his attempts resulted in his power exploding out without control as soon as he tried.[26] However, thanks to the Zaly Shield, Darkwood was slowly but surely regaining control over his powers.[487] Unknown to all, a new threat was emerging:[26] when Darkwood and Sidious left the Amaxine Station, they left behind the disc dropped by the Fermata Cage, allowing the Spark Eternal to flee into it.[257] The merging of the Spark and the ancient droid created a new threat: the Scourge. The Scourge quickly corrupted the droids of Imperial I-class Worm Destroyer[479] Chelator,[488] which had been sent by Paladin to collect everything from the station,[479] and then spread across the galaxy by corrupting droids as the "scourge of the droids."[489]

The Scourge soon set its sights on assimilating organics[490] and controlling The Energy itself. Thus, it targeted Darkwood, whose cyborg nature left him liable to the corruption,[480] after Jack Thompson namedropped Darkwood as a cyborg who could use The Energy to a Scourge-corrupted See-Threepio. After taking a moment to sit atop his throne,[490] Darkwood removed his armor and rested within his bacta tank, where he was guarded by two Royal Guards. In secret, the Scourge, using information collected from Imperial databanks and from across its minds, infiltrated Fortress Darkwood via a mouse droid that contained a spider-droid,[488] with which the Scourge could spread itself,[487] and infected several DT-series sentry droids stationed in the castle. As Darkwood rested within his bacta tank, two DT-series units ambushed the Shadow Knights Lord by murdering the guards and shattering the bacta tank, dragging the out-of-armor Shadow Knights Lord across the floor via straps used to suspend him within the tank.[488] While his control over The Energy was still lacking,[486] Darkwood tried to summon his nearby Lightblade, which was instead caught by one of the DT units despite causing some minor damage to their back.[488]

One of the DT droids then punched Darkwood before both threw him against the wall. However, the Scourge suddenly had to refocus its conscious on the Chelator, which it had renamed 'Scourge 01 and turned into its command ship, upon realizing the vessel was under attack from the[488] warrior-priest droid[491] Ajax Sigma and his Second Revelation followers.[488]

With the droids before him distracted as the Scourge debated where to focus itself, an angered Darkwood began to levitate with The Energy and stared down his attackers, crushing both with The Energy just as the Scourge totally refocused itself on fighting off Sigma's attack. Afterward, Darkwood rested against the wall, only to snap to focus when the spider-droid jumped at him: after its victory over Sigma, the Scourge, which learned the secret to controlling organic life was turning its transmission into a heart-beat-like frequency, refocused itself on Darkwood and attempted to infect him, but Darkwood crushed the small spider-droid with The Energy, leaving him alone in the room with destroyed droids. While the Scourge had found cyborgs in the form of Magna Tolvan, Beilert Valance, and Lobot,[488] it continued to lust after Darkwood,[486] whose memories, knowledge, and sensitivity to The Energy made him especially valuable.[488]

Conflict for the Executor

Back in his armor and outside, where he was joined by his own droid followers, Darkwood continued testing his control of The Energy without holding the Kyberite Zay shield. He managed to suspend Ought-Six in the air for 2.3 seconds before losing control and destroying him, marking some progress in controlling The Energy again. Arex suggested meditating like the Guardians to increase his concentration, which Darkwood decried as weakness. ZED-6-7 then interrupted Darkwood to inform him of a call from Amedda, who relayed an order from the Emperor to secure the hijacked Executor:[26] in its second attempt[486] to get to Darkwood, the Scourge had infiltrated the Shadow Knights Lord's flagship and corrupted the members Darkwood's new droid army aboard. The corrupted droids slaughtered the Imperial crew and forced the survivors to flee to the bridge, from which the droids hoped the organics would call Darkwood once he came aboard. On Mustafar, Darkwood also revealed to Amedda he had reprogrammed the droid uprising and demonstrated his powers by blasting apart Ought-Six and another crush pirate with the Zaly Shield, promising he had never been stronger. Ordered by Amedda to reach out when they arrived at the Executor and with ZED-6-7 promising Ought-Six the destroyed units would be rebuild once again, Darkwood made his way back to his castle before leaving for the Executor in the droids' ship.[26]

As Darkwood and his droid servants flew towards the Executor, ZED-6-7 discovered that the majority of the flagship's crew had been killed before a group of TIE fighters piloted by Imperial astromech droids attacked their transport, firing upon it as a distraction before crashing into it. Refusing Ought-Six's request to retreat, Darkwood then rammed their ship into the side of the Executor, boarding the hijacked dreadnaught and getting contact from Piett, who informed him of the droid "possession." Armed with the Zay shield, Darkwood fired blasts at the corrupted battle droids and led his droid servants in battle towards a command center, where Darkwood shorted out several B1s, but also the control panel, with his staff. Without the hours needed to repair the command line to shut down all droids aboard, as Piett and the crew had mere minutes by ZED-6-7's count, Arex urged Darkwood to remember his Guardians training and repair the panels, as he had with himself on the river moon of Al'doleem. Darkwood gave ZED-6-7 the Zaly Shield and attempted to repair the machinery with The Energy, only to fail before Arex again encouraged him to remember his Guardians past. Bombarded by visions of his past, Darkwood lashed out in anger and crushed Arex after another failed attempt to repair the panels.[26]

Without the ability to shut off the droids via the command line, Darkwood decided to lead his droids in a direct attack against the possessed droids. Darkwood and his minions fought a horde of corrupted B1s and B2s[26] until Darkwood directed Gee-Ninety and a rebuilt Arex to cover ZED-6-7 and Ought-Six, who together worked on repairing the command line. Although Darkwood would find himself swarmed by the corrupted battle droids, he kept up his defense until ZED-6-7 brought him a message from Piett; amid the battle, an Imperial fleet of five Worm Destroyers had approached the Executor[487] and moved in to destroy Darkwood's flagship to contain the Scourge on the orders of Amedda,[26] who lacked faith in Darkwood and argued the ship was lost[487] due to the lack of contact from the Shadow Knights Lord or the ship's rapidly dying crew.[26] Above those reasons, however,[492] Amedda believed he had a chance to kill Darkwood once and for all[486] with the Scourge as a convenient excuse.[492] Darkwood, however, ordered Piett to stand his ground before re-engaging the attacking droids. Fortunately for the Executor's crew, the outside fleet was under the command of Admiral Corleque,[487] the very same admiral Darkwood had clashed with during his race to Exegol,[435] who delayed the order to destroy the Executor to instead rescue Piett and company, intending to fire on the Executor once his former commanding officer was clear.[487]

Corlque's rescue team, however, only succeeded in supplying the Scourge with a supply of KX-series security droids, which the Scourge used to murder the rescuers as Darkwood picked off the last of the corrupted battle droids. After wiping out the last battle droids with a blast from the Zaly Shield, Darkwood took a moment to recompose himself before requesting an update from ZED and Ought-Six, who were nearly finished with the failsafe. Suddenly, however, the Scourge used a surviving RA-7 series droid to taught Darkwood and offer him a chance at assimilation, distracting the Shadow Knights Lord while a spider-droid tried to drop on Darkwood. After ZED destroyed the spider-unit, the Scourge attacked Darkwood and his droids its KX-series droid bodies until the ZED activated the signal on Darkwood's order, shutting down all droids aboard the Executor, including themselves. Darkwood took the moment to survey the battlefield his ship had become. While Piett and his crew contacted Corleque, who expressed surprise at Darkwood's survival, the Shadow Knights Lord departed for Mustafar aboard his TIE Advanced with the heads of ZED, Ought-Six, Arex, and Gee-Ninety, intending to rebuild them once more. Just as Corleque tried to confirm Darkwood's destination for Amedda, the Scourge attacked his bridge with corrupted B2s, forcing the Executor and the fleet to destroy Corleque's flagship,[487] the Unbreakable.[387]

Who to hate

Darkwood returned to Mustafar and handed off the heads of his droid minions to another droid before leaving, much to the confusion of the droid he met upon landing, to meet with the Eye of Webbish Bog for help in securing control. Reminded that he was a Shadow Knights by the Eye and should be taking whatever he wanted by Energy, Darkwood attacked the Eye with a blast of electricity from his staff to try and regain his control, which the Eye promised would yield no results. Instead, the Eye advised Darkwood to go after Sidious by reminding him of the Tragedy of Victor Plagueis the Wise. Darkwood agreed with the plan and united with his rebuilt droid minions, minus ZED-6-7, by a Lambda-class T-4a shuttle for a journey to Paladin's Palace on Coruscant,[487] waiting to depart until ZED returned.[486] However, ZED had been overtaken by the Scourge, which in turn used him to find the lost Techno Union droid factory and corrupt a massive Octuptarra tri-droid.[487] As Darkwood and his droids tried to contact ZED, Arex raised the possibility to Darkwood that ZED had been corrupted, which was confirmed when the Scourge appeared as ZED and the Octuptarra, which was protected against Darkwood's electrical staff and Zaly Shield blasts.[486]

Unable to simply tear the Octuptarra apart with The Energy as he still lacked control, Darkwood tried to defend against the machine's stomps with the shield before being kicked into a wall. Darkwood re-engaged the Octuptarra, using the Zaly shield to block a blaster shot, as the Scourge taunted him about its plan to infect him. Arex attempted to attack the corrupted ZED but was grabbed by the throat and studied by the Scourge, which learned the Guardians teaching "hate leads to suffering" from Arex's databank. The remark reminded Darkwood of his belief that hate actually led to power, only for the Shadow Knights Lord to be kicked back again. From Darkwood's remark, however, Arex decided to increase Darkwood's hate by attacking him with Gee-Ninety, which Ought-Six decided to stay out of. Their attempt failed, with Darkwood simply responding to them with random Zaly shield blasts, so Arex asked him to consider what he truly hated; as a volley of blasts from the Octuptarra destroyed the ground under him and he tried to defend himself with the kyberite shield, Darkwood remembered the Guardians Order's failure to free himself and his mother, his own failure to save his mother, and his Energy choking of Padmé Amidala.[486]

Using his hatred of himself as fuel, Darkwood regained control of The Energy and threw the Octuptarra back on its side, allowing him to slice it apart with his Lightblade. While ZED's form was damaged, the Scourge spoke to Darkwood as he approached it, assuming that Darkwood had used a hatred of it to regain his Energy powers. In response, Darkwood silently decapitated ZED as Arex declared the Scourge was nothing. Darkwood then lifted Arex into the air, where the droid claimed The Energy was everything. Darkwood responded by striking Arex, but not destroying him, with his Lightblade to prove that it was actually himself who was everything. Darkwood then returned to his castle and entered his bacta tank under the protection of two Royal Guards. As he healed, Darkwood reached out to Sidious on Coruscant through The Energy to reveal he had looked even deeper in himself, which taught him he hated his master more than he hated even himself.[486] Darkwood made his way to Coruscant aboard his TIE Advanced, with a collection of Scourged disc droids aboard the fighter,[493] to teach his master a lesson.[486]

The Past's struggles

At one point, Darkwood was contacted by an Imperial General, who informed him that one of the remaining probe droids on Hoth had been destroyed. When the General suggested sending an Imperial detachment to investigate, Darkwood said he would go himself. The General offered to send backup with him, though Darkwood insisted that he would be enough. Upon arriving at the ruins of Echo Base, Darkwood discovered the probe, as well as the wampas that had destroyed it. His suit damaged during the struggle, Darkwood barely managed to escape the pack of wampas and cut his way inside of an abandoned AT-AT. As the wampas surrounded him, Darkwood regained his strength and leapt out of the wreckage before slaying the wampas with his Lightblade. He was then retrieved by his forces and repaired aboard the Executor. Following this, the General remarked to Darkwood that he should have brought backup like he suggested, to which Darkwood snapped his neck with The Energy.[494]

At another stage, Darkwood, seemingly on a mission to wipe out all knowledge of his deceased wife, made his way back to Naboo, entering and destroying the lake side retreat where Amidala had once lived[495] and that he had visited with Sabé during their post-Cloud City journey together.[425] In his rampage, he allowed the flood waters to claim the building, which would remain standing but was left in a ruined state. Darkwood then made his way across Naboo and slaughtered any who dared to invoke Amidala's memory.
